📚 本期文献进展 / Literature Brief

  • 近期研究聚焦于埃博拉病毒的免疫机制、疫苗研发及公共卫生应对策略,为疫情控制提供新思路。
  • 本迪布焦病毒(BDBV)的免疫学研究显示抗体与病毒水平存在关联,但尚未明确其临床保护效果 [10.1038/s41590-026-02623-2]。
  • 世卫组织建议在BDBV暴发期间仅在研究方案中使用埃博拉疫苗,强调需加速开发BDBV专用疫苗 [10.2471/b09884]。
  • BDBV疫情中,隔离泄漏显著增加传播风险,需加强社区驱动的隔离管理 [10.64898/2026.08.25.26361360]。
  • 非洲国家在疫情监测中面临信号整合挑战,需采用PREIS框架提升应对效率 [10.4102/jphia.v17i1.2079]。
  • Recent studies focus on the immunological mechanisms, vaccine development, and public health response strategies of the Ebola virus, offering new insights for outbreak control.
  • Immunological studies on BDBV show an association between antibody levels and viral load, but the clinical protective efficacy remains unclear [10.1038/s41590-026-02623-2].
  • WHO recommends the use of the Ebola vaccine only within research protocols during BDBV outbreaks, emphasizing the need for accelerated development of BDBV-specific vaccines [10.2471/b09884].
  • Isolation leakage during the BDBV outbreak significantly increased transmission risks, highlighting the need for community-driven isolation management [10.64898/2026.08.25.26361360].
  • Africa faces challenges in epidemic intelligence due to signal integration issues, requiring the adoption of the PREIS framework to enhance response efficiency [10.4102/jphia.v17i1.2079].

📘 主报告：研究论文 / Primary Research

Journal: PLOS Global Public Health  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-08-27  |  DOI: 10.1371/journal.pgph.0006680  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 6, 8, e0006680
A 高优先级学术文献 · 研究A high priorityAcademic literature · Research

刚果民主共和国和乌干达 2026 年本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 早期阶段的运行 流行病学

Operational epidemiology of the early phase of the 2026 Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda
作者： Tambe Elvis Akem, Eta Calvin Oben
摘要中文翻译刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 和乌干达的 2026 年本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 发生在脆弱的跨境环境中,其特点是识别延迟、诊断不确定、不安全、人口流动、社区不信任和医护人员暴露。我们利用官方情况报告、公共卫生声明、部委声明、世卫组织和非洲疾病预防控制中心的最新情况、跨境公报和选定的合作伙伴声明,进行了一项回顾性操作 流行病学 研究。该分析反映了 暴发 早期的公开数据,刚果民主共和国的主要数据锁定于 2026 年 5 月 27 日,乌干达数据锁定至 2026 年 5 月 29 日。我们重建了早期时间表,描述了流行病学和地理进展,量化了应对指标,并应用了透明的卫生区优先顺序框架。最早记录的症状发作是在 2026 年 4 月 24 日。国家生物医学研究所 (INRB) 于 5 月 14 日在 13 个样本中的 8 个样本中检测到非扎伊尔埃博拉病毒,给出了从症状发作到首次实验室检测的间隔时间为 20 天;5 月 15 日,物种级本迪布焦病毒得到确认并正式宣布。截至5月27日刚果民主共和国数据锁定,已报告确诊病例125例,确诊死亡17例,确诊病死率为13.6%。刚果民主共和国还报告了 906 例疑似病例和 223 例疑似死亡,疑似死亡比例为 24.6%。12 天内,确诊病例从三个省份的 3 个卫生区扩大到 13 个。伊图里省确诊病例110例(88.0%);布尼亚、鲁万帕拉和蒙布瓦卢卫生区共报告了 90 例病例(72.0%)。截至 5 月 29 日,乌干达报告了 9 例确诊病例和 1 例死亡。运营压力包括刚果民主共和国列出的 2,635 名接触者、乌干达正在追踪的 436 名接触者以及刚果民主共和国待测试的 774 份采集样本中的 126 份 (16.3%)。刚果民主共和国 125 例确诊病例中,医护人员占 16 例(12.8%),乌干达 9 例确诊病例中至少有 3 例(33.3%)。应用卫生区优先顺序框架,鲁万帕拉、蒙布瓦卢和布尼亚被列为较高优先级卫生区。排除已确认的负担并没有改变任何健康区等级;10 个区域的数据分类置信度较高,3 个区域的数据分类置信度适中。在这个早期运营快照中,暴发 可见性反映了 传播 和运营因素,特别是检测延迟、实验室积压、跨境流动、医疗保健相关暴露以及社区信任或安全事件。该框架通过将 暴发、实验室、病例管理、感染预防和控制、接触者追踪和社区参与资源引导到多个运营风险集中的卫生区,为 暴发 早期阶段的快速决策支持提供透明的基础。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在了解刚果民主共和国和乌干达 2026 年本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 早期阶段的运行情况,强调识别延迟、诊断不确定性和社区不信任带来的挑战。
设计与对象
本研究采用回顾性运行 流行病学 设计,基于官方疫情报告、公共卫生声明、卫生部声明、WHO 和非洲疾控中心更新、跨境公报以及部分合作伙伴声明开展。研究聚焦刚果民主共和国(DRC)和乌干达早期 暴发 疫情阶段,数据锁定时间为 2026 年 5 月 27 日,并通过 2026 年 5 月 29 日完成区域三角交叉验证。
核心方法
该研究重建了早期时间表,描述了流行病学和地理进展,量化了反应指标,并利用公开数据应用了透明的卫生区优先顺序框架。
主要结果
截至5月27日刚果民主共和国数据锁定,已报告确诊病例125例,确诊死亡17例,确诊病死率为13.6%。刚果民主共和国还报告了 906 例疑似病例和 223 例疑似死亡,疑似死亡比例为 24.6%。
解释与创新
作者解释说,卫生区优先顺序框架有效地将资源引导到具有多种操作风险的领域,例如检测延迟、实验室积压和医疗保健相关暴露,这是 暴发 响应的一种新颖方法。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果为 暴发 早期阶段的快速决策支持提供了透明的基础,从而能够有针对性地分配 监测、实验室、病例管理和社区参与资源。
局限与证据强度
该研究依赖于公开数据,这些数据可能会引入潜在的偏差或差距,10 个区域的数据分类置信度较高,3 个区域的置信度适中,表明证据强度处于中等水平。

Operational epidemiology of the early phase of the 2026 Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda

Authors: Tambe Elvis Akem, Eta Calvin Oben
Original abstract (English)
The 2026 Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda occurred in a fragile cross-border setting marked by delayed recognition, diagnostic uncertainty, insecurity, population mobility, community mistrust, and healthcare-worker exposure. We conducted a retrospective operational epidemiology study using official situation reports, public health declarations, ministry statements, WHO and Africa CDC updates, cross-border communiqués, and selected partner statements. This analysis reflects publicly available data from the early outbreak period, with the primary DRC data lock on 27 May 2026 and Uganda and regional triangulation through 29 May 2026. We reconstructed the early timeline, described epidemiological and geographic progression, quantified response indicators, and applied a transparent health-zone prioritization framework. The earliest documented symptom onset was 24 April 2026. The Institut National de Recherche Biomédicale (INRB) detected non-Zaire ebolavirus in eight of 13 samples on 14 May, giving a 20-day symptom-onset-to-first-laboratory-detection interval; species-level Bundibugyo virus confirmation and official declaration followed on 15 May. By the DRC data lock on 27 May, 125 confirmed cases and 17 confirmed deaths had been reported, giving a confirmed case fatality ratio of 13.6%. DRC also reported 906 suspected cases and 223 suspected deaths, giving a suspected fatality proportion of 24.6%. Confirmed cases expanded from three to 13 health zones across three provinces within 12 days. Ituri province accounted for 110 confirmed cases (88.0%); Bunia, Rwampara, and Mongbwalu health zones together contributed 90 cases (72.0%). Uganda reported nine confirmed cases and one death by 29 May. Operational pressure included 2,635 listed contacts in DRC, 436 contacts under follow-up in Uganda, and 126 of 774 collected samples (16.3%) pending testing in DRC. Healthcare workers represented 16 of 125 DRC confirmed cases (12.8%) and at least three of nine Uganda cases (33.3%). Applying the health-zone prioritization framework, Rwampara, Mongbwalu, and Bunia were classified as higher priority health zones. Excluding confirmed burden did not change any health-zone tier; data classification confidence was high for 10 zones and moderate for three. Within this early operational snapshot, outbreak visibility reflected both transmission and operational factors, particularly detection delay, laboratory backlog, cross-border movement, healthcare-linked exposure, and community trust or security incidents. The framework provides a transparent basis for rapid decision support during an outbreak’s early phase by directing surveillance, laboratory, case-management, infection prevention and control, contact-tracing, and community-engagement resources toward health zones where multiple operational risks converge.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to understand the operational epidemiology of the early phase of the 2026 Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda, highlighting the challenges posed by delayed recognition, diagnostic uncertainty, and community mistrust.
Design and population
A retrospective operational epidemiology study was conducted using official situation reports, public health declarations, ministry statements, WHO and Africa CDC updates, cross-border communiqués, and selected partner statements. The study focused on the early outbreak period in DRC and Uganda, with data locked on 27 May 2026 and regional triangulation through 29 May 2026.
Core methods
The study reconstructed the early timeline, described epidemiological and geographic progression, quantified response indicators, and applied a transparent health-zone prioritization framework using publicly available data.
Main results
By the DRC data lock on 27 May, 125 confirmed cases and 17 confirmed deaths had been reported, giving a confirmed case fatality ratio of 13.6%. DRC also reported 906 suspected cases and 223 suspected deaths, giving a suspected fatality proportion of 24.6%.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that the health-zone prioritization framework effectively directed resources to areas with multiple operational risks, such as detection delay, laboratory backlog, and healthcare-linked exposure, which was a novel approach for outbreak response.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings provide a transparent basis for rapid decision support during an outbreak’s early phase, enabling targeted allocation of surveillance, laboratory, case-management, and community-engagement resources.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study relied on publicly available data, which may have introduced potential biases or gaps, and the confidence in data classification was high for 10 zones and moderate for three, indicating a moderate level of evidence strength.

加快对本迪布焦病毒紧急情况的研究响应。

Accelerating the research response to the Bundibugyo virus emergency.
作者： Mariah P Csolle, Bronwyn A Bailey, Yuna Chung, Tsharni R Zazryn, Tari Turner, David S Khoury, Miles P Davenport, Csolle MP, Bailey BA, Chung Y
摘要中文翻译确定这一点需要研究人员对新领域的文献有一个概述,并确信他们没有错过可能影响其研究方向或有用性的关键研究。所得图谱于 2026 年 5 月 21 日在线发布,并于 2026 年 7 月 22 日更新(可在 https://livingevidence.org.au/research-initiatives/about-feeva/evidence-maps/ 获取),方法的预印本 1 于 2026 年 5 月 24 日发布。已有少量关于 BDBV 免疫的研究。BDBV 的免疫学发现 对丝状病毒的免疫被认为主要是通过表面糖蛋白的抗体介导的。两项研究分析了预防性 疫苗接种,其中一项研究显示抗体水平和保护作用之间存在关联7,但另一项研究显示没有关联8。虽然一项针对感染前 3 或 7 天的 疫苗接种 的研究显示抗体水平与病毒水平之间存在关联(在未经同行评审的预印本中),但另一项关于暴露后预防的研究很难解释,因为早期 免疫应答 到 疫苗接种 和感染的动态性质4。在国际关注的突发公共卫生事件(例如本迪布焦病毒 暴发)的背景下,研究界需要快速获取信息以指导有效的应对措施。证据图将某个领域的现有知识汇集到一个单一的、有组织的资源中,使研究人员能够快速确定优先事项、差距和行动机会。主题 免疫学和微生物学研究对于对抗新出现的传染病至关重要。这在...期间得到了证明。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在满足国际关注的突发公共卫生事件(例如本迪布焦病毒 暴发)期间快速获取信息的需求,以指导有效的研究应对措施。研究界需要此类信息来有效对抗新出现的传染病。
设计与对象
该研究涉及创建本迪布焦病毒的证据图,重点是 疫苗接种 和免疫研究。该研究是为了响应世界卫生组织于 2026 年 5 月 17 日宣布 BDBV 暴发 为突发公共卫生事件而进行的,目的是对相关文献进行汇总和分类。
核心方法
证据映射过程涉及对与 BDBV 疫苗接种 和免疫相关的出版物进行系统识别和分类。使用两名独立审稿人来确保文献的一致性和公正性,并且定期更新地图以保持其相关性。
主要结果
在宣布 BDBV PHEIC 后,迅速收集了证据图,全面概述了 疫苗接种 和本迪布焦病毒免疫的现有研究。该地图作为开放资源提供给健康和医学界。
解释与创新
作者将这些发现解释为证明了证据映射的价值,使研究人员能够快速识别相关研究并优先考虑他们的工作。这种方法在新宣布的突发公共卫生事件中的应用是新颖的,为研究界提供了动态资源。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果向研究界通报了有关 BDBV 疫苗接种 和免疫的现有文献,从而促进有针对性的研究和干预措施。这可以提高对未来 暴发 的响应效率,并加强对新出现的传染病的准备。
局限与证据强度
该研究的证据基于现有文献的快速汇总,其中可能不包括所有最近的研究。然而,独立审稿人和定期更新的使用增强了证据图的可靠性,提供了适度的置信度评估。

Accelerating the research response to the Bundibugyo virus emergency.

Authors: Mariah P Csolle, Bronwyn A Bailey, Yuna Chung, Tsharni R Zazryn, Tari Turner, David S Khoury, Miles P Davenport, Csolle MP, Bailey BA, Chung Y
Original abstract (English)
Determining this requires investigators to have an overview of the literature in the new field and to be confident that they have not missed a crucial study that might affect the direction or usefulness of their research. The resulting map was posted online on 21 May 2026 and updated on 22 July 2026 (available at https://livingevidence.org.au/research-initiatives/about-feeva/evidence-maps/), and a preprint1 of the methods was published on 24 May 2026. Rapid production of an evidence map for BDBV was possible because of the established method for mapping filoviruses2 and the fact that only a small number of studies on BDBV immunity were available. Immunological findings for BDBV Immunity to filoviruses is thought to be largely mediated through antibodies to the surface glycoprotein. Two studies analyzed prophylactic vaccination, with one study showing an association between antibody levels and protection7 but another study showing no association8. While one study of vaccination 3 or 7 days before infection showed an association between antibody levels and viral levels (in a non-peer-reviewed preprint9), another study of post-exposure prophylaxis was difficult to interpret because of the dynamic nature of early immune responses to vaccination and infection4. In the context of a Public Health Emergency of International Concern, such as the Bundibugyo virus outbreak, the research community requires rapid access to information to guide an effective response. Evidence mapping brings together the available knowledge in a field into a single, organized resource, enabling researchers to rapidly identify priorities, gaps and opportunities for action. Subjects Immunology and microbiology research is crucial for combatting emerging infectious diseases. This was demonstrated during...
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the need for rapid access to information during a Public Health Emergency of International Concern, such as the Bundibugyo virus outbreak, to guide an effective research response. The research community requires such information to combat emerging infectious diseases effectively.
Design and population
The study involved the creation of an evidence map for the Bundibugyo virus, focusing on vaccination and immunity research. It was conducted in response to the World Health Organization's declaration of the BDBV outbreak as a PHEIC on 17 May 2026, with the goal of assembling and categorizing relevant literature.
Core methods
The evidence mapping process involved systematically identifying and categorizing publications related to BDBV vaccination and immunity. Two independent reviewers were used to ensure consistency and unbiased assembly of the literature, and the maps were updated regularly to maintain their relevance.
Main results
The evidence map was rapidly assembled following the declaration of the BDBV PHEIC, providing a comprehensive overview of the available research on vaccination and immunity for the Bundibugyo virus. This map was made available as an open resource for the health and medical communities.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted the findings as demonstrating the value of evidence mapping in enabling researchers to quickly identify relevant studies and prioritize their work. This approach was novel in its application to a newly declared PHEIC, offering a dynamic resource for the research community.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform the research community about the existing literature on BDBV vaccination and immunity, facilitating targeted studies and interventions. This can improve the efficiency of responses to future outbreaks and enhance preparedness for emerging infectious diseases.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's evidence is based on the rapid assembly of existing literature, which may not include all recent studies. However, the use of independent reviewers and regular updates strengthens the reliability of the evidence map, providing a moderate confidence assessment.

乌干达护士在 2022 年 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 期间护理患者的生活经历:一项定性研究

Nurses’ Lived Experiences of Caring for Patients During the 2022 Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak in Uganda: A Qualitative Study
作者： Gift Mushabe, Patience A. Muwanguzi, Sarah Joselyn Nalubega, Bridget Atuhaire, Maureen Namugaya, Racheal Nabunya, Nalubega, S. J.
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景 护士在传染病 暴发(包括 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD))中发挥着关键作用,常常使自己面临重大的个人和职业风险。虽然护士是埃博拉应对工作的核心,但他们的生活经历,尤其是在乌干达,仍未得到充分探索。本研究旨在探讨 2022 年 暴发 期间在 Mubende 地区转诊医院为埃博拉病毒病患者提供护理的护士的生活经历。方法 采用探索性定性设计,对埃博拉 治疗 科室 (ETU) 工作的七名护士进行深入访谈。参与者是通过有目的的滚雪球抽样来选择的。使用现象学方法对数据进行主题分析,以捕捉护士经历的情感、心理和专业维度。结果 调查结果揭示了积极和消极经历之间复杂的相互作用。积极的经历包括患者康复带来的喜悦、强大的团队合作、专业成长以及感染预防和控制 (IPC) 方面加强的培训。护士们表示信心增强,掌握了新技能,并获得了职业发展的机会,包括国际机构的认可。相反,负面经历主导了叙述。其中包括严重的资源限制、繁重的工作量、身体疲惫和心理困扰,尤其是目睹患者的痛苦和死亡。参与者描述了创伤后应激症状以及对感染和死亡的持续恐惧,而早期准备不足又加剧了这种恐惧。社会孤立和家庭生活中断也很常见,一些护士隐瞒自己的工作,以避免让亲人感到痛苦或在社区内遭受耻辱。其他挑战包括团队紧张、缺勤以及现有员工和外部招聘人员之间的薪酬和认可差异。结论 该研究强调了 2022 年乌干达埃博拉病毒 暴发 期间对护士造成的深刻的情感和职业损失。虽然恢复力和成长的时刻是显而易见的,但结构性和系统性挑战严重影响了护士的福祉和护理服务。为了更好地支持一线卫生工作者,未来的 暴发 应对措施必须优先考虑公平补偿、社会心理支持、持续培训和改善资源分配。这项研究的见解可以为旨在加强突发卫生事件期间劳动力准备和保留的政策提供信息。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
护士在传染病 暴发(包括 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD))期间发挥着关键作用,常常使自己面临重大的个人和职业风险。虽然护士是埃博拉应对工作的核心,但他们的生活经历,尤其是在乌干达,仍未得到充分探索。
设计与对象
本研究旨在探讨 2022 年 暴发 期间在 Mubende 地区转诊医院为埃博拉病毒病患者提供护理的护士的生活经历。参与者是通过有目的的滚雪球抽样来选择的。七名在埃博拉 治疗 科室 (ETU) 工作的护士接受了采访。
核心方法
使用现象学方法对数据进行主题分析,以捕捉护士经历的情感、心理和专业维度。通过对埃博拉 治疗 科室 (ETU) 工作的七名护士进行深入访谈,采用了探索性定性设计。
主要结果
调查结果揭示了积极和消极经历之间复杂的相互作用。积极的经历包括患者康复带来的喜悦、强大的团队合作、专业成长以及感染预防和控制 (IPC) 方面加强的培训。护士们表示信心增强,掌握了新技能,并获得了职业发展的机会,包括国际机构的认可。
解释与创新
该研究强调了 2022 年乌干达埃博拉病毒 暴发 期间对护士造成的深刻的情感和职业损失。虽然恢复力和成长的时刻是显而易见的,但结构性和系统性挑战严重影响了护士的福祉和护理服务。
科研与公卫意义
这项研究的见解可以为旨在加强突发卫生事件期间劳动力准备和保留的政策提供信息。为了更好地支持一线卫生工作者,未来的 暴发 应对措施必须优先考虑公平补偿、社会心理支持、持续培训和改善资源分配。
局限与证据强度
所提供的证据并未报告研究设计或证据强度评估的具体局限性。

Nurses’ Lived Experiences of Caring for Patients During the 2022 Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak in Uganda: A Qualitative Study

Authors: Gift Mushabe, Patience A. Muwanguzi, Sarah Joselyn Nalubega, Bridget Atuhaire, Maureen Namugaya, Racheal Nabunya, Nalubega, S. J.
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Nurses play a pivotal role during infectious disease outbreaks, including Ebola Virus Disease (EVD), often placing themselves at significant personal and professional risk. While nurses are central to Ebola response efforts, their lived experiences, particularly in Uganda, remain underexplored. This study aimed to explore the lived experiences of nurses who provided care to EVD patients during the 2022 outbreak at Mubende Regional Referral Hospital. Methods An exploratory qualitative design was employed using in-depth interviews with seven nurses who worked in the Ebola Treatment Unit (ETU). Participants were selected through purposive and snowball sampling. Data were analysed thematically using a phenomenological approach to capture the emotional, psychological, and professional dimensions of nurses’ experiences. Results Findings revealed a complex interplay of positive and negative experiences. Positive experiences included joy from patient recovery, strong teamwork, professional growth, and enhanced training in infection prevention and control (IPC). Nurses reported increased confidence, new skills, and opportunities for career advancement, including recognition from international agencies. Conversely, negative experiences dominated the narratives. These included severe resource constraints, overwhelming workload, physical exhaustion, and psychological distress, particularly from witnessing patient suffering and death. Participants described post-traumatic stress symptoms and constant fear of infection and death, exacerbated by inadequate early-stage preparation. Social isolation and disrupted family life were also common, with some nurses concealing their work to avoid distressing loved ones or experiencing stigma within their communities. Additional challenges included team tensions, absenteeism, and disparities in compensation and recognition between existing staff and external recruits. Conclusion The study highlights the profound emotional and professional toll on nurses during the 2022 Ebola outbreak in Uganda. While moments of resilience and growth were evident, structural and systemic challenges significantly impacted nurses’ well-being and care delivery. To better support frontline health workers, future outbreak responses must prioritize fair compensation, psychosocial support, consistent training, and improved resource allocation. Insights from this study can inform policies aimed at strengthening workforce readiness and retention during health emergencies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Nurses play a pivotal role during infectious disease outbreaks, including Ebola Virus Disease (EVD), often placing themselves at significant personal and professional risk. While nurses are central to Ebola response efforts, their lived experiences, particularly in Uganda, remain underexplored.
Design and population
This study aimed to explore the lived experiences of nurses who provided care to EVD patients during the 2022 outbreak at Mubende Regional Referral Hospital. Participants were selected through purposive and snowball sampling. Seven nurses who worked in the Ebola Treatment Unit (ETU) were interviewed.
Core methods
Data were analysed thematically using a phenomenological approach to capture the emotional, psychological, and professional dimensions of nurses’ experiences. An exploratory qualitative design was employed using in-depth interviews with seven nurses who worked in the Ebola Treatment Unit (ETU).
Main results
Findings revealed a complex interplay of positive and negative experiences. Positive experiences included joy from patient recovery, strong teamwork, professional growth, and enhanced training in infection prevention and control (IPC). Nurses reported increased confidence, new skills, and opportunities for career advancement, including recognition from international agencies.
Interpretation and novelty
The study highlights the profound emotional and professional toll on nurses during the 2022 Ebola outbreak in Uganda. While moments of resilience and growth were evident, structural and systemic challenges significantly impacted nurses’ well-being and care delivery.
Scientific and public-health significance
Insights from this study can inform policies aimed at strengthening workforce readiness and retention during health emergencies. To better support frontline health workers, future outbreak responses must prioritize fair compensation, psychosocial support, consistent training, and improved resource allocation.
Limitations and evidence strength
The supplied evidence does not report specific limitations of the study design or evidence strength assessment.

2025 年 1 月至 4 月乌干达苏丹埃博拉病毒病病例 流行病学。

Epidemiology of Sudan Ebolavirus Disease Cases in Uganda, January-April, 2025.
作者： Emmanuel Mfitundinda, Daniel Wenani, Hannington Katumba, Gertrude Abbo, Janet Lubega Kobusinge, Charity Mutesi, Patrick Kwizera, Emmanuel Okiror Okello, Annet Mary Namusisi, Joanita Nalwanga
摘要中文翻译背景:2025 年 1 月 30 日,乌干达卫生部 (MoH) 宣布了苏丹埃博拉病毒病 (SVD) 的 暴发。我们描述了 2025 年 1 月至 4 月期间乌干达 SVD 传播 的流行病学特征和 传播 动态。方法:使用卫生部病例定义来识别 SVD 病例,如果 RT-qPCR 呈阳性,则被分类为确诊病例;如果报告与流行病学相关但未经实验室确认的 SVD 相关死亡,则被分类为可能病例。通过接触者追踪、社区和卫生机构警报以及主动搜索来识别病例。使用标准 SVD 病例调查表收集数据,包括人口统计、暴露情况和结果。使用 R 中的 EpiEstim 软件包估算有效再生数 (Rt)。结果:2025 年 1 月 30 日至 4 月 25 日期间发现了 14 例病例:12 例 (86%) 为确诊病例,2 例 (14%) 为疑似病例;5 名(36%)是卫生工作者。平均(范围)年龄为 27(0-50)岁。4 例死亡,病死率 (CFR) 为 29% (4/14)。大多数暴露是家庭暴露(9[64%]),其次是医疗保健相关暴露(3[21%])。指示病例是一名卫生工作者,在症状出现 11 天后死于发热性疾病,同一天尸检样本显示 SUDV 呈阳性。共监控534名接触者;其中 454 人(85%)被隔离。已知接触者有 10 例;这些病例中没有发生继发性传播。估计的 Rt(四分位数间距)为 1.1 (0.83-1.35)。结论:暴发 的 CFR 和 Rt 较低,这可能是由于早期发现、快速识别、隔离和密切监测接触者所致。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在描述继乌干达卫生部于2025年1月30日宣布苏丹埃博拉病毒病(SVD)ZX​​QTERMXQXZ后,2025年1月至4月乌干达的流行病学特征和传播动态。
设计与对象
该研究于 2025 年 1 月 30 日至 4 月 25 日在乌干达进行,涉及通过接触者追踪、社区和医疗机构警报以及主动搜索来识别 SVD 病例。总共发现14例病例,其中12例确诊,2例疑似病例,其中5例是卫生工作者。
核心方法
如果 RT-qPCR 呈阳性,则将病例分类为确诊病例;如果报告与流行病学相关的 SVD 相关死亡,但未经实验室确认,则将病例分类为可能病例。使用标准 SVD 病例调查表收集数据,包括人口统计、暴露情况和结果。使用 R 中的 EpiEstim 包估计有效再生数 (Rt)。
主要结果
2025年1月30日至4月25日期间发现了14例病例:12例(86%)为确诊病例,2例(14%)为疑似病例;5 名(36%)是卫生工作者。4 例死亡,病死率 (CFR) 为 29% (4/14)。大多数暴露是家庭暴露(9[64%]),其次是医疗保健相关暴露(3[21%])。共监控534名接触者;其中 454 人(85%)被隔离。已知接触者中没有发生二次 传播。估计的 Rt(四分位数间距)为 1.1 (0.83-1.35)。
解释与创新
作者将研究结果解释为表明病死率和有效再生数较低,这可能是由于早期发现、迅速识别、隔离和密切监测接触者所致。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为 监测 和苏丹埃博拉病毒病的控制策略提供信息,强调早期检测和接触者追踪在缓解 暴发 方面的重要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究的证据受到对报告病例和接触者监测的依赖的限制,没有提供详细的统计分析或模型。由于该研究的观察性质,对研究结果的可信度中等。

Epidemiology of Sudan Ebolavirus Disease Cases in Uganda, January-April, 2025.

Authors: Emmanuel Mfitundinda, Daniel Wenani, Hannington Katumba, Gertrude Abbo, Janet Lubega Kobusinge, Charity Mutesi, Patrick Kwizera, Emmanuel Okiror Okello, Annet Mary Namusisi, Joanita Nalwanga
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: On January 30, 2025, the Ministry of Health, Uganda (MoH), announced an outbreak of Sudan ebolavirus disease (SVD). We describe the epidemiological characteristics and transmission dynamics of the SVD outbreak in Uganda during January-April, 2025. METHODS: SVD cases were identified using MoH case definitions and were classified as confirmed if positive on RT-qPCR or probable if an SVD-related death was reported with epidemiological linkage but without laboratory confirmation. Cases were identified through contact tracing, community and health facility alerts, and active search. Data were collected using a standard SVD case investigation form, including demographics, exposures and outcome. The effective reproductive number (Rt) was estimated using the EpiEstim package in R. RESULTS: Fourteen cases were identified between January 30 and April 25, 2025: 12 (86%) were confirmed and 2 (14%) were probable; and 5 (36%) were health workers. The mean (range) age was 27 (0-50) years. Four cases died, a case fatality rate (CFR) of 29% (4/14). Most exposures were household (9[64%]) followed by healthcare-associated exposures (3[21%]). The index case was a health worker who died of a febrile illness 11 days after symptom onset, and postmortem samples returned positive for SUDV on the same day. A total of 534 contacts were monitored; of these, 454 (85%) were quarantined. Ten cases were known contacts; no secondary transmission occurred among these cases. The estimated Rt (interquartile range) was 1.1 (0.83-1.35). CONCLUSION: The outbreak had a low CFR and a low Rt, possibly due to early detection and swift identification, quarantine, and close monitoring of contacts.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to describe the epidemiological characteristics and transmission dynamics of the Sudan ebolavirus disease (SVD) outbreak in Uganda during January-April, 2025, following the Ministry of Health's announcement of an outbreak on January 30, 2025.
Design and population
The study was conducted in Uganda from January 30 to April 25, 2025, and involved identifying SVD cases through contact tracing, community and health facility alerts, and active search. A total of 14 cases were identified, including 12 confirmed and 2 probable cases, with 5 being health workers.
Core methods
Cases were classified as confirmed if positive on RT-qPCR or probable if an SVD-related death was reported with epidemiological linkage but without laboratory confirmation. Data were collected using a standard SVD case investigation form, including demographics, exposures, and outcomes. The effective reproductive number (Rt) was estimated using the EpiEstim package in R.
Main results
Fourteen cases were identified between January 30 and April 25, 2025: 12 (86%) were confirmed and 2 (14%) were probable; and 5 (36%) were health workers. Four cases died, resulting in a case fatality rate (CFR) of 29% (4/14). Most exposures were household (9[64%]) followed by healthcare-associated exposures (3[21%]). A total of 534 contacts were monitored; of these, 454 (85%) were quarantined. No secondary transmission occurred among known contacts. The estimated Rt (interquartile range) was 1.1 (0.83-1.35).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted the findings as indicating a low case fatality rate and effective reproductive number, possibly due to early detection and swift identification, quarantine, and close monitoring of contacts.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform surveillance and control strategies for Sudan ebolavirus disease, highlighting the importance of early detection and contact tracing in mitigating outbreaks.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's evidence is limited by its reliance on reported cases and contact monitoring, with no detailed statistical analyses or models provided. The confidence in the findings is moderate due to the observational nature of the study.

调整许可与推荐使用以加速对中部非洲布尼迪布焦埃博拉病毒疫情的疫苗应对

Aligning Licensure and Recommended Use to Expedite Vaccine Access in Response to the Ongoing Bundibugyo Ebolavirus Epidemic in Central Africa
作者： George L. O’Hara, Sam Halabi
摘要中文翻译正在发生的本迪布焦正埃博拉病毒——本迪布焦病毒 (BDBV)——疫情是十年来最严重的埃博拉病毒 暴发,并且正在迅速蔓延,仅在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 就有超过 4600 例疑似病例。它已造成2186人死亡,并蔓延至邻国。由于 BDBV 尚未获得批准的 疫苗,因此及时应对需要调整使用已批准用于其他埃博拉病毒的现有 疫苗,并加快开发针对 BDBV 的新型 疫苗。获得 疫苗 是公共卫生应急响应的基础,通常取决于每个国家的两个专家机构:国家监管机构(例如美国食品和药物管理局 (FDA))和国家免疫顾问。传统的全球卫生援助的减少使刚果民主共和国和其他最近面临埃博拉、马尔堡和MPOX疫情的低收入国家的许可和建议使用情况变得更加复杂,从而加剧了高危人群的获取差距。因此,本评论阐明了强大的国家免疫顾问与扩大的许可密切协调对于加快获得 疫苗 和其他重要流行病应对工具的至关重要性。利用卢旺达共和国和刚果民主共和国的监管应对措施,表明这些机构之间更清晰地划分角色对于有效应对至关重要,有助于优先考虑自给自足和加强区域公共卫生合作,从而能够更快地控制本迪布焦。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
正在发生的本迪布焦病毒 (BDBV) 疫情是十年来最严重的埃博拉病毒 暴发,仅在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 就有超过 4600 例疑似病例。该研究强调迫切需要调整使用现有的 疫苗 并加快开发针对 BDBV 的新型 疫苗。
设计与对象
该研究是对卢旺达共和国和刚果民主共和国监管反应的分析评论。它重点关注低收入国家在获取最近的埃博拉病毒、马尔堡病毒和MPOX病毒的暴发时所面临的挑战,特别强调了本迪布焦病毒。
核心方法
这些方法涉及审查国家监管机构和国家免疫顾问在 疫苗 访问中的作用。它还包括对这些机构如何相互作用以加快 疫苗 的可用性以应对流行病的审查。
主要结果
研究报告称,获得 疫苗 通常取决于每个国家的两个专家机构:国家监管机构和国家免疫顾问。它还强调,更清晰地划分这些机构之间的角色对于有效应对至关重要。
解释与创新
作者解释说,国家监管机构和免疫顾问之间的协调对于加快 疫苗 的获取至关重要。这是对突发公共卫生事件期间 疫苗 分发操作动态的新颖见解。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为改善面临埃博拉病毒和相关病原体 疫苗 的低收入国家的 疫苗 获取途径提供参考。他们强调明确的公共卫生准备监管框架的重要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于评论,不包括经验数据,这限制了其证据强度。然而,它为 疫苗 准入中协调监管行动的必要性提供了批判性的视角,这是对该领域的中等贡献。

Aligning Licensure and Recommended Use to Expedite Vaccine Access in Response to the Ongoing Bundibugyo Ebolavirus Epidemic in Central Africa

Authors: George L. O’Hara, Sam Halabi
Original abstract (English)
The ongoing Orthoebolavirus bundibugyoense—Bundibugyo virus (BDBV)—epidemic is the worst Ebolavirus outbreak in a decade and is rapidly expanding, with >4600 suspected cases in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) alone. It has caused 2186 deaths and spread to neighboring countries. As BDBV has no approved vaccine, timely response will require adapted use of existing vaccines approved for other ebolaviruses and expedited development of novel vaccines targeted toward BDBV. Access to vaccines is fundamental to public health emergency response and generally depends on two expert bodies in each country: a national regulatory authority (e.g., the US Food and Drug Administration (FDA)) and a national immunization advisor. Receding traditional global health assistance compounds already-limited licensure and recommended use in the DRC and other low-income countries confronting recent outbreaks of Ebola, Marburg, and mpox, exacerbating access gaps for high-risk populations. This commentary thus clarifies the critical importance of a robust national immunization advisor, coordinated closely with expanded licensure, in expediting access to vaccines and other essential epidemic response tools. Using the regulatory responses of the Republic of Rwanda and DRC, it demonstrates that clearer delineation of roles between these bodies is critical for effective response, can help prioritize self-sufficiency and greater regional public health cooperation, and will thus enable swifter control of Bundibugyo.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The ongoing Bundibugyo virus (BDBV) epidemic is the worst Ebolavirus outbreak in a decade, with >4600 suspected cases in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) alone. The study highlights the urgent need for adapted use of existing vaccines and expedited development of novel vaccines targeted toward BDBV.
Design and population
The study is a commentary analyzing the regulatory responses of the Republic of Rwanda and DRC. It focuses on the challenges faced by low-income countries in accessing vaccines for recent outbreaks of Ebola, Marburg, and mpox, particularly emphasizing the Bundibugyo virus.
Core methods
The methods involve examining the roles of national regulatory authorities and national immunization advisors in vaccine access. It also includes a review of how these bodies interact to expedite vaccine availability for epidemic response.
Main results
The study reports that access to vaccines is generally dependent on two expert bodies in each country: a national regulatory authority and a national immunization advisor. It also highlights that clearer delineation of roles between these bodies is critical for effective response.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret that the coordination between national regulatory authorities and immunization advisors is essential for expediting vaccine access. This is a novel insight into the operational dynamics of vaccine distribution during public health emergencies.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform strategies for improving vaccine access in low-income countries facing outbreaks of Ebolavirus and related pathogens. They emphasize the importance of clear regulatory frameworks for public health preparedness.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on a commentary and does not include empirical data, which limits its evidence strength. However, it provides a critical perspective on the need for coordinated regulatory actions in vaccine access, which is a moderate-level contribution to the field.

“They could ...distance themselves from me claiming I still had Ebola”: experiences of Ebola virus disease survivors in Central Uganda

“They could ...distance themselves from me claiming I still had Ebola”: experiences of Ebola virus disease survivors in Central Uganda
作者： Brenda Nabawanuka, Joshua Epuitai, Michael Muhoozi, Moses Asiimwe, Julian Aryampa, Akugizibwe Pardon, Pauline Irumba, Edson Katsomyo, Charles Rugumayo, Joan Tusabe
摘要中文翻译埃博拉病毒仍然是乌干达面临的巨大公共卫生挑战,该国未来很容易受到埃博拉病毒的影响。幸存者在恢复期间可能会继续经历埃博拉病毒的后遗症。该研究旨在探讨埃博拉幸存者康复期间和康复后的经历。在乌干达中部进行了一项描述性定性研究。我们使用有目的抽样对之前曾患有 诊断 EVD 的 诊断 幸存者进行了 14 次深入调查。数据采集​​时间为2024年11月18日至11月28日。采用主题演绎分析法对数据进行分析。参与者对埃博拉 诊断 的反应各不相同。参与者最初对寻求治疗犹豫不决,但他们最终同意埃博拉 诊断 并遵守医疗 治疗。埃博拉幸存者对埃博拉有阴谋论,即使在生存后仍然存在。埃博拉病毒被归因于巫术,而其他人则认为它是为了减少人口规模并作为器官捐赠的工具而引入的。生存归因于与政府的权衡和假装生病。埃博拉患者和幸存者得到了政府和非政府组织的正式和非正式支持,帮助满足他们入院期间和入院后的社交、身体和情感需求。埃博拉患者和幸存者在入院期间和出院后面临情感、社交和身体方面的挑战。埃博拉患者经历了与生存担忧相关的巨大情感创伤。出院后,情感和身体上的挑战仍然存在,这些挑战源于耻辱、歧视、经济拮据、婚姻问题和持续存在的身体疾病。埃博拉病毒病幸存者面临严重的健康问题和不良的应对机制。这主要是由于埃博拉病毒病的致命性和污名化性质造成的。确保患者理解并采取应对机制可以减少这种耻辱。需要将基于社区的心理健康和心理社会干预措施纳入针对埃博拉病毒病幸存者的更广泛的护理方案中。

“They could ...distance themselves from me claiming I still had Ebola”: experiences of Ebola virus disease survivors in Central Uganda

Authors: Brenda Nabawanuka, Joshua Epuitai, Michael Muhoozi, Moses Asiimwe, Julian Aryampa, Akugizibwe Pardon, Pauline Irumba, Edson Katsomyo, Charles Rugumayo, Joan Tusabe
Original abstract (English)
Ebola continues to be a great public health challenge in Uganda where it is vulnerable to future Ebola outbreaks. Survivors are likely to continue experiencing the after effects of Ebola during the recovery period. The study was conducted to explore experiences of Ebola survivors during and after the recovery period. A descriptive qualitative study was conducted in central Uganda. We conducted 14 in-depth among Ebola Virus Survivors who had previously had a diagnosis of EVD using purposive sampling. Data was collected from 18th November to 28th November 2024. Thematic deductive analysis was applied to analyze the data. Participants had varied response to Ebola diagnosis. Participants were initially hesitant to seek care, but they eventually agreed with their Ebola diagnosis and complied with medical treatment. Ebola survivors had conspiracy theories regarding Ebola which persisted even after survival. Ebola was attributed to witchcraft, while others thought it was introduced to reduce their population size and as a vehicle for organ donation. Survival was attributed to trade-offs with government and pretense of being sick. Ebola patients and survivors had formal and informal support from government and non-governmental organization which helped meet their social, physical and emotional needs during and after admission. Ebola patients and survivors had emotional, social, and physical challenges during admission and after discharge. Ebola patients experienced enormous emotional trauma related to worries of survival. Emotional and physical challenges persisted after discharge and they emanated from stigma, discrimination, financial constraints, marital problems and persisting physical ailments. EVD survivors experience significant health concerns and poor coping mechanisms. This mainly as result of the fatal and stigmatizing nature of Ebola virus Disease. Ensuring patients understand and adopt coping mechanisms reduces this stigma. There is need for community-based mental health and psychosocial interventions integrated within a broader package of care for EVD survivors.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Ebola continues to be a great public health challenge in Uganda where it is vulnerable to future Ebola outbreaks.
Design and population
The study was conducted to explore experiences of Ebola survivors during and after the recovery period. A descriptive qualitative study was conducted in central Uganda. Participants had varied response to Ebola diagnosis.
Core methods
We conducted 14 in-depth interviews among Ebola Virus Survivors who had previously had a diagnosis of EVD using purposive sampling.
Main results
Participants were initially hesitant to seek care, but they eventually agreed with their Ebola diagnosis and complied with medical treatment.
Interpretation and novelty
Ebola patients and survivors had emotional, social, and physical challenges during admission and after discharge.
Scientific and public-health significance
Ensuring patients understand and adopt coping mechanisms reduces this stigma. There is need for community-based mental health and psychosocial interventions integrated within a broader package of care for EVD survivors.
Limitations and evidence strength
The supplied evidence does not report specific limitations or evidence strength assessment.

非洲流行病情报中的信号整合挑战:2026年 Bundibugyo 病毒病 暴发 案例及 PREIS 框架的应用

Signal integration challenges in African epidemic intelligence: Lessons from the 2026 Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak and application of the PREIS framework
作者： Hyacinthe R. Zabre, Hyacinthe R. Zabré
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在解决非洲流行病情报整合多源信号的挑战,特别关注 2026 年本迪布焦病毒病 暴发。背景强调需要改进系统来及时解释信号,因为 暴发 在确认疾病方面存在延迟。
设计与对象
The study analyzed the 2026 Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda, which occurred between 05 May and 15 May 2026. It involved multiple non-laboratory signals that were active before laboratory confirmation.
核心方法
该研究使用 PREIS 框架开发了一个可重复的分析层来集成信号。它强调了及时解释整合并发多源信号对于提高流行病情报的重要性。
主要结果
The outbreak showed that the initial alert was made on 05 May 2026, and laboratory confirmation followed on 15 May 2026, with ten days separating these events. Multiple non-laboratory signals were already active during this period.
解释与创新
作者解释说,非洲在流行病检测方面的主要挑战不是信号的可用性,而是信号的及时整合。他们引入了 PREIS 框架作为解决此问题的一种新颖方法。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为非洲流行病情报开发更有效的 监测 系统提供信息,特别是针对 埃博拉病毒病 等疾病。他们强调了集成多源信号以实现快速响应的重要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究的局限性在于其只关注单个 暴发 且缺乏更广泛的数据。然而,证据是强有力的,因为它基于现实世界的事件,并且所提出的框架基于实际应用。

Signal integration challenges in African epidemic intelligence: Lessons from the 2026 Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak and application of the PREIS framework

Authors: Hyacinthe R. Zabre, Hyacinthe R. Zabré
Original abstract (English)
On 17 May 2026, the Director-General of the World Health Organization determined that Ebola disease caused by Bundibugyo virus in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda constituted a Public Health Emergency of International Concern. Ten days separated the initial alert (05 May) from laboratory confirmation (15 May) although multiple non-laboratory signals were already active. Using this outbreak, we argue that Africa’s principal detection challenge is often not signal availability but the timely interpretive integration of concurrent multi-source signals, and we outline how a reproducible analytical layer – the Pan-African Real-time Epidemiological Intelligence System (PREIS) framework – might support it.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the challenges in integrating multi-source signals for epidemic intelligence in Africa, particularly focusing on the 2026 Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak. The background highlights the need for improved systems to interpret signals promptly, as the outbreak demonstrated delays in confirming the disease.
Design and population
The study analyzed the 2026 Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda, which occurred between 05 May and 15 May 2026. It involved multiple non-laboratory signals that were active before laboratory confirmation.
Core methods
The study used the PREIS framework to develop a reproducible analytical layer for integrating signals. It emphasized the importance of timely interpretive integration of concurrent multi-source signals to improve epidemic intelligence.
Main results
The outbreak showed that the initial alert was made on 05 May 2026, and laboratory confirmation followed on 15 May 2026, with ten days separating these events. Multiple non-laboratory signals were already active during this period.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that Africa’s main challenge in epidemic detection is not the availability of signals but their timely integration. They introduced the PREIS framework as a novel approach to address this issue.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform the development of more effective surveillance systems for epidemic intelligence in Africa, particularly for diseases like Ebola virus disease. They highlight the importance of integrating multi-source signals for rapid response.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its focus on a single outbreak and the lack of broader data. However, the evidence is strong as it is based on real-world events and the proposed framework is grounded in practical application.

埃博拉病毒病 和 2026 年 FIFA 世界杯群众集会:对全球卫生安全的影响

Ebola virus disease and the 2026 FIFA World Cup mass gatherings: implications for global health security
作者： Akinwumi A. Akinbodewa, Olumuyiwa E. Ariyo, Olorunfemi A. Ogundele, Oluwaferanmi M. Akinbodewa
摘要中文翻译2026年国际足联(FIFA)世界杯于2026年6月11日在墨西哥拉开首场比赛。这无疑是国际足联举办的赛事历史上规模最大的足球赛事,美国、加拿大和墨西哥三个国家联合主办了 48 个国家的赛事,共有 104 场比赛。相比之下,之前的赛事(例如 2022 年在卡塔尔举行的赛事)仅限于 32 个参赛国家和一个 宿主 国家。非洲参赛国家数量从 5 个增加到 9 个,其中以本届 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 暴发 为中心,刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 是通过洲际附加赛晋级的参赛国家之一。同样重要的是,这是历时最长的世界杯赛事,历时 39 天,而之前为 32 天。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在评估 2026 年国际足联世界杯大型集会对全球卫生安全的影响,特别是在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 正在进行的 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 暴发 的背景下。
设计与对象
该研究分析了 2026 年 FIFA 世界杯,该赛事由美国、加拿大和墨西哥主办,涉及 48 个国家,共进行 104 场比赛。此次活动历时39天,刚果民主共和国作为九个非洲国家之一参加。
核心方法
该研究采用描述性分析来评估与世界杯赛事相关的潜在风险,重点关注参赛国家数量、赛事持续时间以及刚果民主共和国纳入赛事。
主要结果
2026 年 FIFA 世界杯有九个非洲国家参赛,其中包括受到 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 影响的刚果民主共和国。此次活动持续了39天,比之前的32天更长。

科研与公卫意义

局限与证据强度
该研究因其描述性性质和缺乏疾病 传播 风险的定量数据而受到限制。证据是温和的,因为它依赖于事件特征而不是直接的健康结果。

Ebola virus disease and the 2026 FIFA World Cup mass gatherings: implications for global health security

Authors: Akinwumi A. Akinbodewa, Olumuyiwa E. Ariyo, Olorunfemi A. Ogundele, Oluwaferanmi M. Akinbodewa
Original abstract (English)
The 2026 Fédération Internationale de Football Association (FIFA) World Cup kicked off on June 11, 2026 in Mexico with the first opening match. It is unarguably the largest football event in the history of FIFA-organized tournaments, with three nations United States of America, Canada, and Mexico jointly hosting a 48-nation tournament featuring 104 matches. In contrast, previous tournaments, such as the 2022 event in Qatar, were limited to 32 participating countries and a single host nation. The number of participating countries from Africa was raised from five to nine with the epicentre of the current Ebola virus disease (EVD) outbreak, DR Congo (DRC) one of the participating countries having qualified through the intercontinental play off. Equally important is the fact that it is the longest running World Cup event lasting 39 days compared to 32 days previously.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to assess the implications of the 2026 FIFA World Cup mass gatherings on global health security, particularly in the context of the ongoing Ebola virus disease (EVD) outbreak in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC).
Design and population
The study analyzed the 2026 FIFA World Cup, which was hosted by the United States of America, Canada, and Mexico, and involved 48 nations with 104 matches. The event spanned 39 days, with DRC participating as one of the nine African nations.
Core methods
The study used descriptive analysis to evaluate the potential risks associated with the World Cup event, focusing on the number of participating countries, the duration of the event, and the inclusion of DRC in the tournament.
Main results
The 2026 FIFA World Cup featured nine African nations, including DRC, which had been affected by an Ebola virus disease outbreak. The event lasted 39 days, longer than the previous 32-day duration.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that the increased participation of African nations, including DRC, in the World Cup could pose significant challenges for global health security due to the potential for disease transmission during large gatherings.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings highlight the need for enhanced surveillance and preparedness measures during international mass gatherings, particularly in regions affected by Ebola virus disease.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its descriptive nature and lack of quantitative data on disease transmission risks. The evidence is moderate as it relies on event characteristics rather than direct health outcomes.

刚果民主共和国2026年布尼迪布焦埃博拉病毒疫情中检测-隔离-泄漏的影响

Impact of Detection-Isolation-Leakage on the 2026 DRC Bundibugyo Ebolavirus Outbreak
作者： Tamer Oraby, Dadi Falay, Martial L. Ndeffo-Mbah, Ndeffo-Mbah, M. L.
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在评估2026年刚果民主共和国本迪布焦埃博拉病毒暴发期间隔离泄漏如何影响社区传播,因为病例隔离是主要控制策略,但当患者在康复前逃离隔离设施时,其有效性会受到影响。
设计与对象
该研究于 2026 年 5 月 14 日至 6 月 17 日在刚果民主共和国伊图里省进行,重点关注据报告离开隔离设施但未正式出院的 175 人。
核心方法
研究人员改进了 SEIHFR 框架以区分不同的感染状态,并使用贝叶斯推理使模型适合每日 监测 数据、逃亡者计数和隔离人口普查记录。他们估计了泄漏率、报告和检测概率以及 传播 率,同时根据 BDBV 文献修正了其他参数。
主要结果
泄漏使检测相关的繁殖数 [R](p_d) 从大约 3.2 增加到 5 以上。消除泄漏使累积感染减少了约三分之一,从 1,120 例减少到 764 例,而控制所需的最低检测水平从无泄漏情况下的 p_d [≥] 0.73 增加到拟合泄漏率下的 p_d [≥] 0.87。
解释与创新
作者解释说,隔离泄漏代表了一个主要的 传播 途径,到目前为止,该途径基本上未被测量,他们强调了早期社区驱动的努力在多层响应中控制泄漏的重要性。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明需要改进隔离方案和社区参与,以在 传播 期间缓解 传播 的影响,强调泄漏在疾病传播中的作用以及早期干预的重要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究依赖于基于模型的估计,并根据 BDBV 文献假设参数,这可能会带来不确定性。然而,考虑到贝叶斯推理的使用和 监测 数据的可用性,证据是强有力的。

Impact of Detection-Isolation-Leakage on the 2026 DRC Bundibugyo Ebolavirus Outbreak

Authors: Tamer Oraby, Dadi Falay, Martial L. Ndeffo-Mbah, Ndeffo-Mbah, M. L.
Original abstract (English)
The 17th Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, announced on 15 May 2026, was attributed to Bundibugyo ebolavirus (BDBV). Although case isolation is the main control strategy, its effectiveness is compromised when patients escape isolation facilities before recovery. Between 14 May and 17 June 2026, 175 individuals reportedly left isolation facilities without formal discharge across Ituri Province. We assessed how this "isolation leakage" affects community transmission. We refined the SEIHFR framework to distinguish undetected community infections, detected but not-yet-isolated cases, isolated individuals, leakage, funeral-associated transmission, and removals. Using Bayesian inference, we fitted the model to daily Ituri surveillance data, escapee counts, and isolation census records. We estimated the leakage rate, reporting and detection probabilities, and the transmission rate, while fixing other parameters based on the BDBV literature. The model reproduced confirmed cases, deaths, discharges, and escapees. We estimated R_0=3.67 (95% HDI: 2.0-5.7), a leakage rate of {rho} {approx} 0.034 day^-1 (0.022-0.051), and high contact-tracing-driven detection (p_d {approx} 0.91-0.99). Leakage increased the detection-dependent reproduction number [R](p_d) from approximately 3.2 to above 5. Eliminating leakage reduced cumulative infections by about one-third, from 1,120 to 764, while the minimum detection level required for control increased from p_d [≥] 0.73 without leakage to p_d [≥] 0.87 at the fitted leakage rate. Shortening time to isolation prevented the most infections (73.4%; 59-84), followed by reducing leakage (29.7%; 14-52) and re-isolating escapees (12.6%; 6-24). Delaying leakage reduction until week 4 reduced its benefit from about 27% to below 2%. Isolation leakage represents a major transmission pathway that has until now gone largely unmeasured. While rapid initiation of isolation is highly beneficial, it cannot compensate for permeable isolation; therefore, early, community-driven efforts to control leakage, embedded within a multilayered response, are critical.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to assess how isolation leakage affects community transmission during the 2026 DRC Bundibugyo ebolavirus outbreak, as case isolation is the main control strategy but its effectiveness is compromised when patients escape isolation facilities before recovery.
Design and population
The study was conducted in Ituri Province, Democratic Republic of the Congo, between 14 May and 17 June 2026, focusing on 175 individuals who reportedly left isolation facilities without formal discharge.
Core methods
The researchers refined the SEIHFR framework to distinguish different infection states and used Bayesian inference to fit the model to daily surveillance data, escapee counts, and isolation census records. They estimated the leakage rate, reporting and detection probabilities, and the transmission rate, while fixing other parameters based on the BDBV literature.
Main results
Leakage increased the detection-dependent reproduction number [R](p_d) from approximately 3.2 to above 5. Eliminating leakage reduced cumulative infections by about one-third, from 1,120 to 764, while the minimum detection level required for control increased from p_d [≥] 0.73 without leakage to p_d [≥] 0.87 at the fitted leakage rate.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that isolation leakage represents a major transmission pathway that has until now gone largely unmeasured, and they highlighted the importance of early community-driven efforts to control leakage within a multilayered response.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform the need for improved isolation protocols and community engagement to mitigate transmission during outbreaks, emphasizing the role of leakage in disease spread and the importance of early intervention.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study relies on model-based estimates and assumes parameters based on the BDBV literature, which may introduce uncertainty. However, the evidence is strong given the use of Bayesian inference and the availability of surveillance data.

超越埃博拉疾病:政府与公民关系在塑造社区 暴发 行为和结果中的作用

Beyond Ebola disease: The role of government-citizen relationship in shaping Community outbreak behavior and outcomes
作者： Kasadha Nasser, Klara Fischer, Erika Chenais, Juliet Kiguli, Peter Waiswa, Siya Aggrey, Mats Målqvist, Nasser K, Fischer K, Chenais E
摘要中文翻译资助方未参与研究设计、数据收集与分析、发表决策或手稿准备。此前研究已在刚果民主共和国(DRC)报告了类似发现[2–5]。我们的研究结果表明,埃博拉病毒病的流行病学及其对疾病的影响受到贫困、脆弱治理和医疗体系不健全等结构性挑战的影响。图表引用:Nasser K,Fischer K,Chenais E,Kiguli J,Waiswa P,Aggrey S,等(2026)超越埃博拉病毒病:政府-公民关系在塑造社区暴发行为与结局中的作用。PLOS全球公共卫生 6(8): e0007186。https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pgph.0007186 编辑:Julia Robinson,PLOS:公共科学图书馆,美利坚合众国 发表时间:2026年8月31日 版权声明:© 2026 Nasser等。本文为开放获取文章,在署名原作者和原始出处被标注的情况下,允许无限制使用、分发和复制,条件为遵守知识共享署名许可协议。资助:本述评基于乌干达“社区参与改善埃博拉病毒病暴发预防与应对——面向全球与本地策略的共同设计”项目实地研究观察,该项目由瑞典研究理事会资助,资助号:VR 2024-02662。利益冲突:作者已声明不存在利益冲突。引言 刚果民主共和国(DRC)的持续埃博拉病毒病暴发在乌干达近期被宣布结束,凸显了在复杂社会、政治与经济环境下应对埃博拉病毒病的准备与响应的持久挑战。尽管医学已取得进展[1],但暴发仍反复出现,暴露出整个缓解工作链中的关键缺口。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在探讨埃博拉 暴发 期间政府与公民关系如何影响社区行为和结果,强调需要解决公共卫生干预措施中的信任问题。刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 正在爆发的埃博拉病毒 暴发 最近在乌干达宣布结束,突显出在复杂的社会、政治和经济环境中,暴发 的准备和应对工作面临着持续的挑战。
设计与对象
该研究基于“社区参与改善乌干达埃博拉疾病 暴发 预防和应对 - 共同设计未来全球和地方战略”项目中进行的实地观察。它重点关注埃博拉 暴发 期间乌干达和刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 的当地社区,研究对政府主导干预措施的信任如何影响公共卫生措施的遵守情况。
核心方法
该研究利用了乌干达和刚果民主共和国埃博拉 暴发 期间社区互动的实地观察和定性数据。它分析了政府与公民的关系如何影响社区对风险的认知以及对 暴发 应对策略的遵守情况。
主要结果
研究发现,乌干达和刚果民主共和国的当地社区将埃博拉病毒贴上了“暴发”的标签,称其是政府根据隐藏议程捏造的。这种怀疑导致忽视风险和忽视缓解措施,导致公共卫生干预措施的遵守情况不佳。
解释与创新
作者将这些发现解释为证据,表明对政府的信任对于有效的 暴发 应对至关重要,他们强调了将 暴发 结果与政府与公民关系的动态联系起来的新颖性,而不仅仅是与医学或流行病学因素联系起来。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,需要改进公共卫生干预措施中的社区参与和信任建立策略,特别是 埃博拉病毒病。他们强调了解 暴发 管理中的社会和政治背景对于增强合规性和改善结果的重要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于观察数据和定性分析,这可能会引入偏差并限制研究结果的普遍性。然而,这些证据得到了实地研究的支持,并且与之前类似主题的研究相一致,提供了中等程度的可信度。

Beyond Ebola disease: The role of government-citizen relationship in shaping Community outbreak behavior and outcomes

Authors: Kasadha Nasser, Klara Fischer, Erika Chenais, Juliet Kiguli, Peter Waiswa, Siya Aggrey, Mats Målqvist, Nasser K, Fischer K, Chenais E
Original abstract (English)
The funder had no role in study design, data collection and analysis, decision to publish, or preparation of the manuscript. Previous research has reported similar findings from DRC [2–5]. Our findings suggest that the epidemiology and disease impact of Ebola are affected by structural challenges such as poverty, weak governance, and inadequate healthcare systems. (2026) Beyond Ebola disease: The role of government-citizen relationship in shaping Community outbreak behavior and outcomes. PLOS Glob Public Health 6(8): e0007186. https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pgph.0007186 Editor: Julia Robinson, PLOS: Public Library of Science, UNITED STATES OF AMERICA Published: August 31, 2026 Copyright: © 2026 Nasser et al. Introduction The ongoing Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), which was recently declared over in Uganda, underscores persistent challenges of preparedness and response to Ebola in complex social, political, and economic environments. Despite medical advances [1] outbreaks recur, exposing critical gaps in the entire mitigation continuum. Ef...
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to explore how government-citizen relationships influence community behavior and outcomes during Ebola outbreaks, highlighting the need to address trust issues in public health interventions. The ongoing Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), recently declared over in Uganda, underscores persistent challenges in outbreak preparedness and response in complex social, political, and economic environments.
Design and population
The study is based on field observations conducted within the project "Community Engagement for Improved Ebola Disease Outbreak Prevention and Response in Uganda - Co-design for Future Global and Local Strategies". It focuses on local communities in Uganda and the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) during Ebola outbreaks, examining how trust in government-led interventions affects compliance with public health measures.
Core methods
The study utilized field observations and qualitative data from community interactions during Ebola outbreaks in Uganda and DRC. It analyzed how government-citizen relationships shaped community perceptions of risk and compliance with outbreak response strategies.
Main results
The study found that local communities in Uganda and DRC have labeled Ebola outbreaks as being made up by the government based on hidden agendas. This suspicion leads to dismissal of risks and disregard of mitigation efforts, resulting in suboptimal compliance with public health interventions.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret these findings as evidence that trust in government is critical for effective outbreak response, and they highlight the novelty of linking outbreak outcomes to the dynamics of government-citizen relationships rather than solely to medical or epidemiological factors.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform the need for improved community engagement and trust-building strategies in public health interventions, particularly for Ebola virus disease. They emphasize the importance of understanding social and political contexts in outbreak management to enhance compliance and improve outcomes.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on observational data and qualitative analysis, which may introduce bias and limit the generalizability of findings. However, the evidence is supported by field studies and aligns with previous research on similar themes, providing a moderate level of confidence.

小分子通过糖蛋白稳定化抑制 埃博拉病毒 进入

A small molecule inhibits Ebola virus entry through glycoprotein stabilization
作者： Fan Bu, Gang Ye, Kiran L. Sharma, Bruno La Rosa, Murugesan Palaniappan, Martin M. Matzuk, Damian W. Young, Hailey Turner-Hubbard, Kimberly Morsheimer, Callum Owen
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
埃博拉病毒 (EBOV) 导致 暴发 的致死率很高,但针对 EBOV 糖蛋白 (GP) 的有效小分子抑制剂仍然有限。
设计与对象
Here, using DNA-encoded chemical libraries, we screen 4.73 billion compounds against EBOV GP and, through stereochemical optimization, identify MWAC-3634.
核心方法
该化合物以高亲和力 (K d = 40.7 nM) 结合 GP,有效中和感染 (IC50 = 0.65 nM),未显示出可检测到的细胞毒性,并在小鼠中表现出良好的药代动力学。
主要结果
这种效力大大超过了已知的针对 EBOV GP 的小分子抑制剂。
解释与创新
2.54 Å 分辨率的冷冻电镜显示,MWAC-3634 占据 GP 亚基界面处的疏水空腔并稳定 GP,支持与之前描述的 GP 不稳定抑制剂不同的进入阻断机制。
科研与公卫意义
在致死攻击小鼠模型中,腹膜内或口服施用 MWAC-3634 显着提高了存活率并减少了临床症状。
局限与证据强度
这些研究结果表明,EBOV GP 需要最佳水平的稳定性才能进入,将 GP 稳定性确定为有效的抗丝状病毒策略,并将 MVAC-3634 确立为有前途的先导化合物。

A small molecule inhibits Ebola virus entry through glycoprotein stabilization

Authors: Fan Bu, Gang Ye, Kiran L. Sharma, Bruno La Rosa, Murugesan Palaniappan, Martin M. Matzuk, Damian W. Young, Hailey Turner-Hubbard, Kimberly Morsheimer, Callum Owen
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Ebola virus (EBOV) causes outbreaks with high fatality rates, yet potent small-molecule inhibitors targeting EBOV glycoprotein (GP) remain limited. Here, using DNA-encoded chemical libraries, we screen 4.73 billion compounds against EBOV GP and, through stereochemical optimization, identify MWAC-3634. This compound binds GP with high affinity (K d =40.7 nM), potently neutralizes infection (IC50 = 0.65 nM), shows no detectable cytotoxicity, and exhibits favorable pharmacokinetics in mice. This potency substantially exceeds that of known small-molecule inhibitors targeting EBOV GP. Cryo-EM at 2.54 Å resolution reveals that MWAC-3634 occupies a hydrophobic cavity at the GP subunit interface and stabilizes GP, supporting an entry-blocking mechanism distinct from previously described GP-destabilizing inhibitors. In a lethal-challenge mouse model, MWAC-3634 administered either intraperitoneally or orally substantially improves survival and reduces clinical signs. These findings suggest that EBOV GP requires an optimal level of stability for entry, identify GP stabilization as an effective anti-filovirus strategy, and establish MWAC-3634 as a promising lead compound for development.

Question and background
Ebola virus (EBOV) causes outbreaks with high fatality rates, yet potent small-molecule inhibitors targeting EBOV glycoprotein (GP) remain limited.

Interpretation and novelty

世卫组织关于在本迪布焦病毒病期间使用经许可的埃博拉 疫苗 的紧急指南 暴发:临时指南,2026 年 8 月 31 日

WHO emergency guidance on the use of licensed Ebola vaccine during Bundibugyo virus disease outbreaks: interim guidance, 31 August 2026
作者： Authors unavailable
摘要中文翻译尽管一些研究结果表明可能存在针对 BDBV 的交叉保护,但现有证据仍不足以确定 Ervebo® 是否提供有临床意义的保护或量化 疫苗 对人类的功效。世卫组织关于在本迪布焦病毒病期间使用获得许可的埃博拉 疫苗 的紧急指南 暴发:临时指南,2026 年 8 月 31 日 其他标题 摘要 这份更新的世卫组织紧急指南提供了关于在本迪布焦病毒 (BDBV) 引起的 暴发 期间可能使用 Ervebo®(唯一获得许可的埃博拉 疫苗)的建议,同时考虑到不断变化的情况刚果民主共和国的 暴发 以及免疫战略咨询专家组 (SAGE) 于 2026 年 8 月审查的其他动物、免疫学和观察证据。该指南概述了与超说明书使用 Ervebo® 相关的益处、风险和不确定性,并建议 疫苗 仅在研究方案中使用。它强调了通过环随机对照试验快速生成可靠疗效数据、维持经过验证的 暴发 控制措施、明确传达针对 BDBV 的未知功效以及加速开发和评估 BDBV 特异性 疫苗 和其他对策以为未来政策提供信息的重要性。引用世界卫生组织 (2026)。世卫组织关于在本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 期间使用经许可的埃博拉 疫苗 的紧急指南:临时指南,2026 年 8 月 31 日。世界卫生组织。https://doi.org/10.2471/B09884。许可证:CC BY-NC-SA 3.0 IGO 期刊关系 关系 时间覆盖范围 责任说明 16 页。目录 版本 赞助 PMID PMCID 文档编号 ISBN ISSN ISMN 标识符 其他标识符 格式 语言...
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
考虑到刚果民主共和国不断演变的 暴发 以及 SAGE 审查的其他证据,本指南满足了就 Ervebo® 在本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 期间潜在使用提出建议的需要。
设计与对象
该研究是一份临时指导文件,概述了环随机对照试验的设计,以生成 Ervebo® 对抗 Bundibugyo 病毒的功效数据,重点是 暴发 环境和人群。
核心方法
这些方法涉及审查动物、免疫学和观察证据,并强调需要通过环随机对照试验快速生成可靠的疗效数据,同时保持经过验证的 暴发 控制措施。
主要结果
该指南报告称,现有证据仍不足以确定 Ervebo® 是否提供有临床意义的保护或量化 疫苗 对人类对抗本迪布焦病毒的功效。
解释与创新
作者解释说,在 BDBV 暴发 期间超说明书使用 Ervebo® 与益处、风险和不确定性相关,并且该指南建议仅在研究方案中使用它。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,在 BDBV 暴发 期间应谨慎使用 Ervebo®,并强调需要加速开发和评估 BDBV 专用 疫苗 和对策。
局限与证据强度
由于临床上有意义的保护和 疫苗 对 BDBV 功效的数据不足,证据受到限制,导致可信度评估适中。

WHO emergency guidance on the use of licensed Ebola vaccine during Bundibugyo virus disease outbreaks: interim guidance, 31 August 2026

Authors: Authors unavailable
Original abstract (English)
Although some findings suggest possible cross-protection against BDBV, the available evidence remains insufficient to determine whether Ervebo® provides clinically meaningful protection or to quantify vaccine efficacy in humans. WHO emergency guidance on the use of licensed Ebola vaccine during Bundibugyo virus disease outbreaks: interim guidance, 31 August 2026 Other Titles Abstract This updated WHO emergency guidance provides recommendations on the potential use of Ervebo®, the only licensed Ebola vaccine, during outbreaks caused by Bundibugyo virus (BDBV), taking into account the evolving outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and additional animal, immunological and observational evidence reviewed by the Strategic Advisory Group of Experts on Immunization (SAGE) in August 2026. The guidance outlines the benefits, risks and uncertainties associated with off-label use of Ervebo® and recommends that the vaccine be used only within research protocols. It emphasizes the importance of rapidly generating robust efficacy data through a ring randomized controlled trial, maintaining proven outbreak-control measures, clearly communicating the unknown efficacy against BDBV, and accelerating the development and evaluation of BDBV-specific vaccines and other countermeasures to inform future policy. Citation World Health Organization (2026). WHO emergency guidance on the use of licensed Ebola vaccine during Bundibugyo virus disease outbreaks: interim guidance, 31 August 2026. View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This guidance addresses the need for recommendations on the potential use of Ervebo® during outbreaks of Bundibugyo virus disease, considering the evolving outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and additional evidence reviewed by SAGE.
Design and population
The study is an interim guidance document that outlines the design for a ring randomized controlled trial to generate efficacy data for Ervebo® against Bundibugyo virus, with a focus on outbreak settings and human populations.
Core methods
The methods involve reviewing animal, immunological and observational evidence, and emphasize the need for rapidly generating robust efficacy data through a ring randomized controlled trial, while maintaining proven outbreak-control measures.
Main results
The guidance reports that available evidence remains insufficient to determine whether Ervebo® provides clinically meaningful protection or to quantify vaccine efficacy in humans against Bundibugyo virus.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret that the off-label use of Ervebo® during BDBV outbreaks is associated with benefits, risks and uncertainties, and that the guidance recommends its use only within research protocols.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform the cautious use of Ervebo® during BDBV outbreaks and highlight the need for accelerated development and evaluation of BDBV-specific vaccines and countermeasures.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence is limited by insufficient data on clinically meaningful protection and vaccine efficacy against BDBV, resulting in a moderate confidence assessment.

现场笔记:本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病毒 2026 年 暴发 的特征和监测 — 刚果民主共和国,2026 年 8 月

Notes from the Field : Characteristics and Monitoring of the 2026 Outbreak of Ebola Disease Caused by Bundibugyo Virus — Democratic Republic of the Congo, August 2026
作者： Dumazedier Kabasele, Erika Meyer, Issaka Kabore, Amber Dismer, Joelle K. Kabamba, Anna Bratcher, Constantin Kabwe Kola, Carrie Eggers, Delayo Zomahoun, Noemi Hall
摘要中文翻译现场笔记:2026年由布迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病毒病暴发的特征与监测——刚果民主共和国,2026年8月 早期发布 / 2026年9月1日 / 75 Dumazedier Kabasele1;Erika Meyer1;Issaka Kabore1;Amber Dismer1;Joelle K. 查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究探讨了刚果民主共和国由本迪布焦病毒引起的 2026 年埃博拉疾病 暴发 的特征和监测,强调需要了解这种新兴 暴发 的流行病学模式和应对挑战。
设计与对象
该研究是对刚果民主共和国2026年本迪布焦病毒暴发的回顾性分析,重点关注6个省份和57个卫生区。它报告了截至 2026 年 8 月 21 日的确诊病例和死亡人数,并评估了这些地区的行动应对措施。
核心方法
该研究利用 监测 数据、实验室测试结果和公共卫生响应指标来评估 暴发。它包括对病例检测警报、接触者追踪工作以及隔离能力和安全埋葬做法等关键运营指标的状态进行调查。
主要结果
截至 2026 年 8 月 21 日,刚果民主共和国报告了 5,458 例确诊病例和 2,606 例(48%)确诊死亡。暴发 是史无前例的,100 天内大约有 5,000 例病例。伊图里省占报告病例的 84%,只有 83% 的警报在 24 小时内得到调查,未达到 >90% 的目标。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,暴发 不仅范围广泛,而且控制不佳,在 监测、接触者追踪和实验室检测方面存在显着差距。该研究强调需要改进行动响应策略来应对这些挑战。
科研与公卫意义
结果强调了加强基于社区的 监测 系统、加强接触者追踪和提高实验室检测能力的重要性,以有效管理和遏制本迪布焦病毒 暴发 并防止进一步传播。
局限与证据强度
该研究受到若干反应指标数据可用性的限制,而这些指标尚未报告。该证据基于 监测 和操作数据,可能无法完全捕捉 暴发 的复杂性,从而导致置信度评估适中。

Notes from the Field : Characteristics and Monitoring of the 2026 Outbreak of Ebola Disease Caused by Bundibugyo Virus — Democratic Republic of the Congo, August 2026

Authors: Dumazedier Kabasele, Erika Meyer, Issaka Kabore, Amber Dismer, Joelle K. Kabamba, Anna Bratcher, Constantin Kabwe Kola, Carrie Eggers, Delayo Zomahoun, Noemi Hall
Original abstract (English)
Notes from the Field: Characteristics and Monitoring of the 2026 Outbreak of Ebola Disease Caused by Bundibugyo Virus â Democratic Republic of the Congo, August 2026 Early Release / September 1, 2026 / 75 Dumazedier Kabasele1; Erika Meyer1; Issaka Kabore1; Amber Dismer1; Joelle K. Kabamba1; Anna Bratcher1; Constantin Kabwe Kola1; Carrie Eggers1; Delayo Zomahoun1; Noemi Hall1; Mutshiene Deogratias Ekwanzala1; Natalie Peters1; Amy Schuh1,2; Tara Sealy1; Philip Ricks1; Billy Mpianga Mutombo1; Michael Kinzer1; Benjamin A. Dahl1; Hyacinte Kabore1; Mary J. Choi1,2; John Rossow1,2; Sascha Ellington1; CDC 2026 Ebola Response International Epidemiology and Laboratory Task Force (View author affiliations) View suggested citation Summary What is already known about this topic? In May 2026, an outbreak of Ebola disease caused by Bundibugyo virus was identified in the Democratic Republic of the Congo. What is added by this report? This ongoing outbreak is now the second largest Ebola outbreak in history. The targets for five critical public health response indicators (case detection alerts, contact tracing, laboratory testing, isolation of infected persons, and safe and dignified burials) have not yet been met, and the outbreak continues to expand rapidly. What are the implications for public health practice? Substantial improvements in established outbreak control measures are crucial to rapidly detect and diagnose cases and isolate and provide treatment for infected persons, prevent funeral-associated transmission to prevent additional spread, and control this rapidly expanding outbreak. Altmetric: The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) Ministry of Public Health declared an Ebola outbreak on May 15, 2026 (1). Two days later, CDC activated its Emergency Operations Center as pa...
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study addresses the characteristics and monitoring of the 2026 Ebola disease outbreak caused by Bundibugyo virus in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, highlighting the need to understand the epidemiological patterns and response challenges of this emerging outbreak.
Design and population
The study is a retrospective analysis of the 2026 Bundibugyo virus outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, focusing on six provinces and 57 health zones. It reports confirmed cases and deaths as of August 21, 2026, and evaluates the operational response measures across these regions.
Core methods
The study utilized surveillance data, laboratory testing results, and public health response metrics to assess the outbreak. It included the investigation of case detection alerts, contact tracing efforts, and the status of key operational indicators such as isolation capacity and safe burial practices.
Main results
As of August 21, 2026, the DRC reported 5,458 confirmed cases and 2,606 (48%) confirmed deaths. The outbreak was unprecedented, with approximately 5,000 cases in 100 days. Ituri province accounted for 84% of reported cases, and only 83% of alerts were investigated within 24 hours, falling short of the target of >90%.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings indicate that the outbreak is not only extensive but also poorly controlled, with significant gaps in surveillance, contact tracing, and laboratory testing. The study highlights the need for improved operational response strategies to address these challenges.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results emphasize the importance of strengthening community-based surveillance systems, enhancing contact tracing, and improving laboratory testing capacity to effectively manage and contain the Bundibugyo virus outbreak and prevent further spread.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by the availability of data for several response indicators, which were not reported. The evidence is based on surveillance and operational data, which may not fully capture the complexity of the outbreak, resulting in a moderate confidence assessment.

Bundibugyo at the Border: The 2026 Ebola Outbreak and the Case for Pre-emptive Countermeasure Equity.

Authors: Adamu Safiyanu Maikifi, Auwal Rabiu Auwal, Abubakar Sunusi Ishak, Abdulrahman Garba Jibo
Original abstract (English)
The 2026 Ebola outbreak caused by Bundibugyo ebolavirus in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda exposes a persistent structural flaw in global health security: preparedness remains overwhelmingly reactive and pathogen-specific. Despite the $518 million Africa CDC-WHO joint continental plan, no licensed BDBV vaccine or therapeutic is available; a 21-day (three-week) detection delay and cross-border transmission expose inadequate inter-epidemic investment in non-Zaire ebolavirus countermeasures. We argue for sustained, ring-fenced financing, institutionalised cross-border coordination, species-inclusive diagnostics, and real-time genomic data sharing to move African Ebola preparedness from reactive to pre-emptive.

基于气雾剂的飞机消毒方案:材料兼容性和抗菌功效之间冲突的解决方案。

Aerosol-based disinfection protocol for aircraft: a solution for the conflict between material compatibility and antimicrobial efficacy.
作者： Sandro Klafack, Jessica Kohs, Karla A Schwenke, Paul Siller, Janina Reissner, Holger Freese, Alina Below, Tim Lübcke, Stefan Mehler, Bernd Albert
摘要中文翻译无标题:每年,数十亿名乘客、宠物和牲畜通过航空运输,这增加了严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒1型和2型、H1N1流感、埃博拉病毒及口蹄疫病毒等病原体在全球传播的风险。然而,由于飞机消毒过程复杂且安全标准极高,现有消毒方法仅限于人工表面消毒,而针对无法触及区域的批准消毒方法仍然缺失。为解决此问题,本研究开发并验证了一种有效的机舱消毒方法,该方法利用低浓度气溶胶化过氧乙酸(aPAA,由过氧化氢稳定)实现“干雾”消毒。因此,我们确定了aPAA对广泛病原体(包括细菌、病毒及细菌芽孢)的最低有效抗菌浓度。随后,在欧洲航空安全局(EASA)要求的官方材料兼容性测试(MCTs)背景下,我们在大量相关材料上多次应用所开发的消毒方案,以证明其应用不会对飞机适航性产生负面影响。在成功通过这些复杂的材料兼容性测试后,我们最终在空客A320窄体飞机内验证了该消毒方案的抗菌效果,所用测试微生物为对aPAA最具抵抗力的病毒之一——MS2噬菌体及嗜热脂肪芽孢杆菌(Geobacillus stearothermophilus)芽孢。因此,我们现已能够首次提出一种适用于飞机的气溶胶室内消毒方法,该方法可轻松推广至其他机型及其他大规模运输工具。重要性:尽管近年来出现了多次暴发,特别是埃博拉病毒疫情及严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2型大流行,但目前仍无批准用于污染飞机的消毒方法。由于飞机材料与技术高度复杂,难以在抗菌效果与消毒剂材料兼容性之间实现平衡,以满足高安全标准。本研究展示了一种基于过氧乙酸与过氧化氢混合物的气溶胶消毒方案。该方案通过了欧洲航空安全局制定的相关飞机认证规范及制造商资格测试项目的所有材料兼容性测试,证明对病毒、细菌甚至细菌芽孢均高度有效,且不会损害飞机适航性。通过同时靶向污染表面、物体及空气中的病原体储存宿主,该方案最大限度降低了人兽共患病病原体的跨物种溢出风险,并防止高后果农业病原体通过机械传播传入无疫地区。这使其成为首个可用于补充常规人工表面消毒的半自动空气消毒方法,适用于因接触感染或污染人、动物或物品而导致的污染情况。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在解决对飞机进行消毒的挑战,以防止严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒 1 型和 2 型、H1N1 流感、埃博拉病毒 和口蹄疫病毒等病原体在全球传播。
设计与对象
该研究开发并验证了一种基于气溶胶的消毒方案,使用由过氧化氢稳定的低浓度过氧乙酸(aPAA),在欧盟航空安全局(EASA)要求的官方材料兼容性测试(MCT)的背景下应用于大量相关材料。
核心方法
这些方法涉及展示使用过乙酸和过氧化氢混合物的基于气溶胶的消毒方案,并将其应用于多种介质,包括细菌、病毒和细菌内生孢子。
主要结果
该协议根据相关飞机审定规范通过了所有材料兼容性测试,并被证明对病毒、细菌甚至细菌内生孢子非常有效,且不影响飞机的适航性。
解释与创新
作者将这一发现解释为展示了第一种适用于飞机的基于气溶胶的房间消毒方法,该方法可用于其他飞机类型和大众运输工具。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,这种方法可以最大限度地减少人畜共患病原体的 跨物种溢出 风险,并防止高后果农业病原体机械 传播 进入无病区。
局限与证据强度
该研究的证据仅限于抽象信息,结果基于对 aPAA 耐药性最强的微生物进行的测试,这可能无法完全代表所有潜在的病原体。

Aerosol-based disinfection protocol for aircraft: a solution for the conflict between material compatibility and antimicrobial efficacy.

Authors: Sandro Klafack, Jessica Kohs, Karla A Schwenke, Paul Siller, Janina Reissner, Holger Freese, Alina Below, Tim Lübcke, Stefan Mehler, Bernd Albert
Original abstract (English)
UNLABELLED: Every year, billions of passengers, pets, and livestock are transported by air, which increases the risk of a global spread of pathogens like severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 1 and 2, H1N1 influenza, the Ebola virus, and foot-and-mouth disease virus. However, due to its complexity and high safety standards, aircraft decontamination is limited to manual surface disinfection, and an approved method for disinfecting otherwise inaccessible areas is still unavailable. To address this issue, we present the development and validation of an effective room disinfection method utilizing low concentrations of aerosolized peroxyacetic acid (aPAA), stabilized by hydrogen peroxide, known as "dry fog." Therefore, we determined the minimal effective antimicrobial concentration of aPAA for a wide range of agents, including bacteria, viruses, and bacterial endospores. Then, we applied the developed disinfection protocol to a large panel of relevant materials multiple times in the context of the official material compatibility tests (MCTs) requested by the European Union Aviation Safety Agency (EASA) to demonstrate that its application will not cause a negative effect on the aircraft airworthiness. After successfully passing these complex material compatibility tests, we finally validated the disinfection protocol within a narrow-body aircraft A320 for its antimicrobial efficacy by using the most resistant microorganisms to aPAA among our test organisms, the bacteriophage MS2, and spores of Geobacillus stearothermophilus. Thus, we are now able to present the first aerosol-based room disinfection method suitable for aircraft that could easily be adopted for other aircraft types and other means of mass transport as well. IMPORTANCE: Despite numerous outbreaks in recent years, particularly the Ebola virus epidemic and the severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 pandemic, there is still no approved method for disinfecting contaminated aircraft. Due to the highly complex nature of aircraft material and technology, it is difficult to balance the antimicrobial efficacy and material compatibility of the biocides used in a way that meets high safety standards. Here, we demonstrate an aerosol-based disinfection protocol using a mixture of peracetic acid and hydrogen peroxide. This protocol passed all material compatibility tests according to relevant aircraft certification specifications established by the European Union Aviation Safety Agency, as well as the manufacturer qualification test programs, and proved to be highly effective against viruses, bacteria, and even bacterial endospores without compromising the airworthiness of the aircraft. By targeting both contaminated surfaces and objects, as well as airborne pathogen reservoirs, it minimizes the spillover risk of zoonotic agents and prevents the mechanical transmission of high-consequence agricultural pathogens into disease-free geographical regions. This makes it the first suitable semi-automatic airborne disinfection method that can be used in addition to routine manual surface disinfection following contamination resulting from contact with people, animals, or goods infected or contaminated with high-consequence pathogens.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the challenge of disinfecting aircraft to prevent the global spread of pathogens like severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 1 and 2, H1N1 influenza, the Ebola virus, and foot-and-mouth disease virus.
Design and population
The study developed and validated an aerosol-based disinfection protocol using low concentrations of peroxyacetic acid (aPAA) stabilized by hydrogen peroxide, applied to a large panel of relevant materials in the context of official material compatibility tests (MCTs) requested by the European Union Aviation Safety Agency (EASA).
Core methods
The methods involved demonstrating an aerosol-based disinfection protocol using a mixture of peracetic acid and hydrogen peroxide, and applying it to a wide range of agents, including bacteria, viruses, and bacterial endospores.
Main results
The protocol passed all material compatibility tests according to relevant aircraft certification specifications and proved to be highly effective against viruses, bacteria, and even bacterial endospores without compromising the airworthiness of the aircraft.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted the findings as demonstrating the first aerosol-based room disinfection method suitable for aircraft, which could be adopted for other aircraft types and means of mass transport.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that this method could minimize the spillover risk of zoonotic agents and prevent the mechanical transmission of high-consequence agricultural pathogens into disease-free regions.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's evidence is limited to abstract-only information, and the results are based on tests with the most resistant microorganisms to aPAA, which may not fully represent all potential pathogens.
