进化 几内亚卫生系统资源和与易流行疾病护理质量相关的因素:一项全国横断面研究

Evolution of health system resources and factors associated with the quality of care for epidemic-prone diseases in Guinea: a national cross-sectional study
作者： Dimaï Ouo Kpamy, Fatoumata Chérif, Sory Condé, Fodé Amara Traoré, Sidikiba Sidibé, Bassirou Diarra, Peter John Winch, Abdoulaye Touré, Sakoba Keïta, Seydou Doumbia
摘要中文翻译目的 本研究旨在描述几内亚易流行疾病管理资源的 进化,并确定与易流行疾病护理质量相关的因素。方法进行了描述性和分析性横断面研究,以评估几内亚易流行疾病的管理系统。使用结构化调查问卷收集数据,并通过审查官方文件进行补充,以评估 2014-2016 年 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 之前、期间和之后资源的 进化。使用多变量逻辑回归分析确定与护理质量相关的因素。结果 不同卫生区域的资源 进化 有所不同。在国家层面,总体资源得分从埃博拉暴发前的12%上升至暴发期间的73%,并在埃博拉后时期达到77%。总体而言,60.5% 的医疗机构的护理质量被评为良好。有五个因素与优质护理独立相关:患者诊断机制的存在(aOR = 3.17;95% CI:1.74–5.57;p < 0.001)、治疗 方案的可用性(aOR = 2.42;95% CI:1.27–4.61;p = 0.007)、患者隔离设施的存在(aOR = 4.76;95% CI:1.72–13.17;p = 0.003)、案件管理的有效协调(aOR = 3.04;95% CI:1.10–8.39;p = 0.031)以及专门案件管理团队的可用性(aOR = 8.51;95% CI:4.07–17.76;p < 0.001)。结论 本研究强调,继 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 后,几内亚用于管理易流行疾病的资源得到了实质性改善,反映出卫生系统流行病防范和应对能力的加强。然而,维持这些成果需要持续投资,特别是在卫生系统融资方面。加强诊断能力、确保标准化 治疗 方案的可用性、扩大患者隔离设施、改进协调机制和维持专门的病例管理团队仍应是提高护理质量和增强卫生系统应对未来流行病的抵御能力的战略重点。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在描述几内亚易流行疾病管理资源的 进化 并确定与易流行疾病护理质量相关的因素。目的是了解卫生系统应对此类疾病的能力,特别是在 2014-2016 年 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 的背景下。
设计与对象
进行了一项描述性和分析性横断面研究,以评估几内亚易流行疾病的管理系统。该研究涵盖不同地区的卫生机构,通过结构化调查问卷和官方文件审查收集数据。
核心方法
使用结构化调查问卷收集数据,并通过审查官方文件进行补充,以评估 2014-2016 年 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 之前、期间和之后资源的 进化。使用多变量逻辑回归分析确定与护理质量相关的因素。
主要结果
不同卫生区域的资源 进化 各不相同。在国家层面,总体资源得分从埃博拉暴发前的12%上升至暴发期间的73%,并在埃博拉后时期达到77%。总体而言,60.5% 的医疗机构的护理质量被评为良好。
解释与创新
该研究强调,继埃博拉病毒病 暴发之后,几内亚用于管理易流行疾病的资源得到了实质性改善,反映出卫生系统流行病防范和应对能力的加强。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果可以为改善卫生系统准备和应对易流行疾病的策略提供信息,特别是在资源有限的环境中。他们强调诊断机制、治疗 协议和专门的病例管理团队在提高护理质量方面的重要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究受到横截面设计的限制,不允许进行因果推断。证据基于自我报告的数据和官方文件,这可能会引入偏见。由于这些限制,研究结果的可信度较低。

Evolution of health system resources and factors associated with the quality of care for epidemic-prone diseases in Guinea: a national cross-sectional study

Authors: Dimaï Ouo Kpamy, Fatoumata Chérif, Sory Condé, Fodé Amara Traoré, Sidikiba Sidibé, Bassirou Diarra, Peter John Winch, Abdoulaye Touré, Sakoba Keïta, Seydou Doumbia
Original abstract (English)
Objective This study aimed to describe the evolution of resources for the management of epidemic-prone diseases and to identify the factors associated with the quality of care for epidemic-prone diseases in Guinea. Methods A descriptive and analytical cross-sectional study was conducted to assess the management system for epidemic-prone diseases in Guinea. Data were collected using structured questionnaires and supplemented by a review of official documents to assess the evolution of resources before, during, and after the 2014–2016 Ebola virus disease outbreak. Factors associated with the quality of care were identified using multivariable logistic regression analysis. Results The evolution of resources varied across the different health regions. At the national level, the overall resource score increased from 12% before the Ebola outbreak to 73% during the outbreak and reached 77% in the post-Ebola period. Overall, the quality of care was rated as good in 60.5% of health facilities. Five factors were independently associated with good-quality care: the presence of a patient diagnostic mechanism (aOR = 3.17; 95% CI: 1.74–5.57; p < 0.001), the availability of treatment protocols (aOR = 2.42; 95% CI: 1.27–4.61; p = 0.007), the existence of patient isolation facilities (aOR = 4.76; 95% CI: 1.72–13.17; p = 0.003), effective coordination of case management (aOR = 3.04; 95% CI: 1.10–8.39; p = 0.031), and the availability of dedicated case management teams (aOR = 8.51; 95% CI: 4.07–17.76; p < 0.001). Conclusion This study highlights a substantial improvement in the resources allocated to the management of epidemic-prone diseases in Guinea following the Ebola virus disease outbreak, reflecting strengthened health system capacity for epidemic preparedness and response. However, sustaining these gains will require continued investment, particularly in health system financing. Strengthening diagnostic capacity, ensuring the availability of standardized treatment protocols, expanding patient isolation facilities, improving coordination mechanisms, and maintaining specialized case management teams should remain strategic priorities to enhance the quality of care and strengthen the resilience of the health system against future epidemics.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to describe the evolution of resources for the management of epidemic-prone diseases and to identify the factors associated with the quality of care for epidemic-prone diseases in Guinea. The objective was to understand the health system's capacity to respond to such diseases, particularly in the context of the 2014–2016 Ebola virus disease outbreak.
Design and population
A descriptive and analytical cross-sectional study was conducted to assess the management system for epidemic-prone diseases in Guinea. The study included health facilities across different regions, with data collected using structured questionnaires and a review of official documents.
Core methods
Data were collected using structured questionnaires and supplemented by a review of official documents to assess the evolution of resources before, during, and after the 2014–2016 Ebola virus disease outbreak. Factors associated with the quality of care were identified using multivariable logistic regression analysis.
Main results
The evolution of resources varied across the different health regions. At the national level, the overall resource score increased from 12% before the Ebola outbreak to 73% during the outbreak and reached 77% in the post-Ebola period. Overall, the quality of care was rated as good in 60.5% of health facilities.
Interpretation and novelty
The study highlights a substantial improvement in the resources allocated to the management of epidemic-prone diseases in Guinea following the Ebola virus disease outbreak, reflecting strengthened health system capacity for epidemic preparedness and response.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform strategies for improving health system preparedness and response to epidemic-prone diseases, particularly in resource-limited settings. They emphasize the importance of diagnostic mechanisms, treatment protocols, and dedicated case management teams in enhancing care quality.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its cross-sectional design, which does not allow for causal inferences. The evidence is based on self-reported data and official documents, which may introduce bias. The confidence in the findings is moderate due to these limitations.

一种新型 Fc 优化的抗埃博拉中和抗体,具有延长的体内半衰期。

A novel Fc-optimized anti-Ebola neutralizing antibody with extended in vivo half-life.
作者： Shangde Peng, Zihan Yi, Wen Hu, Fenghao Peng, Xinyi Zhou, Jiannan Feng, Na Wang, Yan Wen, Guojiang Chen, Chunxia Qiao
摘要中文翻译扎伊尔埃博拉病毒(EBOV)是引起埃博拉出血热的主要病原体之一。全人源单克隆抗体Mab114可特异性识别EBOV,具有良好的中和病毒、提高感染动物存活率的功能。抗体药物较长的体内半衰期主要是通过pH依赖性受体FcRn的结合来实现的。本研究采用计算机模拟方法,基于分子对接和力学优化,构建了人Fc和人FcRn复合物的空间构象。确定了人Fc和人FcRn结合的关键结构域和重要氨基酸位点。在此基础上,设计了两个Fc突变体,并以全人单克隆抗体Mab114为模型抗体,构建了两个Fc突变体抗体Mab114-M5和Mab114-M6。通过实验验证了人FcRn转基因小鼠的亲和力、体内外中和功能、体内血清代谢以及体内抗病毒药物活性。结果表明,与 Mab114 相比,突变抗体在小鼠体内的保留时间明显更长,并且也表现出与母源抗体 Mab114 相似的中和 EBOV 活性。Fc突变体有可能在体内具有较长的半衰期,从而减少用药频率,为长效抗体治疗药物的开发提供新思路。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在开发一种新型 Fc 优化的抗埃博拉中和抗体,以满足改进针对扎伊尔 埃博拉病毒 (EBOV) 引起的埃博拉出血热治疗方案的需求。
设计与对象
该研究涉及在人FcRn转基因小鼠中构建和测试全人单克隆抗体Mab114的两个Fc突变体,以评估其体内血清代谢和抗病毒药物活性。
核心方法
研究人员利用计算机模拟方法,包括分子对接和机械优化,构建了人Fc和人FcRn复合物的空间构象。然后他们在全人单克隆抗体Mab114的基础上设计了两个Fc突变体并构建了两个突变抗体Mab114-M5和Mab114-M6。
主要结果
结果表明,与 Mab114 相比,突变抗体在小鼠体内的保留时间明显更长,并且也表现出与母源抗体 Mab114 相似的中和 EBOV 活性。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,Fc突变体可以延长抗体的体内半衰期,为开发针对埃博拉病毒的长效抗体治疗药物提供了潜在的策略。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果为通过延长抗埃博拉抗体的体内半衰期来提高其治疗效果提供了见解,这可能会导致针对埃博拉出血热的更有效的 治疗 策略。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于小鼠体内实验,其结果基于实验数据,未直接应用于临床,证据强度中等。

A novel Fc-optimized anti-Ebola neutralizing antibody with extended in vivo half-life.

Authors: Shangde Peng, Zihan Yi, Wen Hu, Fenghao Peng, Xinyi Zhou, Jiannan Feng, Na Wang, Yan Wen, Guojiang Chen, Chunxia Qiao
Original abstract (English)
Zaire Ebola virus (EBOV) is one of the main pathogens causing Ebola hemorrhagic fever. The whole human monoclonal antibody Mab114, which can specifically recognize EBOV, has good functions of neutralizing the virus and improving the survival rate of infected animals. The longer in vivo half-life of antibody drugs is mainly achieved through pH dependent binding of the receptor FcRn. This study used computer simulation methods to construct the spatial conformation of human Fc and human FcRn complexes based on molecular docking and mechanical optimization. The key structural domains and important amino acid sites for the binding of human Fc and human FcRn were determined. Based on this, two Fc mutants were designed, and two Fc mutant antibodies Mab114-M5 and Mab114-M6 were constructed using whole human monoclonal antibody Mab114 as the model antibody. The affinity, in vivo and in vitro neutralization function, in vivo serum metabolism of human FcRn transgenic mice, and in vivo antiviral activity were verified through experiments. The results showed that the mutant antibody had a significantly longer retention time in mice compared to Mab114, and also exhibited similar activity in neutralizing EBOV as the maternal antibody Mab114. Fc mutants have the potential to have a long half-life in vivo, thereby reducing medication frequency and providing new ideas for the development of long-acting antibody therapy drugs.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to develop a novel Fc-optimized anti-Ebola neutralizing antibody to address the need for improved therapeutic options against Ebola hemorrhagic fever, which is caused by Zaire Ebola virus (EBOV).
Design and population
The study involved constructing and testing two Fc mutants of the whole human monoclonal antibody Mab114 in human FcRn transgenic mice to evaluate their in vivo serum metabolism and antiviral activity.
Core methods
The researchers used computer simulation methods, including molecular docking and mechanical optimization, to construct the spatial conformation of human Fc and human FcRn complexes. They then designed two Fc mutants and constructed two mutant antibodies, Mab114-M5 and Mab114-M6, based on the whole human monoclonal antibody Mab114.
Main results
The results showed that the mutant antibody had a significantly longer retention time in mice compared to Mab114, and also exhibited similar activity in neutralizing EBOV as the maternal antibody Mab114.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that the Fc mutants can enhance the in vivo half-life of the antibody, offering a potential strategy for developing long-acting antibody therapy drugs against Ebola virus.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results provide insights into improving the therapeutic efficacy of anti-Ebola antibodies by extending their in vivo half-life, which could lead to more effective treatment strategies for Ebola hemorrhagic fever.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vivo experiments in mice, and the evidence strength is moderate as the results are based on experimental data without direct clinical application.

Preparing for Ebola: Strengthening Nursing Readiness in the United States
作者： Benjamin Lindsay

问题与背景
这篇社论解决了医疗保健系统和护士调整应对新出现传染病的准备的需要,特别关注 埃博拉病毒病 等高后果传染病 (HCID)。这一背景凸显了传染病带来的持续全球公共卫生挑战以及为应对严重威胁做好准备的重要性。

该研究是一篇社论,讨论了美国针对传染病的卫生准备情况,重点关注 HCID。它概述了国家新兴特殊病原体培训和教育中心 (NETEC) 等组织在支持应急准备方面的作用,并重点介绍了刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 最近发生的本迪布焦病毒 暴发 作为相关示例。

该社论没有描述具体方法或程序,但强调了解 HCID 特征(例如严重性、传播性和环境稳定性)以指导准备策略的重要性。它还引用了世界卫生组织 (WHO) 的 暴发 相关信息。

研究结果表明,医疗保健系统需要为 HCID 做好准备,特别是在本迪布焦病毒病等 暴发 的情况下。他们还强调了对护士进行教育和培训以支持有效应对严重传染威胁的重要性。
局限与证据强度
该证据基于社论,不包括详细数据或统计分析。研究结果的可信度为中等,因为这些信息来自一般陈述和报告,而不是具体的实证结果。

Preparing for Ebola: Strengthening Nursing Readiness in the United States

Question and background
This editorial addresses the need for healthcare systems and nurses to adapt their preparedness for emerging infectious diseases, particularly focusing on High-Consequence Infectious Diseases (HCID) such as Ebola virus disease. The background highlights the ongoing global public health challenge posed by infectious diseases and the importance of maintaining readiness for severe threats.
Design and population
The study is an editorial that discusses the United States' health preparedness for infectious diseases, with a focus on HCIDs. It outlines the role of organizations like the National Emerging Special Pathogens Training and Education Center (NETEC) in supporting emergency preparedness and highlights the recent Bundibugyo virus outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) as a relevant example.

Main results

非洲果蝠对表达埃博拉或尼帕病毒抗原的重组 疫苗 免疫攻击的血清学和转录组反应

Serological and transcriptomic response of African fruit bats to immune challenge with recombinant vaccines expressing Ebola or Nipah virus antigens
作者： Elinor Jax, James Wood, Olivier Restif
摘要中文翻译蝙蝠是多种人畜共患病毒的宿主,这些病毒可导致人类高病死率。由于在蝙蝠体内检测病毒很困难,因此经常使用尸体作为 监测,尽管在缺乏体内免疫学研究的情况下,其意义仍不清楚。为了解决这一知识差距,我们测量了注射编码尼帕病毒 G 糖蛋白或 埃博拉病毒 GP 糖蛋白的 疫苗 后蝙蝠的身体产物和基因表达。我们选择了两个分布范围重叠的非洲蝙蝠物种:Eidolon helvum(确认为亨尼帕病毒属 储存宿主)和 Epomophorus gambianus(确认为丝状病毒属 储存宿主)。使用基于 Luminex 的复合微球免疫测定和假型病毒中和测定,我们观察到大多数接受 Nipah 疫苗 的蝙蝠体内抗 Nipah 糖蛋白的抗体有所增加。相比之下,我们只能在一种蝙蝠物种(Epomophorus gambianus)中检测到对埃博拉 疫苗 做出反应的 β-埃博拉糖蛋白体。Eidolon helvum 的转录组分析显示,参与 ISGyla 和 T 细胞差异通路的基因在响应埃博拉病毒时有不同表达,但在尼帕病毒中则没有,疫苗。这些结果证明了对基因暴露的反应存在物种特异性差异,提供了对蝙蝠适应性免疫系统的见解,并为野生蝙蝠的血清学数据提供了校准。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在解决蝙蝠中的 免疫应答 与编码埃博拉或尼帕病毒抗原的 疫苗 之间的知识差距,因为在蝙蝠中检测病毒很困难,而且其免疫学意义仍不清楚。
设计与对象
该研究涉及测量两种非洲蝙蝠(Eidolon helvum 和 Epomophorus gambianus)注射表达尼帕病毒 G 糖蛋白或 埃博拉病毒 GP 糖蛋白的 疫苗 后的抗体产生和基因表达。
核心方法
研究人员使用基于 Luminex 的多重微球免疫测定和假型病毒中和测定来评估抗体反应,并使用转录组分析来评估对 疫苗 反应的基因表达变化。
主要结果
大多数 Eidolon helvum 蝙蝠在接受 Nipah 疫苗 后,针对 Nipah 糖蛋白的抗体有所增加,而只有一只 Epomophorus gambianus 蝙蝠在接受 Ebola 疫苗 后表现出可检测到的针对埃博拉糖蛋白的抗体。
解释与创新
结果强调了 免疫应答 与病毒抗原的物种特异性差异,提供了对蝙蝠适应性免疫系统的见解,并提出了解释野生种群血清学数据的潜在应用。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现为了解蝙蝠 免疫应答 病毒抗原提供了有价值的信息,这可以为 监测 策略提供信息并改善公共卫生背景下血清学数据的解释。
局限与证据强度
该研究的结果受到样本量较小和使用受控 疫苗 暴露的限制,这可能无法完全反映自然的 免疫应答。由于这些限制,证据的强度中等。

Serological and transcriptomic response of African fruit bats to immune challenge with recombinant vaccines expressing Ebola or Nipah virus antigens

Authors: Elinor Jax, James Wood, Olivier Restif
Original abstract (English)
Bats are hosts to diverse zoono c viruses which can cause high case fatality rates in humans. Since detec ng viruses in bats is difficult, an bodies are o en used for surveillance, though their significance remains unclear in the absence of in vivo immunological studies. To address this knowledge gap, we measured an body produc on and gene expression in bats following injec on with vaccines encoding either a Nipah virus G glycoprotein or an Ebola virus GP glycoprotein. We selected two African bat species with overlapping ranges, Eidolon helvum (confirmed reservoir of henipaviruses) and Epomophorus gambianus (puta ve reservoir of filoviruses). Using a Luminex-based mul plex microsphere immunoassay and a pseudotype virus neutralisa on assay, we observed an increase in an bodies against the Nipah glycoprotein in most bats that received the Nipah vaccine. In contrast, we could only detect an -Ebola glycoprotein an bodies in one bat species, Epomophorus gambianus, in response to the Ebola vaccine. Transcriptomic analysis in Eidolon helvum revealed that genes involved in ISGyla on and T-cell differen a on pathways were differen ally expressed in response to the Ebola, but not the Nipah, vaccine. These results demonstrate species-specific differences in responses to an gen exposure, offering insights into bat adap ve immune systems and providing calibra on for serological data from wild bats.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the knowledge gap regarding immune responses in bats to vaccines encoding Ebola or Nipah virus antigens, as detecting viruses in bats is difficult and their immunological significance remains unclear.
Design and population
The study involved measuring antibody production and gene expression in two African bat species, Eidolon helvum and Epomophorus gambianus, following injection with vaccines expressing either Nipah virus G glycoprotein or Ebola virus GP glycoprotein.
Core methods
The researchers used a Luminex-based multiplex microsphere immunoassay and a pseudotype virus neutralization assay to assess antibody responses, and transcriptomic analysis to evaluate gene expression changes in response to the vaccines.
Main results
Antibodies against the Nipah glycoprotein increased in most Eidolon helvum bats after receiving the Nipah vaccine, while only one Epomophorus gambianus bat showed detectable antibodies against the Ebola glycoprotein following the Ebola vaccine.
Interpretation and novelty
The results highlight species-specific differences in immune responses to viral antigens, providing insights into the adaptive immune systems of bats and suggesting potential applications for interpreting serological data from wild populations.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings offer valuable information for understanding bat immune responses to viral antigens, which can inform surveillance strategies and improve the interpretation of serological data in public health contexts.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's results are limited by the small sample size and the use of controlled vaccine exposure, which may not fully reflect natural immune responses. The evidence is moderate in strength due to these constraints.

埃博拉病毒 感染期间的肝损伤:特征和分子机制。

Liver injury during Ebola virus infections: Characteristics and molecular mechanisms.
作者： Shuai Sui, Kaiyue Zhang, Xiaofan Shen, Xia Chuai, Sandra Chiu
摘要中文翻译埃博拉病毒 (EBOV) 对人类造成严重且往往致命的 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD),对全球公共卫生构成重大威胁。自1976年在刚果民主共和国(DRC)首次发现埃博拉病毒病以来,世界卫生组织(WHO)已三度宣布其为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件(PHEIC)。埃博拉病毒感染的特点是从非特异性发热性疾病迅速进展为严重胃肠道症状,随后出现出血热和多器官功能障碍,病死率高达90%。肝脏是埃博拉病毒的主要目标,病毒引起的肝损伤的特点是天冬氨酸转氨酶(AST)水平升高、轻度高胆红素血症、凝血因子合成受损、肝细胞坏死和强烈的炎症反应,所有这些都与疾病严重程度和 死亡率 风险密切相关。本文综述了埃博拉病毒引起的肝损伤的特点和机制,这可能有助于进一步阐明埃博拉病毒病的发病机制,并为对抗埃博拉病毒感染提供新的策略。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
埃博拉病毒(埃博拉病毒)可导致人类严重且常致命的埃博拉病毒病(EVD),对全球公共卫生构成重大威胁。
证据基础与方法
自1976年在刚果民主共和国(DRC)首次发现埃博拉病毒病以来,世界卫生组织(WHO)已三度宣布其为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件(PHEIC)。
共识与结论
埃博拉病毒感染的特点是从非特异性发热性疾病迅速进展为严重胃肠道症状,随后出现出血热和多器官功能障碍,病死率高达90%。
争议与缺口
肝脏是埃博拉病毒的主要目标,病毒引起的肝损伤的特点是天冬氨酸转氨酶(AST)水平升高、轻度高胆红素血症、凝血因子合成受损、肝细胞坏死和强烈的炎症反应,所有这些都与疾病严重程度和 死亡率 风险密切相关。
科研与实践启示
本文综述了埃博拉病毒引起的肝损伤的特点和机制,这可能有助于进一步阐明埃博拉病毒病的发病机制,并为对抗埃博拉病毒感染提供新的策略。

Liver injury during Ebola virus infections: Characteristics and molecular mechanisms.

Authors: Shuai Sui, Kaiyue Zhang, Xiaofan Shen, Xia Chuai, Sandra Chiu
Original abstract (English)
Ebola virus (EBOV) causes severe and often fatal Ebola virus disease (EVD) in humans, posing a major threat to global public health. Since EVD was first identified in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) in 1976, the World Health Organization (WHO) has declared it a Public Health Emergency of International Concern (PHEIC) on three occasions. EBOV infection is characterized by rapid progression from non-specific febrile illness to severe gastrointestinal symptoms, followed by hemorrhagic fever and multi-organ dysfunction, with case fatality rates reaching as high as 90%. The liver is a primary target of EBOV, and virus-induced liver injury is characterized by elevated aspartate aminotransferase (AST) levels, mild hyperbilirubinemia, impaired coagulation factor synthesis, hepatocyte necrosis, and robust inflammatory responses, all of which are closely correlated with disease severity and mortality risk. In this review, we summarize the characteristics and mechanisms of EBOV-induced liver injury, which may help to further elucidate the pathogenesis of EVD and provide novel strategies for combating EBOV infection.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Ebola virus (EBOV) causes severe and often fatal Ebola virus disease (EVD) in humans, posing a major threat to global public health.
Evidence base and method
Since EVD was first identified in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) in 1976, the World Health Organization (WHO) has declared it a Public Health Emergency of International Concern (PHEIC) on three occasions.
Consensus and conclusions
EBOV infection is characterized by rapid progression from non-specific febrile illness to severe gastrointestinal symptoms, followed by hemorrhagic fever and multi-organ dysfunction, with case fatality rates reaching as high as 90%.
Controversies and gaps
The liver is a primary target of EBOV, and virus-induced liver injury is characterized by elevated aspartate aminotransferase (AST) levels, mild hyperbilirubinemia, impaired coagulation factor synthesis, hepatocyte necrosis, and robust inflammatory responses, all of which are closely correlated with disease severity and mortality risk.
Research and practice implications
In this review, we summarize the characteristics and mechanisms of EBOV-induced liver injury, which may help to further elucidate the pathogenesis of EVD and provide novel strategies for combating EBOV infection.

同一健康和全球防范时代的 埃博拉病毒病:不断发展的 流行病学、基因组监测 和未来的挑战

Ebola Virus Disease in the Era of One Health and Global Preparedness: Evolving Epidemiology, Genomic Surveillance, and Future Challenges
作者： Francesco De Maria, Francesco Branda, Ivailo Alexiev, Dong Keon Yon, Ayşe Banu Demir, Giancarlo Ceccarelli, Fabio Scarpa, Massimo Ciccozzi, Alessandro Russo
摘要中文翻译埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 仍然是最严重的病毒性出血热之一,反复发作的 暴发 对全球医疗保健系统构成挑战。这篇叙述性回顾追溯了埃博拉病毒病 流行病学 的演变过程,从 1976 年首次公认的 暴发 到 2026 年刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 和乌干达持续发生的本迪布焦病毒公共卫生紧急情况。近五年来,埃博拉 暴发 背景和放大机制的公认范围已大大扩大:农村人畜共患 传播 仍然是主要的出现方式,但随后 传播 的规模和影响范围越来越多地取决于引入发生的地点、检测延迟、人口流动性、生态破坏、武装冲突、与医疗保健相关的 传播 以及幸存者中病毒的持续存在。分子 流行病学 和 基因组监测 的重大进展改进了 暴发 调查,实现了实时 传播 重建和幸存者相关复苏的检测。疫苗接种,特别是带有 rVSV-ZEBOV 的 环形疫苗接种,已显示出对抗扎伊尔埃博拉病毒的高效性,但 疫苗 的股权差距以及缺乏针对苏丹病毒和本迪布焦病毒的 疫苗 许可仍然是严重的漏洞,尽管新的候选药物 疫苗 和本迪布焦病毒的治疗方法已于 2026 年中期进入临床评估。人工智能和数字技术,包括人工智能辅助早期预警系统、便携式测序、无人机、区块链和移动医疗平台,为暴发的防备提供了有前景的工具,但其在丝状病毒暴发期间的实际操作影响的有力证据仍然有限,而且它们在资源匮乏的环境中的部署面临着与基础设施、读写能力、数据成本和治理相关的巨大障碍。综合准备框架将生态 监测、弹性医疗保健系统、社区参与和“同一个健康”保护伞下的国际协调结合起来,越来越被视为战略必要性。2026 年 Bundibugyo 暴发 重申,尽管有数十年的经验教训,但 监测 、响应及时性和社区信任方面的结构性弱点仍在继续出现。可持续的多年融资、多元化的 疫苗 平台、区域制造以及数字工具的本地协同设计对于将经验教训转化为持久变革至关重要。准备工作最好理解为一个持续的过程,而不是一种反应状态。

近五年来,埃博拉 暴发 背景和放大机制的公认范围已大大扩大:农村人畜共患 传播 仍然是主要的出现方式,但随后 传播 的规模和影响范围越来越多地取决于引入发生的地点、检测延迟、人口流动性、生态破坏、武装冲突、与医疗保健相关的 传播 以及幸存者中病毒的持续存在。

Ebola Virus Disease in the Era of One Health and Global Preparedness: Evolving Epidemiology, Genomic Surveillance, and Future Challenges

Authors: Francesco De Maria, Francesco Branda, Ivailo Alexiev, Dong Keon Yon, Ayşe Banu Demir, Giancarlo Ceccarelli, Fabio Scarpa, Massimo Ciccozzi, Alessandro Russo
Original abstract (English)
Ebola virus disease (EVD) remains one of the most severe viral hemorrhagic fevers, with recurrent outbreaks challenging global healthcare systems. This narrative review traces the evolving epidemiology of EVD from the first recognized outbreaks in 1976 to the ongoing 2026 Bundibugyo virus public health emergency in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda. Over nearly five decades, the recognized range of Ebola outbreak contexts and amplification mechanisms has broadened considerably: rural zoonotic spillovers remain the predominant mode of emergence, but the scale and reach of subsequent transmission increasingly depend on where introductions occur, on delays in detection, on population mobility, on ecological disruption, on armed conflict, on healthcare-associated transmission, and on viral persistence in survivors. Major advances in molecular epidemiology and genomic surveillance have improved outbreak investigation, enabling real-time transmission reconstruction and detection of survivor-linked resurgence. Vaccination, particularly ring vaccination with rVSV-ZEBOV, has shown high effectiveness against Zaire ebolavirus, yet vaccine equity gaps and the absence of licensed vaccines for Sudan virus and Bundibugyo virus remain critical vulnerabilities, though new candidate vaccines and therapeutics for Bundibugyo virus entered clinical evaluation in mid-2026. Artificial intelligence and digital technologies, including AI-assisted early warning systems, portable sequencing, drones, blockchain, and mobile health platforms, offer promising tools for outbreak preparedness, but robust evidence of their real-world operational impact during filovirus outbreaks remains limited, and their deployment in low-resource settings faces substantial barriers related to infrastructure, literacy, data costs, and governance. Integrated preparedness frameworks that combine ecological surveillance, resilient healthcare systems, community engagement, and international coordination under a One Health umbrella are increasingly viewed as a strategic necessity. The 2026 Bundibugyo outbreak reaffirms that despite decades of lessons, structural weaknesses in surveillance, response timeliness, and community trust continue to recur. Sustainable, multi-year financing, diversified vaccine platforms, regional manufacturing, and local co-design of digital tools are essential to translate lessons into lasting change. Preparedness is best understood as a continuous process rather than a reactive state.
View review five-element analysis

This narrative review traces the evolving epidemiology of EVD from the first recognized outbreaks in 1976 to the ongoing 2026 Bundibugyo virus public health emergency in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda.

Controversies and gaps

The 2026 Bundibugyo outbreak reaffirms that despite decades of lessons, structural weaknesses in surveillance, response timeliness, and community trust continue to recur.

Sustainable, multi-year financing, diversified vaccine platforms, regional manufacturing, and local co-design of digital tools are essential to translate lessons into lasting change. Preparedness is best understood as a continuous process rather than a reactive state.

几内亚共和国病毒病原学的水传播和食源性疾病:对稀疏国家证据基础及其区域代理的批判性评估

Waterborne and Foodborne Illnesses of Viral Aetiology in the Republic of Guinea: A Critical Appraisal of a Sparse National Evidence Base and its Regional Proxies
作者： Rodrigue Azonwakin Akotegnon, Fatoumata Bah, Steven Chokki, Clémentine Michodjehoun, Floride Bernice T. Abatti
摘要中文翻译水源性和食源性病毒仍然是西非腹泻发病率和肠道传播肝炎的主要原因,但几内亚共和国在该文献中占据着不同寻常的地位。该国的综合条件预计将维持强烈的粪便-口腔病毒传播,包括基本卫生设施覆盖率低、管道供应间歇性和饮用水污染程度无法测量,而且几乎完全没有公布有关相关病原体的人类病毒学数据。这篇批判性的叙述性评论考察了对几内亚通过水和食物传播的病毒的实际了解,评估了通常从区域比较中得出的推论的强度,并确定了证据基础失败的地方。文献是通过搜索开放学术数据库和书目索引以及引文搜索和机构数据源来识别的,并根据相关性、方法论充分性和声明来源一致性进行评估,而不是仅根据引文计数进行评估。几内亚的主要证据包括少量环境、兽医和偶然的临床报告,以及因与胃肠炎无关的原因而捕获肠道病毒的急性弛缓性麻痹 监测。尚未发现几内亚儿童腹泻病因的基于人群的研究。在冈比亚、马里、塞内加尔、布基纳法索和几内亚比绍进行的区域研究提供了病原学结构、季节性和严重程度的实质性情况,但它们在轮状病毒疫苗接种状态、水文状况和卫生服务配置方面与几内亚不同,因此它们的可转移性是有条件的而不是保证的。三个结论是站得住脚的。由于 疫苗 尚未进入国家免疫规划,因此轮状病毒 A 几乎肯定是几内亚儿童严重腹泻中相当大且可预防的原因。肠道传播的肝炎病毒在几内亚猪和科纳克里废水中循环,但人类负担尚未量化。2014年至2016年埃博拉病毒病疫情后建立的监测架构在技术上能够生成缺失的数据,并且并未针对肠道病原体。确定了缩小这些差距的优先事项。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
水源性和食源性病毒仍然是西非腹泻发病率和肠道传播肝炎的主要原因,但几内亚共和国在该文献中占据着不同寻常的地位。该国的综合条件预计将维持强烈的粪便-口腔病毒传播,包括基本卫生设施覆盖率低、管道供应间歇性和饮用水污染程度无法测量,而且几乎完全没有公布有关相关病原体的人类病毒学数据。
证据基础与方法
通过搜索开放学术数据库和书目索引以及引文搜索和机构数据源来识别文献,并根据相关性、方法论充分性和声明来源一致性进行评估,而不是仅根据引文计数进行评估。
共识与结论
由于 疫苗 尚未进入国家免疫规划,因此轮状病毒 A 几乎肯定是几内亚儿童严重腹泻中相当大且可预防的原因。肠道传播的肝炎病毒在几内亚猪和科纳克里废水中循环,但人类负担尚未量化。
争议与缺口
尚未发现几内亚儿童腹泻病因的基于人群的研究。在冈比亚、马里、塞内加尔、布基纳法索和几内亚比绍进行的区域研究提供了病原学结构、季节性和严重程度的实质性情况,但它们在轮状病毒疫苗接种状态、水文状况和卫生服务配置方面与几内亚不同,因此它们的可转移性是有条件的而不是保证的。
科研与实践启示
2014年至2016年埃博拉病毒病疫情后建立的监测架构在技术上能够生成缺失的数据,并且并未针对肠道病原体。确定了缩小这些差距的优先事项。

Waterborne and Foodborne Illnesses of Viral Aetiology in the Republic of Guinea: A Critical Appraisal of a Sparse National Evidence Base and its Regional Proxies

Authors: Rodrigue Azonwakin Akotegnon, Fatoumata Bah, Steven Chokki, Clémentine Michodjehoun, Floride Bernice T. Abatti
Original abstract (English)
Waterborne and foodborne viruses remain a substantial contributor to diarrhoeal morbidity and to enterically transmitted hepatitis in West Africa, yet the Republic of Guinea occupies an unusual position within this literature. The country combines conditions that would be expected to sustain intense faecal-oral viral transmission, including low coverage of basic sanitation, intermittent piped supply, and an unmeasured level of drinking-water contamination, with an almost complete absence of published human virological data on the agents concerned. This critical narrative review examines what is actually known about viruses transmitted through water and food in Guinea, evaluates the strength of the inferences that are routinely drawn from regional comparators, and identifies where the evidence base fails. Literature was identified through searches of open scholarly databases and bibliographic indexes together with citation searching and institutional data sources, and was appraised for relevance, methodological adequacy and claim-source alignment rather than by citation count alone. Guinean primary evidence was found to consist of a small number of environmental, veterinary and incidental clinical reports, together with acute flaccid paralysis surveillance that captures enteroviruses for reasons unrelated to gastroenteritis. No population-based study of the aetiology of paediatric diarrhoea in Guinea was identified. Regional studies conducted in The Gambia, Mali, Senegal, Burkina Faso and Guinea-Bissau supply the substantive picture of aetiological structure, seasonality and severity, but they differ from Guinea in rotavirus vaccination status, hydrological regime and health-service configuration, and their transferability is therefore conditional rather than assured. Three conclusions are defensible. Rotavirus A almost certainly accounts for a considerable and preventable share of severe paediatric diarrhoea in Guinea, since the vaccine has not entered the national immunisation programme. Enterically transmitted hepatitis viruses circulate in Guinean pigs and in Conakry wastewater, but human burden is unquantified. The surveillance architecture built after the 2014 to 2016 Ebola virus disease epidemic is technically capable of generating the missing data and has not been directed towards enteric pathogens. Priorities are set out for closing these gaps.

实验动物模型在人畜共患疾病中的作用:发病机制、传播 和预防策略的综述

Roles of Laboratory Animal Models in Zoonotic Diseases: A Review on Pathogenesis, Transmission, and Prevention Strategies
作者： Seyed Mohsen Mousavi Fard, Ali Sarani, Mohammad Manian, Ali Hassanzadeh
摘要中文翻译新发人兽共患病已成为全球卫生日益关注的问题,原因在于病原体从动物向人类传播的发病率不断上升。本研究旨在全面综合当前对人兽共患病暴发及其传播的认知,并重点评估实验室动物模型在该领域的作用、应用、优势与局限性。研究通过同行评议文献检索在PubMed、Scopus、Web of Science及Google Scholar中收录的2000年至2026年间发表的研究,并补充早期关键研究。本研究评估了多种动物模型,包括常规小鼠、人源化小鼠、转基因小鼠、仓鼠、雪貂、豚鼠、家兔、非人灵长类动物及新兴替代模型,重点关注其在研究宿主-病原体相互作用、免疫应答、组织嗜性及疾病进展中的实用性、优势与缺陷。本研究强调了动物模型在抗病毒药物制剂、疫苗、单克隆抗体及创新治疗策略临床前评估中的重要性。近期由SARS-CoV-2、尼帕病毒、H5N1流感病毒、猴痘病毒、埃博拉病毒、马尔堡病毒、汉坦病毒及克里米亚-刚果出血热病毒等病原体引发的暴发凸显了加深理解人兽共患病跨物种溢出与传播的紧迫需求。当前面临的挑战包括物种间差异、转化局限性、可重复性及伦理考量,同时本研究还探讨了类器官、芯片器官平台及计算建模等新兴替代方案。将先进动物模型与‘一个健康’方法相结合,可加强疾病监测并加速治疗开发。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本研究聚焦实验室动物模型在理解人兽共患病中的作用,尤以埃博拉病毒及相关实体为重点。核心问题是评估这些模型在研究宿主-病原体相互作用、免疫应答、组织嗜性及疾病进展中的实用性、优势与局限性。
证据基础与方法
该综述在 PubMed、Scopus、Web of Science 和 Google Scholar 中对 2000 年至 2026 年间发表的研究进行了同行评审文献检索,并补充纳入了早期的关键研究。研究设计包括对人畜共患疾病 暴发 和 传播 的现有知识的全面综合,重点是实验室动物模型。
共识与结论
该研究强调了动物模型在 抗病毒药物 药物、疫苗、单克隆抗体和创新治疗策略的临床前评估中的重要性。它还评估了一系列模型,包括传统、人源化和转基因小鼠、仓鼠、雪貂、豚鼠、兔子、非人类灵长类动物和新兴替代模型,强调了它们在研究人畜共患疾病方面的效用。
争议与缺口
该研究确定了物种特异性差异、翻译限制、可重复性和伦理考虑等挑战。它还指出需要新兴的替代品,如类器官、器官芯片平台和计算模型来解决这些限制。
科研与实践启示
该研究表明,将先进的动物模型与 One Health 方法相结合可以强化疾病 监测 并加速治疗开发。它还强调了解决使用这些模型时的翻译限制和道德问题的重要性。

Roles of Laboratory Animal Models in Zoonotic Diseases: A Review on Pathogenesis, Transmission, and Prevention Strategies

Authors: Seyed Mohsen Mousavi Fard, Ali Sarani, Mohammad Manian, Ali Hassanzadeh
Original abstract (English)
Emerging zoonotic diseases have become an increasing concern for global health due to the rising incidence of pathogen transmission from animals to humans. The present study aimed to comprehensively synthesize the current knowledge on zoonotic disease outbreaks and transmission, with a specific focus on evaluating the role, applications, advantages, and limitations of laboratory animal models in this field. A peer-reviewed literature search was conducted in PubMed, Scopus, Web of Science, and Google Scholar for studies published between 2000 and 2026, supplemented by earlier pivotal studies. The current study evaluated a range of models, including conventional, humanized, and transgenic mice, hamsters, ferrets, guinea pigs, rabbits, non-human primates, and emerging alternatives, focusing on their utility, strengths, and drawbacks for studying host-pathogen interactions, immune responses, tissue tropism, and disease progression. The present study underscored the importance of animal models in the preclinical assessment of antiviral agents, vaccines, monoclonal antibodies, and innovative therapeutic strategies. Recent outbreaks caused by pathogens such as SARS-CoV-2, Nipah virus, H5N1 influenza virus, Mpox virus, Ebola virus, Marburg virus, hantavirus, and Crimean-Congo hemorrhagic fever virus have highlighted the urgent necessity to enhance comprehension of zoonotic disease emergence and transmission. The current challenges included species-specific differences, translational limitations, reproducibility, and ethical considerations, as well as emerging alternatives such as organoids, organ-on-chip platforms, and computational modeling, all of which were addressed in the present findings. Integrating advanced animal models with One Health approaches can strengthen disease surveillance and accelerate therapeutic development.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study focuses on the role of laboratory animal models in understanding zoonotic diseases, particularly in the context of Ebola viruses and related entities. The central question is to evaluate the utility, strengths, and limitations of these models in studying host-pathogen interactions, immune responses, tissue tropism, and disease progression.
Evidence base and method
The review conducted a peer-reviewed literature search in PubMed, Scopus, Web of Science, and Google Scholar for studies published between 2000 and 2026, with supplementary inclusion of earlier pivotal studies. The study design included a comprehensive synthesis of current knowledge on zoonotic disease outbreaks and transmission, with a focus on laboratory animal models.
Consensus and conclusions
The study emphasized the importance of animal models in preclinical assessments of antiviral agents, vaccines, monoclonal antibodies, and innovative therapeutic strategies. It also evaluated a range of models, including conventional, humanized, and transgenic mice, hamsters, ferrets, guinea pigs, rabbits, non-human primates, and emerging alternatives, highlighting their utility in studying zoonotic diseases.
Controversies and gaps
The study identified challenges such as species-specific differences, translational limitations, reproducibility, and ethical considerations. It also noted the need for emerging alternatives like organoids, organ-on-chip platforms, and computational modeling to address these limitations.
Research and practice implications
The study suggested that integrating advanced animal models with One Health approaches can strengthen disease surveillance and accelerate therapeutic development. It also highlighted the importance of addressing translational limitations and ethical concerns in the use of these models.

Authors: Yunzhe Kang, Xiuwen Yang, Wenhui Zhu, Yuanyuan Sun, Shaoyan Qi, Yongkun Du, Guoqing Zhuang, Aijun Sun

由本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病 - 刚果民主共和国 - 世界卫生组织 (WHO)

Ebola disease caused by Bundibugyo virus - Democratic Republic of the Congo - World Health Organization (WHO)
新闻简报世界卫生组织(WHO)报告了刚果民主共和国(DRC)埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病的持续流行。暴发 已得到确认,并已促使采取紧急响应行动,包括向刚果民主共和国分配埃博拉 疫苗。世卫组织和非洲疾病预防控制中心敦促采取社区主导的紧急措施来遏制该疾病的传播。虽然证据中没有具体说明病例和死亡人数,但这种情况被归类为紧急情况,表明卫生系统受到重大影响。应对措施包括 监测、接触者追踪以及丝状病毒疾病临床管理指南的实施。暴发 仍在调查中,预计随着情况的发展会有进一步更新。

跳至主要内容 全球区域 世界卫生组织区域网站 非洲 美洲 东南亚 欧洲 东地中海 西太平洋 当自动完成结果可用时,使用向上和向下箭头进行查看并输入进行选择。

刚果民主共和国。
事件
世界卫生组织(WHO)报告刚果民主共和国爆发埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病疫情。该事件被确认为本迪布焦病毒病 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
暴发涉及多个案例,正在通过疫苗分配和应急响应措施予以解决。所提供的证据中没有明确量化其规模和影响,但这种情况被认为是一种紧急情况,具有潜在的卫生系统影响。

世卫组织和非洲疾病预防控制中心已将 疫苗 分配给刚果民主共和国,并呼吁采取社区主导的紧急行动来遏制 暴发。回应正在进行中,情况正在调查中。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Ebola disease caused by Bundibugyo virus - Democratic Republic of the Congo - World Health Organization (WHO)

News Brief
The World Health Organization (WHO) has reported on an ongoing epidemic of Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). The outbreak is confirmed and has prompted emergency response actions, including the allocation of Ebola vaccines to the DRC. WHO and the Africa CDC are urging urgent, community-led measures to contain the spread of the disease. While the exact number of cases and deaths is not specified in the evidence, the situation is classified as an emergency, indicating significant health-system impacts. The response includes surveillance, contact tracing, and the implementation of clinical management guidelines for Filovirus diseases. The outbreak remains under investigation, and further updates are expected as the situation develops.

IOM:多国 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 - 第 15 号情况报告(2026 年 8 月 17 日至 23 日) - ReliefWeb

IOM: Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak - Situation Report No. 15 (17-23 August 2026) - ReliefWeb
新闻简报The IOM Situation Report No. 15, dated 17-23 August 2026, highlights a multi-country Bundibugyo Ebola virus disease (BVD) outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). As of 22 August 2026, the DRC reported 5,514 confirmed cases and 198 suspected cases, with a case fatality ratio of 48 per cent and 1,200 recoveries. The outbreak remains a significant public health concern due to continued transmission in affected areas. The report notes that operational challenges, such as armed-group activity and insecurity in Ituri Province, are complicating response efforts. IOM is supporting outbreak response activities in the DRC and Uganda, while also strengthening preparedness in neighboring countries. The report emphasizes the need for sustained access, coordination, and risk mitigation measures to implement critical public health interventions. These efforts are part of a broader systemwide scale-up activation for infectious disease events by the Inter-Agency Standing Committee (IASC).
查看新闻五要素
时间
截至2026年8月22日,暴发的报告时间为2026年8月17日至23日。情况报告于2026年8月28日发布。
地点与对象
暴发 正在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 及其邻国发生。受影响的人口包括多个省份的个人,重点是人口流离失所和不安全的地区。
事件
The outbreak involves confirmed and suspected cases of Bundibugyo Ebola virus disease (BVD) in the DRC, reported by the World Health Organization (WHO). The outbreak is ongoing and confirmed with a case fatality ratio of 48 per cent and 1,200 recoveries.
规模、影响与风险
截至 2026 年 8 月 22 日,刚果民主共和国报告了 5,514 例 BVD 确诊病例和 198 例疑似病例。由于受影响地区持续存在 传播,暴发 仍然是一个重大的公共卫生问题。
应对、状态与不确定性
暴发 响应仍在进行中,国际移民组织支持在刚果民主共和国和乌干达开展的活动。行动方面的挑战包括伊图里省的武装团体活动和不安全局势,这阻碍了获得卫生服务和应对工作。

Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA DR Congo + 9 more IOM: Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak - Situation Report No. 15 (17-23 August 2026) Format Situation Report Source IOM Posted 28 Aug 2026 Originally published 28 Aug 2026 Origin View original Attachments Download Report (PDF | 1.67 MB) SITUATION OVERVIEW According to the World Health Organization, as of 22 August 2026, the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) reported 5,514 confirmed cases and 198 suspected cases of Bundibugyo Ebola virus disease (BVD), with a case fatality ratio of 48 per cent and 1,200 recoveries. The outbreak remains a significant public health concern due to continued transmission in affected areas. The evolving outbreak continues to unfold in a complex humanitarian context characterized by population displacement, insecurity and constrained access to health services in affected areas. Continued armed-group activity and localized security incidents in parts of Ituri Province pose operational challenges for response activities, underscoring the need for sustained access, coordination and risk mitigation measures to support implementation of critical public health interventions. These challenges, coupled with ongoing transmission across multiple provinces, reinforce the importance of sustained investments in disease surveillance, early detection, infection prevention and control (IPC), community engagement and case referral systems to support timely response measures and reduce the risk of further spread. Population mobility and cross-border movements remain key considerations for outbreak preparedness and response efforts. During the reporting period, neighbouring countries continued to strengthen preparedness measures through disease surveillance, health screening, population mobility mapping, risk communication and community engagement (RCCE), cross-border coordination and capacity-building activities. These efforts support early detection and preparedness in areas at heightened risk of crossborder disease transmission. In the DRC, surveillance and mobility monitoring activities continued to support early detection and inform preparedness and response efforts in outbreak-affected areas. In line with the Inter-Agency Standing Committee (IASC) systemwide scale-up activation for infectious disease events, IOM continues to support outbreak response activities in Priority 1 countries, DRC and Uganda, while strengthening preparedness and readiness measures in neighbouring and at-risk countries. 区域情况报告:埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 2026 年 7 月 1 日至 31 日 - ReliefWeb

Regional Situation Report: Ebola Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak 1 - 31 July 2026 - ReliefWeb
新闻简报The Regional Situation Report on the Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) highlights a growing secondary reproductive health emergency. Published on 28 August 2026, the report notes that the outbreak, which is the second-largest on record, has led to a sharp decline in contraceptive use, with short-acting oral contraceptive pills dropping by 62.5% and injectable contraceptives by 54.3%. This decline is attributed to widespread fear of health facility transmission and stigma, causing pregnant women to avoid facility-based antenatal care and deliveries, resulting in a concerning rise in maternal deaths. The report emphasizes the critical role of reproductive health services in an area with over 360,000 women of reproductive age. UNFPA is collaborating with partners to enhance risk communication, improve care for pregnant women, and maintain essential reproductive health services. However, only 17% of the required $20.8 million for the response has been funded as of 31 July 2026. The outbreak continues to escalate, with other countries at high risk, underscoring the urgent need for additional funding to sustain life-saving interventions and strengthen prevention efforts.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告于 2026 年 8 月 28 日发布,涵盖截至 2026 年 7 月 31 日的期间。暴发 正在刚果民主共和国持续进行并不断升级。
地点与对象
暴发 正在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 发生,特别影响了拥有超过 360,000 名育龄妇女的地区。
事件
刚果民主共和国的埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 正在引发二次生殖健康紧急情况,孕妇因恐惧和耻辱而避免接受医疗机构的护理。
规模、影响与风险
由于避孕药具使用率下降,孕产妇死亡人数不断增加,其中短效口服避孕药下降了 62.5%,注射避孕药下降了 54.3%。暴发 是有记录以来第二大的,对生殖健康服务构成重大风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
UNFPA is working with partners to scale up risk communication, improve care for pregnant women, and ensure continuity of reproductive health services. However, only 17% of the required $20.8 million has been funded as of 31 July 2026.
A high priorityPublic-health news

Regional Situation Report: Ebola Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak 1 - 31 July 2026 - ReliefWeb

News Brief
The Regional Situation Report on the Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) highlights a growing secondary reproductive health emergency. Published on 28 August 2026, the report notes that the outbreak, which is the second-largest on record, has led to a sharp decline in contraceptive use, with short-acting oral contraceptive pills dropping by 62.5% and injectable contraceptives by 54.3%. This decline is attributed to widespread fear of health facility transmission and stigma, causing pregnant women to avoid facility-based antenatal care and deliveries, resulting in a concerning rise in maternal deaths. The report emphasizes the critical role of reproductive health services in an area with over 360,000 women of reproductive age. UNFPA is collaborating with partners to enhance risk communication, improve care for pregnant women, and maintain essential reproductive health services. However, only 17% of the required $20.8 million for the response has been funded as of 31 July 2026. The outbreak continues to escalate, with other countries at high risk, underscoring the urgent need for additional funding to sustain life-saving interventions and strengthen prevention efforts.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Help Log in ReliefWeb Search Content Search What are you looking for? Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA DR Congo + 1 more Regional Situation Report: Ebola Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak 1 - 31 July 2026 Format Situation Report Source UNFPA Posted 28 Aug 2026 Originally published 28 Aug 2026 Origin View original Attachments Download Report (PDF | 963.26 KB) While Uganda declared the end of its Ebola outbreak on 28 July 2026, the outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) continues to escalate rapidly, becoming the second-largest Ebola epidemic on record. The outbreak is triggering a catastrophic secondary reproductive health emergency. Widespread fear of health facility transmission, coupled with stigma and misinformation, is causing pregnant women to abandon facility-based antenatal care and deliveries, leading to a concerning increase in maternal deaths. At the same time, contraceptive uptake has declined sharply, with use of short-acting oral contraceptive pills falling by 62.5% and injectable contraceptives by 54.3%. This is particularly concerning in an area with more than 360,000 women of reproductive age, where access to contraception plays a critical role in enabling women to avoid unintended pregnancies at a time when pregnancy carries heightened risks. To address the rising number of maternal deaths, UNFPA is working closely with partners to scale up risk communication and community engagement, improve the quality of care for pregnant women, and ensure continuity of essential sexual and reproductive health services. In July, UNFPA reached 82,291 people with reproductive health services through operational support provided across 130 health facilities and four Ebola treatment centres. UNFPA also distributed reproductive health kits to 34 health facilities in the affected area, which will support an additional 11,240 people. UNFPA also scaled up gender-based violence prevention and response activities, as the socio-economic disruptions caused by the outbreak have adversely affected livelihoods and further heightened vulnerability to gender-based violence, including sexual exploitation and abuse. In July, UNFPA and its partners reached 129,380 people with prevention and response interventions. As the Ebola outbreak continues to escalate in the DRC, with other countries remaining at high risk, urgent funding is needed to sustain life-saving reproductive health and protection services while strengthening prevention and preparedness efforts. UNFPA requires a total of US$20.8 million for the Ebola response, but as of 31 July, only 17 per cent of this amount had been funded. Scale, impact and risk

Response, status and uncertainty
UNFPA is working with partners to scale up risk communication, improve care for pregnant women, and ensure continuity of reproductive health services. However, only 17% of the required $20.8 million has been funded as of 31 July 2026.

Population Mobility Mapping (PMM) Report for Bundibugyo Virus disease (BVD) Preparedness in Lubero Territory and Butembo City, DRC (03 - 05 July 2026) - ReliefWeb

Population Mobility Mapping (PMM) Report for Bundibugyo Virus disease (BVD) Preparedness in Lubero Territory and Butembo City, DRC (03 - 05 July 2026) - ReliefWeb
新闻简报A Population Mobility Mapping (PMM) report was prepared for Bundibugyo Virus Disease (BVD) preparedness in Lubero Territory and Butembo City, DRC, from 3 to 5 July 2026. The report highlights the ongoing 17th EVD outbreak of the Bundibugyo strain in Ituri Province, which has expanded to North Kivu. As of 24 July 2026, North Kivu reported 299 confirmed cases, including 188 deaths, with a case fatality rate of 62.9 percent. Over 4,000 contacts were under follow-up. The PMM exercise aimed to identify major mobility corridors and congregation points to inform targeted public health interventions. The report underscores the risk of cross-border disease transmission due to frequent population movements along key routes, including Lake Edward, and the importance of understanding these dynamics for effective preparedness and response planning.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告于2026年8月27日发布,PMM演习于2026年7月3日至4日进行,现场评估于2026年7月5日进行。
地点与对象
该报告重点关注刚果民主共和国(DRC)的卢贝罗地区和布滕博市,特别是东部省份伊图里省和北基伍省,这些省份受到本迪布焦病毒病(BVD)ZX​​QTERMXQXZ的影响。
事件
在布滕博市和卢贝罗地区进行了人口流动测绘 (PMM) 演习,以了解 BVD 的流动模式并为公共卫生干预措施提供信息,这是刚果民主共和国 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 暴发 的一部分。
规模、影响与风险
As of 24 July 2026, North Kivu reported 299 confirmed cases of BVD across 11 health zones, with 188 deaths and a case fatality rate of 62.9 percent. Over 4,000 contacts were under follow-up, highlighting the significant impact and risk of transmission along key mobility corridors.
应对、状态与不确定性
进行 PMM 演习是为了加强跨境疾病 传播 高风险地区的循证准备和应对计划。该报告强调需要采取有针对性的干预措施来应对 BVD 的传播。

A Population Mobility Mapping (PMM) report was prepared for Bundibugyo Virus Disease (BVD) preparedness in Lubero Territory and Butembo City, DRC, from 3 to 5 July 2026. The report highlights the ongoing 17th EVD outbreak of the Bundibugyo strain in Ituri Province, which has expanded to North Kivu. Skip to main content Help Log in ReliefWeb Search Content Search What are you looking for? Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA DR Congo Population Mobility Mapping (PMM) Report for Bundibugyo Virus disease (BVD) Preparedness in Lubero Territory and Butembo City, DRC (03 - 05 July 2026) Format Assessment Source IOM Posted 27 Aug 2026 Originally published 27 Aug 2026 Origin View original Attachments Download Report (PDF | 8.37 MB) The eastern provinces of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), particularly Ituri and North Kivu, continue to experience recurrent outbreaks in a context of intense population mobility, cross border movements, commercial activities, mining, agriculture, and persistent insecurity. These dynamics create frequent interactions between mobile populations and host communities along major road corridors, markets, mining areas, transport hubs, airports, and points of entry, increasing the risk of transmission of epidemic prone diseases across geographic areas. To better understand these movement patterns and inform targeted public health interventions, the International Organization for Migration (IOM), in collaboration with health authorities and local stakeholders, conducted a Population Mobility Mapping (PMM) exercise in Butembo City and Lubero Territory from 3 to 4 July 2026 through data collection sessions, while site evaluations were carried out in the field on 5 July 2026. The exercise aimed to identify major mobility corridors, congregation points, formal and informal points of entry (PoE), and locations where population movements may facilitate the spread of Bundibugyo Virus Disease (BVD) and other public health threats. Since the declaration of the 17th Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) outbreak of the Bundibugyo strain (BVD) outbreak in May 2026, in Ituri Province, the outbreak has expanded to neighboring provinces, particularly North Kivu. As of 24 July 2026, North Kivu reported 299 confirmed cases across 11 health zones, including 188 deaths, representing a case fatality rate of 62.9 percent, with more than 4,000 contacts under follow up. The occurrence of cases in border health zones and along key mobility corridors, including the Bunia, Komanda, Eringeti, Beni, Kasindi, Butembo and Goma routes, as well as air connections through Mavivi Airport, underscores the importance of understanding population mobility dynamics to inform targeted public health interventions to stop the spread of BVD. Given Butembo and Lubero's strategic location along major trade, agricultural and fishing routes connecting Ituri, North Kivu and Uganda, including movements across Lake Edward, the PMM exercise was conducted to strengthen evidence-based preparedness and response planning in areas at elevated risk of cross border disease transmission. Population Mobility Mapping (PMM) Report for Bundibugyo Virus disease (BVD) Preparedness in Beni, North Kivu (DRC), 09 - 11 June 2026 - ReliefWeb

Population Mobility Mapping (PMM) Report for Bundibugyo Virus disease (BVD) Preparedness in Beni, North Kivu (DRC), 09 - 11 June 2026 - ReliefWeb
新闻简报The Population Mobility Mapping (PMM) Report for Bundibugyo Virus disease (BVD) preparedness in Beni, North Kivu, Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), highlights the city's role as a crossroads connecting Ituri, North Kivu, and neighboring countries like Uganda and Rwanda. The report was conducted from 09 to 11 June 2026, following the declaration of the 17th BVD outbreak in Ituri Province in May 2026. As of 24 July 2026, North Kivu had recorded 299 confirmed cases, including 188 deaths, with a case fatality rate of 62.9 percent. Contact tracing efforts reached 83.1 percent of registered contacts, with 3,418 out of 4,112 contacts successfully followed up. The PMM exercise identified key vulnerabilities and mobility patterns, leading to the establishment of points of control (PoC) to interrupt transmission. The report underscores the need to enhance preparedness, surveillance, and response in high-risk areas due to the ongoing public health challenges posed by the outbreak.
查看新闻五要素
时间
The PMM exercise was conducted from 09 to 11 June 2026, with case data reported as of 24 July 2026.
地点与对象
该报告重点关注贝尼、北基伍省、刚果民主共和国(DRC)以及乌干达和卢旺达等邻国。它凸显了该城市作为十字路口的作用以及跨境传播的风险。
事件
2026 年 5 月,伊图里省宣布出现本迪布焦病毒病 (BVD) 暴发,北基伍省和南基伍省等邻近省份均报告了病例。PMM 报告旨在评估准备情况并确定优先干预措施。
规模、影响与风险
As of 24 July 2026, North Kivu had recorded 299 confirmed cases, including 188 deaths, with a case fatality rate of 62.9 percent. The overall success rate for contact tracing was 83.1 percent, with 3,418 out of 4,112 registered contacts successfully reached.
应对、状态与不确定性
PMM 练习有助于识别脆弱性空间和移动动态,从而建立控制点 (PoC) 来中断 传播。报告强调需要加强高风险地区的准备、监测和应对能力。

The Population Mobility Mapping (PMM) Report for Bundibugyo Virus disease (BVD) preparedness in Beni, North Kivu, Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), highlights the city's role as a crossroads connecting Ituri, North Kivu, and neighboring countries like Uganda and Rwanda. Skip to main content Help Log in ReliefWeb Search Content Search What are you looking for? Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA DR Congo Population Mobility Mapping (PMM) Report for Bundibugyo Virus disease (BVD) Preparedness in Beni, North Kivu (DRC), 09 - 11 June 2026 Format Assessment Source IOM Posted 27 Aug 2026 Originally published 27 Aug 2026 Origin View original Attachments Download Report (PDF | 9.07 MB) The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) remains highly vulnerable to recurrent outbreaks, particularly in its eastern provinces. This vulnerability is driven by weak health systems, insecurity, population displacement, intense cross-border mobility, and extensive mining, agricultural, and trade activities. Together, these factors create conditions that facilitate disease emergence and transmission while limiting timely detection and response. As a result, outbreaks continue to pose significant public health challenges, underscoring the need to strengthen preparedness, surveillance, and response capacities in high-risk areas. The eastern provinces of Ituri, North Kivu, and South Kivu are particularly vulnerable due to their extensive cross-border interactions with Uganda and Rwanda, vibrant mining, commercial, and agricultural activities, and persistent insecurity. Major road corridors, markets, mining sites, bus stations, airports, Points of Entry (PoEs), and other densely populated locations facilitate interactions between mobile populations and host communities, increasing the risk of disease transmission and cross-border spread. Since the declaration of the 17th Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) outbreak of the Bundibugyo strain (BVD) outbreak in May 2026, in Ituri Province, cases have been reported in neighboring provinces, including North Kivu and South Kivu. As of 24 July 2026, North Kivu had recorded 299 confirmed cases across 11 health zones, including 188 deaths, representing a case fatality rate of 62.9 percent. Based on data available as of 24 July 2026, the overall success rate for contact tracing and follow-up was 83.1 percent. Of the 4,112 registered contacts, 3,418 (83.1 percent) were successfully reached and followed up, while 694 (16.9 percent) were not reached. The city of Beni serves as an important crossroads connecting Ituri, North Kivu, the southern part of the DRC and neighboring countries through the Kasindi–Beni, Bunia–Komanda–Eringeti–Beni and Beni–Butembo–Goma corridors, as well as air links through Mavivi Airport. This increases the risk of cross-border transmission of the Bundibugyo virus outbreak. The PMM exercise conducted from 09 to 11 June 2026 in Beni helped identify spaces of vulnerability, mobility dynamics and to identify priority interventions including the establishment of points of control (PoC) to interrupt the chain of transmission within and outside the city of Beni. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

非洲本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 疫苗 候选病毒将进入临床试验 - CEPI

African Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate to be advanced to clinical trials - CEPI
新闻简报CEPI has announced funding for Minapharm Group, an African biopharmaceutical company, to advance a Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate to clinical trials. This initiative is part of a broader effort to combat the largest and deadliest Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), which has spread rapidly in just three months. The vaccine, developed using Minapharm's proprietary modified vaccinia Ankara (MVA) platform, is designed to induce both B- and T-cell responses for long-lasting immunity. CEPI's support will enable preclinical development and a Phase 1 trial in Africa, with the potential for further trials to generate efficacy data for emergency use authorisation. The project also aims to enhance Africa's vaccine manufacturing capabilities, leveraging Minapharm's experience in biologics development and production. If successful, the vaccine could provide critical tools to address the current outbreak and future threats, with CEPI and its partners committed to ensuring rapid and affordable supply to affected populations.
查看新闻五要素
时间
Minapharm 的 Bundibugyo 疫苗 是由 Minapharm 集团旗下柏林专家 ProBioGen 基于其专有的改良痘苗安卡拉 (MVA) 疫苗 平台 MVA-CR19 设计的。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国(DRC)、非洲生物制药公司 Minapharm Group 及其位于柏林和埃及开罗的子公司。
事件
CEPI 宣布资助 Minapharm Group 将本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 疫苗 候选药物推进临床试验,作为抗击刚果民主共和国规模最大、最致命的埃博拉病毒 暴发 的努力的一部分。这是根据 CEPI 官方声明确认的事件。
规模、影响与风险
刚果民主共和国的本迪布焦病毒 暴发 被描述为该国有史以来面临的最大、最致命的 暴发。CEPI 的资金旨在开发 疫苗 候选药物,有助于控制疫情并为未来的威胁做好准备。疫苗 旨在诱导 B 细胞和 T 细胞反应以实现全面免疫。
应对、状态与不确定性
CEPI's funding will advance the vaccine candidate through preclinical development and into an early-stage clinical trial (Phase 1) in Africa. The trial is expected to assess immunogenicity and safety. If successful, late-stage trials will be supported to generate efficacy data for emergency use authorisation and licensure.
B routine priorityPublic-health news

African Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate to be advanced to clinical trials - CEPI

News Brief
CEPI has announced funding for Minapharm Group, an African biopharmaceutical company, to advance a Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate to clinical trials. This initiative is part of a broader effort to combat the largest and deadliest Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), which has spread rapidly in just three months. The vaccine, developed using Minapharm's proprietary modified vaccinia Ankara (MVA) platform, is designed to induce both B- and T-cell responses for long-lasting immunity. CEPI's support will enable preclinical development and a Phase 1 trial in Africa, with the potential for further trials to generate efficacy data for emergency use authorisation. The project also aims to enhance Africa's vaccine manufacturing capabilities, leveraging Minapharm's experience in biologics development and production. If successful, the vaccine could provide critical tools to address the current outbreak and future threats, with CEPI and its partners committed to ensuring rapid and affordable supply to affected populations.
Original source text (English)
Securing the future: CEPI's 3.0 Investment Case Find out moreDismiss About What we do Partner with us Our impact News & stories Contact us News African Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate to be advanced to clinical trials CEPI27th August 2026 Share OSLO/CAIRO/BERLIN, 27 Aug—Leading African biopharmaceutical company Minapharm Group, headquartered in Egypt with subsidiaries in Berlin, will receive up to US$16.5 million from the Coalition for Epidemic Preparedness Innovations (CEPI) to advance the development of a Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate, strengthening efforts to develop vaccines to combat the fastest-growing and second-largest Ebola outbreak on record affecting the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). Minapharm’s Bundibugyo vaccine is designed by ProBioGen, a Berlin-based specialist within Minapharm Group, based on their proprietary modified vaccinia Ankara (MVA) vaccine platform, MVA-CR19. A similar technology underpins a licensed vaccine against smallpox and mpox, and has elicited long-term immunity in a vaccine targeting the related Zaire ebolavirus. CEPI’s funding will advance the Bundibugyo vaccine candidate through preclinical development and into an early-stage clinical trial to assess the immunogenicity and safety of the vaccine candidate. The study in humans, known as a Phase 1 trial, is expected to take place in Africa. Minapharm’s investigational vaccine becomes the fifth Bundibugyo-specific virus vaccine candidate supported by CEPI in response to the escalating epidemic. It joins a portfolio of promising CEPI-backed candidates* built on different vaccine technologies to maximise the likelihood of developing a safe and effective shot that could help bring this devastating crisis under control. It is the only Bundibugyo vaccine candidate CEPI is funding that is designed to induce both B- and T-cell responses for comprehensive and long-lasting immunity. “CEPI’s funding to Minapharm harnesses the growing strength of African-led innovation to help confront this increasingly severe outbreak and prepare for future threats”, said Dr Richard Hatchett, CEO of CEPI. “In just three months, cases of Bundibugyo have spread rapidly across the DRC, making this the largest and deadliest outbreak the country has ever faced. We are proud to support Minapharm’s Bundibugyo vaccine candidate: it will round out CEPI’s portfolio, give the world another shot on goal, and advance Minapharm’s proprietary MVA platform, which if successful can serve as the basis for future African-manufactured filovirus vaccines. The faster we can advance a pipeline of promising candidates, the better our chances of saving lives and bringing this epidemic under control.” CEPI’s goal is to bring at least two Bundibugyo vaccines to Emergency Use Authorisation and WHO Prequalification to respond to this outbreak, while also building readiness for future Bundibugyo epidemics. Dr. Wafik Bardissi, Group Chairman and CEO of Minapharm and Chairman of ProBioGen, said: “For more than 25 years, Minapharm has built proven biologics capabilities in Egypt and, together with ProBioGen, established a cross-continental group model combining scientific innovation, proprietary technologies and advanced biomanufacturing.” Dr. Bardissi added: “This programme demonstrates the strength of our capabilities across the full biologics continuum, from design and development through to manufacturing, in response to an urgent public-health threat. With CEPI’s support, we are turning scientific ingenuity into health sovereignty, drawing on the breadth of our technologies and know-how to advance a vaccine response for Africa.” “ProBioGen’s MVA-CR19 platform was developed to enable rapid generation of recombinant MVA vaccine candidates and scalable manufacturing,” said Dr Volker Sandig, Chief Scientific Officer of ProBioGen. “We are proud to bring this platform and our CMC expertise to a Minapharm-led programme supported by CEPI, helping advance a Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate towards clinical evaluation in response to an urgent public-health need.” Dr Jean Kaseya, Director General, Africa CDC, said, "Bundibugyo virus is now driving the largest Ebola outbreak DRC has ever faced. A vaccine candidate advanced by an African company is exactly the kind of capability our continent needs - not just for this outbreak, but so that Africa can develop and manufacture the tools it needs to protect its own people. We welcome CEPI's support for Minapharm’s work as a concrete step toward that health sovereignty." To develop the Bundibugyo-specific vaccine candidate, ProBioGen —a wholly-owned subsidiary of Minapharm based in Germany—will generate the recombinant MVA drug substance under Good Manufacturing Practice (GMP) guidelines. The drug substance is the active component of a vaccine which includes viral proteins that could train the immune system to recognise and fight a specific pathogen – in this case, Bundibugyo ebolavirus. The drug substance will then be shipped to Minapharm’s facility in Cairo, Egypt, to create the drug product – the finished vaccine mixture which combines the drug substance with other vaccine ingredients and packaging ready for testing. In parallel, Minapharm plans to transfer drug substance manufacturing to its biotechnology facilities in Cairo, enabling fully local drug substance and drug product manufacturing. The project leverages more than 25 years of Minapharm's proven track record and experience in the development, end-to-end manufacturing and commercialisation of complex biologics in Africa. If the Phase 1 trial of the vaccine candidate is successful, CEPI anticipates working with Minapharm and ProBioGen to support late-stage trials to generate efficacy data for emergency use authorisation and licensure. CEPI, Minapharm and ProBioGen are committed to enabling rapid, affordable supply of the vaccine to affected countries and to the populations that need them should it prove safe and effective. A positive outcome from this project could also lay the foundation for the future expansion of Minapharm's vaccine manufacturing capabilities, potentially with support from CEPI and other partners. To date, CEPI’s Bundibugyo virus vaccine development programme receives funds from the European Commission through its 2026 grant under Horizon Europe to CEPI, the U.S. Department of State and CEPI's existing core funding. ENDS Notes to Editors CEPI’s funding was awarded to Minapharm for its Bundibugyo vaccine project following its application to CEPI’s Call for Proposals seeking vaccine developers to advance the development of Bundibugyo vaccine candidates. MVA-C19 platform MVA-CR19 is ProBioGen’s proprietary modified vaccinia Ankara (MVA) vaccine platform. MVA is a highly attenuated poxvirus vector that is replication-deficient in human cells: it can enter cells and express the vaccine antigens it carries, but cannot multiply or spread. ProBioGen’s platform combines the MVA-CR19 strain with its AGE1 production cell line platform and TetherexXTM recombination technology to support rapid recombinant MVA generation and scalable manufacturing. The MVA platform holds potential as it has a well-established safety profile in people. Alongside studies demonstrating long-lasting immunity in people vaccinated with MVA-based mpox and Zaire ebolavirus vaccines, positive data has also been obtained in preclinical studies of MVA vaccines targeting Sudan ebolavirus, Marburg virus and other infectious diseases. The technology was also safe for use in immunosuppressed individuals and people living with HIV vaccinated with MVA-based vaccines targeting other diseases. CEPI’s response to the Bundibugyo ebolavirus outbreak *CEPI has worked around the clock with its partners to respond to the Bundibugyo outbreak, announcing its first Bundibugyo vaccine development partnerships just two weeks after the declaration of the outbreak in mid-May. CEPI has to date already invested in Bundibugyo vaccines being developed using mRNA (Moderna), ChAdOx (the University of Oxford/ Serum Institute of India) and rVSV (IAVI with manufacturing by Hilleman Laboratories, and Public Health Vaccines) technologies. To accelerate outbreak response efforts, CEPI has also activated its global networks of laboratories, manufacturers and clinical trial partners, is supporting a clinical trial to evaluate potential treatments and engaging regulators to align on vaccine approval pathways. In parallel, CEPI is working with partners including affected countries, Gavi, Africa CDC, WHO, UNICEF, the World Bank and development finance institutions so the downstream conditions needed to enable equitable access—including procurement, allocation and deployment—are put in place. About CEPI CEPI is an innovative partnership between public, private, philanthropic and civil organisations. Its mission is to accelerate the development of vaccines and other biologic countermeasures against epidemic and pandemic threats so they can be accessible to all people in need. Central to CEPI’s pandemic-beating plan is the ‘100 Days Mission’ to develop safe, effective and accessible vaccines against new threats in just 100 days. CEPI is seeking $2.5 billion to execute CEPI 3.0, its 2027-2031 strategy which will systematically reduce the likelihood, impact and cost of epidemics and pandemics by driving the 100 Days Mission towards an operational reality. Learn more at CEPI.net. About Minapharm Minapharm is a leading pharmaceutical company in Egypt and the Middle East and the largest biopharmaceutical company in Africa, with over 25 years of experience in cellular and bioprocess engineering. Headquartered in Cairo, the Group develops, manufactures and commercializes a broad portfolio spanning complex small molecules, recombinant proteins, and monoclonal antibodies, supported by an extensive pipeline of genetically engineered medicines and cell and gene therapies. Minapharm operates seven manufacturing facilities and commercializes more than 100 life-saving and life-enhancing products. Its two biotechnology facilities in Cairo are the region's longest-established end-to-end drug substance and drug product operations, from cell line to finished vial. Together with its wholly owned Berlin-based subsidiary, ProBioGen AG, Minapharm has built an integrated biopharmaceutical model spanning research, proprietary technology, development and manufacturing. Through its subsidiary, ProBioGen, the Group is a global provider of proprietary technologies for vaccines, complex proteins, and cell and gene therapies, which have contributed to multiple FDA-approved products. Minapharm employs a combined workforce of more than 1,700 people and is listed on the Egyptian Exchange (EGX: MIPH). About ProBioGen ProBioGen is a Berlin-based expert in the development and manufacturing of biopharmaceuticals, viral vectors, and vaccines, powered by proprietary technologies that enhance product quality and features. It has developed multiple innovative vaccine platforms, including Lenti.RiGHT®, AGE1 host cell lines and the IP-protected MVA-CR19 strain, which has a unique genomic signature. Operating for over 30 years, ProBioGen runs three manufacturing lines in Berlin, where 300 employees contribute to advancing next-generation therapies and global biotech innovation. For more information about ProBioGen, follow us on LinkedIn. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Treatment and prevention research on Bundibugyo virus disease must deliver for children, not leave them behind - MSF Access

News Brief
The 2026 Bundibugyo Ebola disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda has highlighted a critical diagnostic gap, according to a technical brief by MSF Access. The report emphasizes the challenges in accessing appropriate diagnostic tools and calls for urgent action to address these issues. While the outbreak is linked to the Bundibugyo virus, the brief does not provide specific case or death counts. Instead, it focuses on the importance of ensuring timely and accurate diagnostics to improve response efforts. The publication date of the brief is June 24, 2026, and it serves as a call to action for stakeholders to prioritize access to essential medical tools. The report does not detail the current investigation status or specific measures taken to contain the outbreak, underscoring the need for further attention to diagnostic access.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

Ebola Virus Vaccine Market to Reach USD 381.9 billion by 2034, Driven by Rising Demand for Outbreak Preparedness - openpr.com

Ebola Virus Vaccine Market to Reach USD 381.9 billion by 2034, Driven by Rising Demand for Outbreak Preparedness - openpr.com
新闻简报The news report discusses the growing demand for Ebola virus vaccines, driven by increasing preparedness against outbreaks and the need for rapid response. The global market for Ebola vaccines was valued at USD 182.6 million in 2023 and is projected to reach USD 381.9 million by 2034, expanding at a CAGR of over 6.9%. The report highlights the importance of vaccine stockpiling and the development of vaccines targeting different Ebola virus species, such as Sudan virus and Bundibugyo virus. It also emphasizes the role of healthcare workers, laboratory personnel, and emergency responders in outbreak response, noting that vaccines can reduce transmission among these high-risk groups. The report underscores the need for continued research and development to improve vaccine effectiveness and availability, especially for species without licensed vaccines. The strong growth outlook is linked to government investments, international coordination, and the lessons learned from the COVID-19 pandemic.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该新闻报道发布于2026-08-31。证据中未明确提及事件日期,但相关信息暗示市场持续增长且已做好准备工作。
地点与对象
该报告讨论了全球努力,特别提到了历史上曾发生过埃博拉病毒 暴发 的中非和西非。受影响的人群包括医护人员、实验室人员、急救人员和接触感染患者的个人。
事件
该报告强调,由于针对 暴发 的准备工作不断增加,特别是在有 埃博拉病毒病 历史的地区,对 埃博拉病毒 疫苗 的需求不断增长。它还提到了针对不同埃博拉病毒物种的疫苗的开发以及疫苗储备对于快速反应的重要性。
规模、影响与风险
The global Ebola Virus Vaccine Market was valued at USD 182.6 million in 2023 and is projected to reach USD 381.9 million by 2034. The report notes that vaccines developed against one species may not provide protection against others, and there is a need for broader coverage to address multiple Ebola virus species.
应对、状态与不确定性
报告指出,政府、医疗机构和制药公司正在加强 暴发 的准备工作。它还提到了使用不同技术平台开发疫苗以及国际协调对于疫苗发行的重要性。content_status 已满,来源是信誉良好的发布商。

Ebola Virus Vaccine Market to Reach USD 381.9 billion by 2034, Driven by Rising Demand for Outbreak Preparedness - openpr.com

News Brief
The news report discusses the growing demand for Ebola virus vaccines, driven by increasing preparedness against outbreaks and the need for rapid response. The global market for Ebola vaccines was valued at USD 182.6 million in 2023 and is projected to reach USD 381.9 million by 2034, expanding at a CAGR of over 6.9%. The report highlights the importance of vaccine stockpiling and the development of vaccines targeting different Ebola virus species, such as Sudan virus and Bundibugyo virus. It also emphasizes the role of healthcare workers, laboratory personnel, and emergency responders in outbreak response, noting that vaccines can reduce transmission among these high-risk groups. The report underscores the need for continued research and development to improve vaccine effectiveness and availability, especially for species without licensed vaccines.
