Original source text (English)
Ebola Virus Vaccine Market to Reach USD 381.9 billion by 2034, Driven by Rising Demand for Outbreak Preparedness The global Ebola Virus Vaccine Market is witnessing increasing attention as governments, healthcare organizations, pharmaceutical companies, and international health agencies strengthen preparedness against Ebola virus disease outbreaks. Ebola is a severe viral disease that can cause high mortality and spread rapidly through direct contact with infected individuals or contaminated materials. The availability of effective vaccines has significantly improved outbreak response capabilities, particularly in regions where Ebola outbreaks have occurred repeatedly. The global Ebola Virus Vaccine Market was valued at approximately USD 182.6 million in 2023 and is projected to reach USD 381.9 million by 2034, expanding at a CAGR of more than 6.9% from 2026 to 2035. The strong growth outlook is associated with increasing demand for outbreak preparedness, government-supported vaccination initiatives, strategic vaccine reserves, and continued development of vaccines targeting different Ebola virus species. Request the Sample Report: https://www.transparencymarketresearch.com/sample/sample.php?flag=S&rep_id=86176 Increasing Ebola Outbreaks Drive Vaccine Demand The occurrence of Ebola outbreaks remains a major factor influencing market development. Ebola outbreaks can emerge unexpectedly and require immediate access to vaccines, diagnostic capabilities, medical supplies, and trained healthcare personnel. Countries in Central and West Africa have historically experienced several outbreaks, creating a continuing need for preparedness measures. Healthcare workers, laboratory personnel, emergency responders, and individuals who have been exposed to infected patients represent important target populations during outbreak responses. Vaccination programs can help reduce transmission among these high-risk groups while supporting broader containment efforts. The increasing focus on epidemic preparedness following the COVID-19 pandemic has also encouraged governments and international organizations to strengthen their ability to respond rapidly to emerging infectious diseases. This shift is expected to benefit specialized vaccine markets, including Ebola vaccines. Development of Advanced Ebola Vaccines Research and development activities are a major opportunity for the Ebola Virus Vaccine Market. Ebola viruses consist of multiple species, and vaccines developed against one species may not provide established protection against another. Consequently, researchers are working to develop vaccines capable of targeting additional Ebola viruses. The development of vaccines against Sudan virus and Bundibugyo virus represents an important area of research. Candidate vaccines using different technological platforms are being investigated to determine their safety, immunogenicity, and effectiveness. Next-generation vaccines could potentially provide broader protection and improve preparedness for outbreaks caused by Ebola virus species for which licensed vaccines are currently unavailable. Advances in viral-vector technology, recombinant vaccine platforms, and other innovative approaches could further expand the product pipeline. Importance of Vaccine Stockpiling Strategic vaccine stockpiling is another important factor supporting market growth. Unlike vaccines used routinely in national immunization programs, Ebola vaccines are primarily required during outbreaks and for populations facing elevated exposure risks. Maintaining emergency vaccine reserves allows public-health authorities to respond quickly when cases are detected. International coordination also plays an important role in ensuring that vaccine supplies can reach affected countries without significant delays. The establishment and expansion of global vaccine stockpiles create a more structured demand environment for manufacturers. Pharmaceutical companies must maintain manufacturing capabilities and supply chains even when there is no active outbreak, ensuring that doses can be produced and distributed when required. Increasing Government and Healthcare Investments Governments are increasingly investing in infectious disease preparedness, surveillance, vaccination programs, and healthcare infrastructure. Ebola preparedness is particularly important for countries located in regions where outbreaks have previously occurred. Public-health agencies are strengthening disease surveillance systems to identify suspected cases at an early stage. Early identification combined with rapid vaccination can help prevent localized outbreaks from developing into larger public-health emergencies. International organizations and humanitarian agencies also contribute to vaccine procurement, distribution, healthcare worker training, and outbreak response. Such partnerships are expected to remain essential for the expansion of the Ebola Virus Vaccine Market. Role of Healthcare Workers in Market Development Healthcare workers are among the most important groups considered during Ebola vaccination campaigns. Doctors, nurses, laboratory workers, ambulance personnel, and other frontline professionals may face elevated exposure risks while treating suspected or confirmed patients. Vaccinating healthcare workers can provide an additional layer of protection and help maintain essential medical services during an outbreak. Protecting frontline workers also reduces the possibility of healthcare facilities becoming transmission centers. As countries improve their outbreak preparedness programs, demand for targeted vaccination among healthcare and emergency personnel is expected to increase. Technological Advancements in Vaccine Development Technological innovation is transforming vaccine research and manufacturing. Modern vaccine development increasingly focuses on improving immune responses, shortening development timelines, increasing manufacturing flexibility, and enhancing vaccine stability. For Ebola vaccines, improved formulations and delivery technologies could help address some of the logistical challenges associated with transporting and storing vaccines in remote areas. Innovations that reduce temperature requirements could be particularly valuable because maintaining specialized cold-chain infrastructure can be difficult in regions with limited healthcare resources. Research into broader-spectrum vaccines could also reduce dependence on individual products designed for specific Ebola virus species. Challenges in the Ebola Virus Vaccine Market Despite its strong growth prospects, the market faces several challenges. One of the primary issues is the unpredictable nature of Ebola outbreaks. Demand can remain relatively low between outbreaks and rise sharply when an outbreak occurs, creating difficulties for manufacturers in balancing production capacity and commercial sustainability. Cold-chain requirements represent another challenge. Some Ebola vaccine products require highly controlled storage conditions, which can complicate distribution in remote and resource-limited regions. Another important challenge is the limited availability of licensed vaccines for different Ebola virus species. While significant progress has been made in protecting against Ebola virus disease caused by Zaire ebolavirus, additional vaccine development is required to address other medically important species. Regulatory requirements, clinical trial complexities, limited patient populations, and high research and development costs can further affect the pace at which new Ebola vaccines reach the market. Countries in Central and West Africa are likely to remain important markets for vaccine stockpiling, emergency procurement, and vaccination campaigns. North America and Europe are also expected to contribute through pharmaceutical research, vaccine development, manufacturing capabilities, government preparedness programs, and international funding initiatives. Emerging economies may increasingly strengthen infectious disease surveillance and emergency response infrastructure, creating additional opportunities for vaccine suppliers and healthcare organizations. Future Outlook The future of the Ebola Virus Vaccine Market is expected to be shaped by increasing investments in pandemic preparedness, vaccine stockpiling, technological innovation, and research into broader protection against Ebola virus species. The development of vaccines targeting Sudan virus and Bundibugyo virus could significantly expand the market's product landscape. At the same time, improvements in vaccine storage, transportation, manufacturing, and distribution could make Ebola vaccination programs more accessible in regions with limited healthcare infrastructure. Overall, the Ebola Virus Vaccine Market is expected to remain a strategically important component of global infectious disease preparedness. While its market size is relatively small compared with mainstream vaccine categories, the need for rapid outbreak response, protection of frontline workers, and development of vaccines against multiple Ebola virus species is expected to create significant opportunities for pharmaceutical companies and research organizations. 非洲本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 疫苗 候选药物将进入临床试验

African Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate to be advanced to clinical trials
新闻简报African biopharmaceutical company Minapharm Group, in collaboration with ProBioGen, has received up to US$16.5 million from the Coalition for Epidemic Preparedness Innovations (CEPI) to advance a Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate to clinical trials. The vaccine, based on ProBioGen's proprietary MVA-CR19 platform, is designed to induce both B- and T-cell responses for long-lasting immunity. CEPI's funding supports preclinical development and a Phase 1 trial in Africa, aiming to address the largest and deadliest Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). The project is part of CEPI's broader strategy to develop multiple Bundibugyo vaccines for emergency use authorisation and WHO Prequalification, while also enhancing Africa's vaccine manufacturing capabilities. The vaccine candidate joins a portfolio of other CEPI-backed candidates using different technologies, such as mRNA and rVSV, to maximise the chances of creating a safe and effective solution. If successful, the vaccine could help bring the outbreak under control and prepare for future epidemics.

这是付费新闻稿。

刚果民主共和国(DRC),非洲大陆。
事件
本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 疫苗 候选药物将由 Minapharm Group 在 CEPI 的支持下进入临床试验。该事件已根据新闻稿得到确认。
规模、影响与风险
本迪布焦病毒正在刚果民主共和国造成规模最大、最致命的埃博拉病毒 暴发。CEPI 的资金旨在开发 疫苗 来对抗此 暴发 和未来的威胁。疫苗 候选药物旨在诱导 B 细胞和 T 细胞反应以实现全面免疫。
应对、状态与不确定性
流行病防范创新联盟(CEPI)正在资助Bundibugyo 疫苗候选疫苗通过临床前阶段并进入早期临床试验。预计第1期试验将在非洲进行。该疫苗候选疫苗是CEPI投资组合的一部分,用于应对暴发并为未来流行病做好准备。

African Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate to be advanced to clinical trials

News Brief
African biopharmaceutical company Minapharm Group, in collaboration with ProBioGen, has received up to US$16.5 million from the Coalition for Epidemic Preparedness Innovations (CEPI) to advance a Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate to clinical trials. The vaccine, based on ProBioGen's proprietary MVA-CR19 platform, is designed to induce both B- and T-cell responses for long-lasting immunity. CEPI's funding supports preclinical development and a Phase 1 trial in Africa, aiming to address the largest and deadliest Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). The project is part of CEPI's broader strategy to develop multiple Bundibugyo vaccines for emergency use authorisation and WHO Prequalification, while also enhancing Africa's vaccine manufacturing capabilities. The vaccine candidate joins a portfolio of other CEPI-backed candidates using different technologies, such as mRNA and rVSV, to maximise the chances of creating a safe and effective solution. If successful, the vaccine could help bring the outbreak under control and prepare for future epidemics.
Original source text (English)
This is a paid press release. Contact the press release distributor directly with any inquiries. African Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate to be advanced to clinical trials PR Newswire Thu, August 27, 2026 at 6:27 AM CDT 8 min read CEPI backs Minapharm's Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate, based on ProBioGen's proprietary MVA-CR19 platform OSLO, Norway, CAIRO and BERLIN, Aug. 27, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Leading African biopharmaceutical company Minapharm Group, headquartered in Egypt with subsidiaries in Berlin, will receive up to US$16.5 million from the Coalition for Epidemic Preparedness Innovations (CEPI) to advance the development of a Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate, strengthening efforts to develop vaccines to combat the fastest-growing and second-largest Ebola outbreak on record affecting the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). CEPI backs Minapharm's Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate, based on ProBioGen's proprietary MVA-CR19 platform Minapharm's Bundibugyo vaccine is designed by ProBioGen, a Berlin-based specialist within Minapharm Group, based on their proprietary modified vaccinia Ankara (MVA) vaccine platform, MVA-CR19. A similar technology underpins a licensed vaccine against smallpox and mpox, and has elicited long-term immunity in a vaccine targeting the related Zaire ebolavirus . CEPI's funding will advance the Bundibugyo vaccine candidate through preclinical development and into an early-stage clinical trial to assess the immunogenicity and safety of the vaccine candidate. The study in humans, known as a Phase 1 trial, is expected to take place in Africa. Minapharm's investigational vaccine becomes the fifth Bundibugyo-specific virus vaccine candidate supported by CEPI in response to the escalating epidemic. It joins a portfolio of promising CEPI-backed candidates* built on different vaccine technologies to maximise the likelihood of developing a safe and effective shot that could help bring this devastating crisis under control. It is the only Bundibugyo vaccine candidate CEPI is funding that is designed to induce both B- and T-cell responses for comprehensive and long-lasting immunity. "CEPI's funding to Minapharm harnesses the growing strength of African-led innovation to help confront this increasingly severe outbreak and prepare for future threats", said Dr Richard Hatchett, CEO of CEPI. "In just three months, cases of Bundibugyo have spread rapidly across the DRC, making this the largest and deadliest outbreak the country has ever faced. We are proud to support Minapharm's Bundibugyo vaccine candidate: it will round out CEPI's portfolio, give the world another shot on goal, and advance Minapharm's proprietary MVA platform, which if successful can serve as the basis for future African-manufactured filovirus vaccines. The faster we can advance a pipeline of promising candidates, the better our chances of saving lives and bringing this epidemic under control." CEPI's goal is to bring at least two Bundibugyo vaccines to Emergency Use Authorisation and WHO Prequalification to respond to this outbreak, while also building readiness for future Bundibugyo epidemics. Dr. Wafik Bardissi, Group Chairman and CEO of Minapharm and Chairman of ProBioGen , said: "For more than 25 years, Minapharm has built proven biologics capabilities in Egypt and, together with ProBioGen, established a cross-continental group model combining scientific innovation, proprietary technologies and advanced biomanufacturing." Dr. Bardissi added: "This programme demonstrates the strength of our capabilities across the full biologics continuum, from design and development through to manufacturing, in response to an urgent public-health threat. With CEPI's support, we are turning scientific ingenuity into health sovereignty, drawing on the breadth of our technologies and know-how to advance a vaccine response for Africa." "ProBioGen's MVA-CR19 platform was developed to enable rapid generation of recombinant MVA vaccine candidates and scalable manufacturing," said Dr Volker Sandig, Chief Scientific Officer of ProBioGen . "We are proud to bring this platform and our CMC expertise to a Minapharm-led programme supported by CEPI, helping advance a Bundibugyo ebolavirus vaccine candidate towards clinical evaluation in response to an urgent public-health need." Dr Jean Kaseya, Director General, Africa CDC , said, "Bundibugyo virus is now driving the largest Ebola outbreak DRC has ever faced. A vaccine candidate advanced by an African company is exactly the kind of capability our continent needs - not just for this outbreak, but so that Africa can develop and manufacture the tools it needs to protect its own people. We welcome CEPI's support for Minapharm's work as a concrete step toward that health sovereignty." To develop the Bundibugyo-specific vaccine candidate, ProBioGen —a wholly-owned subsidiary of Minapharm based in Germany—will generate the recombinant MVA drug substance under Good Manufacturing Practice (GMP) guidelines. The drug substance is the active component of a vaccine which includes viral proteins that could train the immune system to recognise and fight a specific pathogen – in this case, Bundibugyo ebolavirus. The drug substance will then be shipped to Minapharm's facility in Cairo, Egypt, to create the drug product – the finished vaccine mixture which combines the drug substance with other vaccine ingredients and packaging ready for testing. In parallel, Minapharm plans to transfer drug substance manufacturing to its biotechnology facilities in Cairo, enabling fully local drug substance and drug product manufacturing. The project leverages more than 25 years of Minapharm's proven track record and experience in the development, end-to-end manufacturing and commercialisation of complex biologics in Africa. If the Phase 1 trial of the vaccine candidate is successful, CEPI anticipates working with Minapharm and ProBioGen to support late-stage trials to generate efficacy data for emergency use authorisation and licensure. CEPI, Minapharm and ProBioGen are committed to enabling rapid, affordable supply of the vaccine to affected countries and to the populations that need them should it prove safe and effective. A positive outcome from this project could also lay the foundation for the future expansion of Minapharm's vaccine manufacturing capabilities, potentially with support from CEPI and other partners. To date, CEPI's Bundibugyo virus vaccine development programme receives funds from the European Commission through its 2026 grant under Horizon Europe to CEPI, the U.S. Department of State and CEPI's existing core funding. Notes to Editors CEPI's funding was awarded to Minapharm for its Bundibugyo vaccine project following its application to CEPI's Call for Proposals seeking vaccine developers to advance the development of Bundibugyo vaccine candidates. MVA-C19 platform MVA-CR19 is ProBioGen's proprietary modified vaccinia Ankara (MVA) vaccine platform. MVA is a highly attenuated poxvirus vector that is replication-deficient in human cells: it can enter cells and express the vaccine antigens it carries, but cannot multiply or spread. ProBioGen's platform combines the MVA-CR19 strain with its AGE1 production cell line platform and TetherexXTM recombination technology to support rapid recombinant MVA generation and scalable manufacturing. The MVA platform holds potential as it has a well-established safety profile in people . Alongside studies demonstrating long-lasting immunity in people vaccinated with MVA-based mpox and Zaire ebolavirus vaccines, positive data has also been obtained in preclinical studies of MVA vaccines targeting Sudan ebolavirus , Marburg virus and other infectious diseases. The technology was also safe for use in immunosuppressed individuals and people living with HIV vaccinated with MVA-based vaccines targeting other diseases. CEPI's response to the Bundibugyo ebolavirus outbreak *CEPI has worked around the clock with its partners to respond to the Bundibugyo outbreak, announcing its first Bundibugyo vaccine development partnerships just two weeks after the declaration of the outbreak in mid-May. CEPI has to date already invested in Bundibugyo vaccines being developed using mRNA (Moderna), ChAdOx (the University of Oxford/ Serum Institute of India) and rVSV (IAVI with manufacturing by Hilleman Laboratories, and Public Health Vaccines) technologies. To accelerate outbreak response efforts, CEPI has also activated its global networks of laboratories, manufacturers and clinical trial partners, is supporting a clinical trial to evaluate potential treatments and engaging regulators to align on vaccine approval pathways. In parallel, CEPI is working with partners including affected countries, Gavi, Africa CDC, WHO, UNICEF, the World Bank and development finance institutions so the downstream conditions needed to enable equitable access—including procurement, allocation and deployment—are put in place. About CEPI CEPI is an innovative partnership between public, private, philanthropic and civil organisations. Its mission is to accelerate the development of vaccines and other biologic countermeasures against epidemic and pandemic threats so they can be accessible to all people in need. Central to CEPI's pandemic-beating plan is the '100 Days Mission' to develop safe, effective and accessible vaccines against new threats in just 100 days. CEPI is seeking $2.5 billion to execute CEPI 3.0, its 2027-2031 strategy which will systematically reduce the likelihood, impact and cost of epidemics and pandemics by driving the 100 Days Mission towards an operational reality. Learn more at CEPI.net . About Minapharm Minapharm is a leading pharmaceutical company in Egypt and the Middle East and the largest biopharmaceutical company in Africa, with over 25 years of experience in cellular and bioprocess engineering. Headquartered in Cairo, the Group develops, manufactures and commercializes a broad portfolio spanning complex small molecules, recombinant proteins, and monoclonal antibodies, supported by an extensive pipeline of genetically engineered medicines and cell and gene therapies. Minapharm operates seven manufacturing facilities and commercializes more than 100 life-saving and life-enhancing products. Its two biotechnology facilities in Cairo are the region's longest-established end-to-end drug substance and drug product operations, from cell line to finished vial. Together with its wholly owned Berlin-based subsidiary, ProBioGen AG, Minapharm has built an integrated biopharmaceutical model spanning research, proprietary technology, development and manufacturing. Through its subsidiary, ProBioGen, the Group is a global provider of proprietary technologies for vaccines, complex proteins, and cell and gene therapies, which have contributed to multiple FDA-approved products. Minapharm employs a combined workforce of more than 1,700 people and is listed on the Egyptian Exchange (EGX: MIPH). Learn more at minapharm.com About ProBioGen ProBioGen is a Berlin-based expert in the development and manufacturing of biopharmaceuticals, viral vectors, and vaccines, powered by proprietary technologies that enhance product quality and features. It has developed multiple innovative vaccine platforms, including Lenti.RiGHT®, AGE1 host cell lines and the IP-protected MVA-CR19 strain, which has a unique genomic signature. Operating for over 30 years, ProBioGen runs three manufacturing lines in Berlin, where 300 employees contribute to advancing next-generation therapies and global biotech innovation. For more information about ProBioGen , follow us on LinkedIn . 埃博拉市政厅:一线医疗机构需要了解有关本迪布焦病毒疾病防范的哪些信息 - 国家新兴特殊病原体培训和教育中心

Ebola Town Hall: What Frontline Healthcare Facilities Need to Know About Bundibugyo Virus Disease Preparedness - National Emerging Special Pathogens Training & Education Center
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 28 日,国家新兴特殊病原体培训和教育中心 (NETEC) 主办了埃博拉市政厅会议,以解决一线医疗机构的本迪布焦病毒病防备问题。该活动重点关注个人防护装备(PPE)、实验室安全、早期识别和隔离、沟通、患者稳定和转移协调等关键领域。该计划是 NETEC 为加强美国医疗保健系统针对特殊病原体做好准备而做出的更广泛努力的一部分。虽然没有报告具体的 暴发 数据或风险级别,但该事件强调了准备和培训在管理潜在威胁方面的重要性。此外,2026年8月24日,NETEC重点介绍了国家检疫部门最近的汉坦病毒应对措施,进一步强调了其在特殊病原体防范方面的作用。该事件被确认为一项准备举措,没有证据表明存在活跃的 暴发 或确诊病例。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件于2026年8月28日报告,相关活动于2026年8月24日发生。
地点与对象
国家新兴特殊病原体培训和教育中心 (NETEC) 是参与的主要实体,针对美国各地的医疗机构和专业人员。
事件
NETEC 主办了埃博拉市政厅会议,讨论了一线医疗机构的本迪布焦病毒病防范工作,重点关注个人防护装备、实验室安全、早期识别、隔离、沟通、患者稳定和转移协调。该事件被确认为一项准备举措。
规模、影响与风险
该活动强调了针对特殊病原体做好准备和培训的必要性,但没有报告具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。风险没有明确说明,但对本迪布焦病毒病的关注意味着对潜在 暴发 的担忧。
应对、状态与不确定性
该活动是 NETEC 的一项教育活动,旨在加强对包括本迪布焦病毒病在内的特殊病原体的防范。响应状态正在调查中,因为它涉及规划和培训,而不是确认的 暴发 管理。

Ebola Town Hall: What Frontline Healthcare Facilities Need to Know About Bundibugyo Virus Disease Preparedness - National Emerging Special Pathogens Training & Education Center

News Brief
On August 28, 2026, the National Emerging Special Pathogens Training & Education Center (NETEC) hosted an Ebola Town Hall to address Bundibugyo virus disease preparedness for frontline healthcare facilities. The event focused on critical areas such as personal protective equipment (PPE), laboratory safety, early identification and isolation, communication, patient stabilization, and transfer coordination. This initiative is part of NETEC's broader efforts to strengthen the U.S. healthcare system's readiness for special pathogens. While no specific outbreak data or risk levels were reported, the event underscores the importance of preparedness and training in managing potential threats. Additionally, on August 24, 2026, NETEC highlighted the National Quarantine Unit's recent hantavirus response, further emphasizing its role in special pathogen preparedness. The event is confirmed as a preparedness initiative, with no evidence of an active outbreak or confirmed cases.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

刚果民主共和国抗击本迪布焦病毒,最致命的埃博拉疫情导致 2,744 人死亡 - Streamlinefeed.co.ke

Deadliest Ebola Epidemic Kills 2,744 as DRC Fights Bundibugyo Virus - streamlinefeed.co.ke
新闻简报The article reports on the deadliest Ebola epidemic in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) history, caused by the Bundibugyo virus. As of 26 August 2026, the DRC has recorded 5,713 confirmed cases and 2,744 deaths, surpassing the previous record from 2018 to 2020. The World Health Organization confirmed the outbreak on 16 May 2026 and declared it a public health emergency of international concern on 18 August. Uganda, which shares an 877 km border with the DRC, was declared Ebola-free on 26 August after a 42-day monitoring period with 20 cases and two deaths. The case fatality ratio is about 46 percent, described as staggering due to the lack of a licensed vaccine for the Bundibugyo strain. The United Nations and Doctors Without Borders (MSF) have deployed response teams to manage the crisis, with MSF operating over 1,400 staff in treatment centres. The DRC's 42-day monitoring period ended on 26 August, and the next marker is the 42-day countdown in Ituri. The outbreak has also reached beyond Africa, with an imported case confirmed in France in July 2026. Kenya has strengthened its preparedness at its busiest port of entry, Jomo Kenyatta International Airport (JKIA), and at the Malaba border point, remaining case-free.
查看新闻五要素
时间
世界卫生组织于2026年5月16日确认本迪布焦病毒疫情,截至2026年8月26日,确诊病例达到5,713例,死亡人数为2,744人。刚果民主共和国的42天监测期于2026年8月26日结束,下一个标志是伊图里的42天倒计时。该文章发表于2026年8月27日。
地点与对象
暴发 以刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 为中心,特别是伊图里省,该省仍然是震中。乌干达与刚果民主共和国接壤 877 公里的边界,于 2026 年 8 月 26 日宣布已消除埃博拉病毒。肯尼亚还加强了其最繁忙的入境口岸乔莫·肯雅塔国际机场 (JKIA) 和马拉巴边境点的准备工作。
事件
刚果民主共和国正在经历由本迪布焦病毒引起的最致命的埃博拉疫情,截至 2026 年 8 月 26 日,已有 5,713 例确诊病例和 2,744 人死亡。世界卫生组织于 2026 年 5 月 16 日确认了 暴发,并于 2026 年 8 月 18 日宣布其为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件。与刚果民主共和国接壤的乌干达于 2019 年被宣布为无埃博拉病毒。经过 42 天的监测后,2026 年 8 月 26 日出现 20 例病例和 2 例死亡。
规模、影响与风险
The outbreak has resulted in 5,713 confirmed cases and 2,744 deaths in the DRC, making it the deadliest in the country's history. The case fatality ratio is about 46 percent, described as staggering due to the lack of a licensed vaccine for the Bundibugyo strain. The epidemic has also affected Uganda, with 20 cases and two deaths, and has reached beyond Africa, with an imported case confirmed in France in July 2026. The World Health Organization and the United Nations have deployed response teams to manage the crisis.
应对、状态与不确定性
刚果民主共和国和乌干达的卫生工作者正在筛查过境的货运司机,联合国在伊图里开设了一个新的应对协调中心。无国界医生组织 (MSF) 在 治疗 中心拥有 1,400 多名员工。国际移民组织报告称,所有货运司机都接受体温检查和症状筛查。

Streamline Feed OfficialVerified Aug 28, 20263 min read Follow on Google News Try askingGive me the 30-second brief Advertisement from Treadville Coffee Kenya . The first 10 seconds cannot be skipped. Ad playing Ad playing. Learn more Build your reading streak0/3 today·Counts at 25% Health workers now screen every cargo driver crossing from Ituri Province toward Uganda, five months into an epidemic that has killed 2,744 people in the Democratic Republic of Congo, the deadliest Ebola outbreak in the country's history. The Bundibugyo virus epidemic that the World Health Organization confirmed on 16 May 2026 has recorded 5,713 confirmed cases and 2,744 deaths in the DRC, according to the European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control's 26 August update. The WHO declared the outbreak a public health emergency of international concern on 18 August as infections passed 5,000. Uganda, which shares an 877 kilometre border with the DRC, was declared Ebola free on 26 August after passing the mandatory 42 day monitoring period with only 20 cases and two deaths. The Deadliest Outbreak in Congolese History Ituri Province remains the epicentre with 4,607 recorded cases, while North Kivu has registered 714. The crude case fatality ratio stands at about 46 percent, a figure the University of Minnesota's CIDRAP describes as staggering, because Bundibugyo arrived in communities with no recent experience of Ebola and no licensed vaccine exists for the strain. The toll surpasses the roughly 2,300 deaths of the 2018 to 2020 eastern DRC epidemic, which until this year stood as the worst in the country's history. Doctors Without Borders, known as MSF, now runs more than 1,400 staff across treatment centres, and the United Nations opened a new response coordination centre in Ituri on 3 August to anchor isolation, safe burial and contact tracing teams. The WHO is also supporting cross border preparedness in South Sudan and Rwanda, where diagnostic testing capacity has been expanded since June. An 877 Kilometre Border Under Screening Uganda's declaration of freedom removed one front line, but the border stays open for trade, fishing and family visits. The International Organization for Migration reports that every cargo driver passing through formal crossings undergoes temperature checks and symptom screening, while community alert desks watch the informal paths that satellite mapping shows crisscross the boundary. The United States moved early. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention issued an order on 18 May 2026 suspending the entry of certain travellers from the DRC, Uganda and South Sudan, and an imported case confirmed in France in July demonstrated the epidemic's reach beyond Africa. Kenya Raises Its Guard at JKIA and Malaba Kenya remains case free but is not complacent. Health Principal Secretary Mary Muthoni inspected port health services at Jomo Kenyatta International Airport this month. We have strengthened preparedness against Ebola at the country's busiest port of entry to enhance the safety of travellers, she said in a public statement. Screening has also been reinforced at the Malaba border point, and the Kenya Medical Research Institute has stepped up laboratory vigilance for travellers with fever. For East African readers the stakes are economic as well as medical. The DRC is a major source of regional gold and mineral trade, and a prolonged emergency raises compliance and insurance costs for carriers across the Great Lakes corridor, costs that ultimately land on import invoices in Kampala and Nairobi. DRC confirmed cases: 5,713 as of 26 August, per ECDC DRC deaths: 2,744, the highest Ebola toll in the country's history Uganda: 20 cases and two deaths, declared Ebola free on 26 August Case fatality ratio: about 46 percent Response staff: more than 1,400 MSF personnel in DRC and Uganda The next marker is the 42 day countdown in Ituri. If the DRC passes it without a new case, the world's deadliest Ebola epidemic will move from emergency to recovery, and the 877 kilometre border will finally breathe again. How did this story land? 刚果确认超过 6,000 例埃博拉病例,近 3,000 人死亡

Congo confirms more than 6,000 Ebola cases, almost 3,000 deaths
新闻简报据金沙萨信息部称,刚果民主共和国目前已确诊 6,000 多例 埃博拉病毒病 病例,近 3,000 人死亡。世界卫生组织于 2026 年 5 月中旬宣布了与 Bundibugyo 菌株相关的 暴发,最初的病例可能可以追溯到 2 月份。暴发已影响六个省份,专家怀疑存在大量未报告病例。目前,有 619 人处于隔离状态或正在接受 治疗 治疗。两种 抗病毒药物、治疗 和 Ervebo 疫苗 的临床试验自 7 月初以来一直在进行,尽管 疫苗 仅被批准用于扎伊尔菌株。非洲疾病控制和预防中心尚未确认 Bundibugyo 菌株中存在任何 突变,由于缺乏特定的 疫苗 或 治疗,控制该菌株特别具有挑战性。目前情况仍在积极调查和管理中。
查看新闻五要素
时间
世界卫生组织(WHO)于2026年5月中旬宣布了暴发,最新报告于2026年8月31日发布。据专家称,第一例感染可能早在2026年2月就发生了。
地点与对象
暴发 正在刚果民主共和国发生,特别是在六个省。受影响人群包括 6,000 多人感染 埃博拉病毒,据报告死亡人数为 2,911 人。
事件
刚果民主共和国新闻部报告称,已有 6,000 多人感染了 埃博拉病毒,目前的 暴发 已有 2,911 人死亡。这与该病毒的 Bundibugyo 毒株有关,由于缺乏特定的 疫苗 或 治疗,该毒株很难被遏制。
规模、影响与风险
已报告超过 6,000 例病例和 2,911 例死亡。专家认为,可能还有许多未报告的病例。目前,有 619 人处于隔离状态或正在接受 治疗 治疗。非洲疾病控制和预防中心 (Africa CDC) 尚未确认 Bundibugyo 菌株中存在任何 突变。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Congo confirms more than 6,000 Ebola cases, almost 3,000 deaths

News Brief
The Democratic Republic of the Congo has confirmed over 6,000 cases of Ebola virus disease and nearly 3,000 deaths in the current outbreak, according to the Information Ministry in Kinshasa. The outbreak, associated with the Bundibugyo strain, was announced by the World Health Organization in mid-May 2026, with initial cases possibly dating back to February. The outbreak has affected six provinces, and experts suspect a high number of unreported cases. Currently, 619 individuals are in isolation or receiving treatment. Clinical trials for two antiviral treatments and the Ervebo vaccine have been ongoing since early July, though the vaccine is approved only for the Zaire strain. The Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention have not confirmed any mutations in the Bundibugyo strain, which has been particularly challenging to contain due to the absence of specific vaccines or treatments. The situation remains under active investigation and management.
Original source text (English)

(is associated with: «Congo confirms more than 6,000 Ebola cases, almost 3,000 deaths») Eva Krafczyk/dpa More than 6,000 people have contracted the Ebola virus in the Democratic Republic of the Congo in the current outbreak, and 2,911 of them have died, the Information Ministry reported in Kinshasa on Monday. Experts believe there is a high number of unreported cases. At present, 619 people are reported to be in isolation or receiving treatment. The current outbreak of the Bundibugyo strain of the disease has been particularly difficult to contain, partly because there is so far neither a specific vaccine nor a specific treatment. Advertisement Advertisement Advertisement Advertisement The World Health Organization (WHO) announced the outbreak in mid-May. Given the already high number of cases at the time, experts believe that the first Ebola infections may have occurred undetected as early as February. The north-eastern province of Ituri was initially affected. Cases have now been reported in six provinces of Africa's second-largest country by area. Clinical trials of two antiviral treatments have been under way since early July. In addition, a clinical trial involving the Ervebo vaccine began last week. Ervebo is approved for the more common Zaire strain of the virus. According to the Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention (Africa CDC), suspicions that the Bundibugyo strain may have mutated have not been confirmed. Time
The outbreak was announced by the World Health Organization (WHO) in mid-May 2026, with the latest report published on August 31, 2026. The first infections may have occurred as early as February 2026, according to experts.
Location and population
The outbreak is occurring in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, specifically in six provinces. The affected population includes more than 6,000 people infected with the Ebola virus, with 2,911 deaths reported.
Event
The Democratic Republic of the Congo's Information Ministry reported that over 6,000 people have contracted the Ebola virus, and 2,911 have died in the current outbreak. This is associated with the Bundibugyo strain of the virus, which has been difficult to contain due to the lack of specific vaccines or treatments.
Scale, impact and risk
More than 6,000 cases and 2,911 deaths have been reported. Experts believe there are likely many unreported cases. Currently, 619 individuals are in isolation or receiving treatment. The Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention (Africa CDC) have not confirmed any mutations in the Bundibugyo strain.
Response, status and uncertainty
Clinical trials of two antiviral treatments have been ongoing since early July, and a clinical trial involving the Ervebo vaccine began last week. However, the vaccine is approved only for the Zaire strain. The outbreak is being managed with efforts such as training in safe practices and containment strategies, but the Bundibugyo strain remains difficult to control.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

The Scan by 2 Minute Medicine®: Viral menopause cocktail spreads without clinical evidence, maxxing trends raise body dysmorphia concerns, Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak surpasses 5,000 cases, and coffee intake is linked to lower visceral fat - 2 Minute Medicine

News Brief
The article, published on September 1, 2026, by 2 Minute Medicine, discusses several health-related topics. It highlights the spread of a viral menopause cocktail consisting of fexofenadine and famotidine, which lacks clinical evidence for treating menopause symptoms. The article also reports on the Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, which has surpassed 5,000 confirmed cases and 2,786 deaths as of August 26, 2026, with a 48.1% fatality rate. The likelihood of the outbreak spreading to the United States is considered very low. Additionally, the article explores maxxing trends, which range from sleep optimization to body image concerns, particularly among adolescents. These trends raise concerns about self-directed interventions with unproven efficacy and safety. The psychological context of these trends, including body dysmorphic disorder, is emphasized as a significant issue. The article advises clinicians to evaluate symptoms carefully and consider alternative diagnoses rather than relying on unverified social media protocols.
Original source text (English)
<em><strong>The Scan by 2 Minute Medicine® is a pop-culture medical newsletter and exclusive benefit for <a href="https://www.2minutemedicine.com/subscription-plans/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">2 Minute Medicine Plus subscribers.</a></strong></em> <div> <b style="font-size: 15px;">Viral menopause cocktail lacks evidence for treating vasomotor symptoms</b> A combination of fexofenadine (Allegra) and famotidine (Pepcid) has gained traction on social media as an over-the-counter approach to hot flashes, brain fog, sleep disruption, and other symptoms attributed to perimenopause. The viral <a href="https://www.aarp.org/health/drugs-supplements/viral-menopause-cocktail-risks/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">menopause cocktail</a> is appealing because both medications are inexpensive, familiar, and available without a prescription. Fexofenadine blocks histamine H1 receptors, while famotidine blocks histamine H2 receptors, creating a mechanistically plausible explanation that has helped the combination spread online. Plausibility, however, is not efficacy. No randomized clinical trial has established that the combination improves vasomotor symptoms, and neither medication is approved by the Food and Drug Administration (FDA) for menopause. Famotidine also reduces gastric acidity, and its prescribing information identifies clinically relevant <a href="https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/label/2019/019527s031lbl.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">drug interactions</a> with medications whose absorption depends on gastric pH. The larger concern is what patients may be substituting the cocktail for. New or atypical flushing, palpitations, sleep disturbance, or other presumed menopausal symptoms may warrant evaluation for thyroid disease, medication effects, or other medical causes rather than empiric treatment based on a social media protocol. When menopause is the diagnosis, hormone therapy remains the most effective treatment for vasomotor symptoms in appropriately selected patients, particularly symptomatic women younger than 60 years or within 10 years of menopause onset who do not have contraindications. Patients who cannot or do not want to use hormones also have evidence-based nonhormonal options, including selected selective serotonin reuptake inhibitors, serotonin-norepinephrine reuptake inhibitors, gabapentin, fezolinetant, and other approaches. When a patient mentions Allegra and Pepcid for menopause, the most useful response is not simply to dismiss what she found online. Ask which symptoms prompted treatment, determine whether further evaluation is warranted, review medication interactions, and redirect the conversation toward therapies with established efficacy and a risk-benefit profile that can actually be discussed. <b>Maxxing trends range from sleep optimization to adolescent body image concerns</b> The suffix "-maxxing" has expanded from niche appearance communities into sleep, nutrition, exercise, fertility, and nearly every other corner of online health optimization. Some versions package familiar health advice under new language. Sleepmaxxing may involve a consistent sleep schedule, a dark room, less evening screen exposure, and other basic sleep hygiene practices. Problems emerge when optimization becomes rigid measurement and patients begin treating wearable-generated scores as clinical endpoints. Sleep medicine has described <a href="https://link.springer.com/article/10.5664/jcsm.6472/fulltext.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">orthosomnia</a>, in which patients become preoccupied with improving or perfecting wearable sleep data and may inadvertently reinforce sleep-related anxiety or poor sleep behaviors. Looksmaxxing raises a different set of concerns, particularly among adolescents and young men. The trend ranges from ordinary skincare, grooming, nutrition, and resistance training to mewing, extreme dieting, injectable peptides, repeated facial measurements, and attempts to manipulate facial structure. It has now been characterized in <i>Pediatrics</i> as an emerging <a href="https://publications.aap.org/pediatrics/article-abstract/doi/10.1542/peds.2026-077705/208535/From-Mewing-to-Peptides-Looksmaxxing-as-an" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">pediatric concern</a> because appearance-focused communities can normalize self-directed interventions whose efficacy and safety have not been established. Some practices move considerably beyond harmless experimentation. Clinicians caring for adolescents have described extreme dieting, unapproved peptides, stimulant misuse, anabolic steroid use, and attempts to physically alter facial features among the <a href="https://www.hopkinsmedicine.org/news/articles/2026/08/looksmaxxing-trend-poses-risks-for-boys-and-teens" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">higher-risk behaviors</a> circulating within looksmaxxing communities. The psychological context deserves at least as much attention as the specific technique. Body dysmorphic disorder has an estimated <a href="https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/cns-spectrums/article/abs/prevalence-of-body-dysmorphic-disorder-in-the-united-states-adult-population/664486A24D8E14CD236C506295E46C48" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">2.4% prevalence</a> in the United States adult population and can involve substantial distress and functional impairment. For clinicians, the practical value of learning terms such as looksmaxxing and mewing is not keeping up with internet slang. It is recognizing what the terminology may be signaling. Repetitive mirror checking, persistent facial measurements, restrictive eating, requests for unnecessary cosmetic procedures, social withdrawal, or significant distress over perceived defects should prompt assessment for body dysmorphic disorder and related psychiatric concerns. A teenager asking whether mewing works may need a short anatomy explanation. A teenager organizing daily life around achieving a mathematically "ideal" face requires a different clinical conversation. <b>Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak in Congo surpasses 5,000 confirmed cases</b> The Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) expanded dramatically through the summer and is now the <a href="https://www.who.int/emergencies/disease-outbreak-news/item/2026-DON616" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">largest outbreak</a> of Ebola disease ever recorded in the country, irrespective of Ebola virus species. The epidemic is caused by Bundibugyo virus rather than the Ebola virus responsible for the major Zaire-species outbreaks, an important distinction because the available vaccine and species-specific therapeutics cannot automatically be assumed to provide clinically meaningful protection against Bundibugyo virus. As of August 26, the DRC had reported 5,794 confirmed cases and 2,786 deaths across 60 health zones in six provinces, corresponding to a <a href="https://www.who.int/news/item/28-08-2026-second-meeting-of-the-ihr-emergency-committee-on-the-epidemic-of-ebola-bundibugyo-virus-disease-in-the-democratic-republic-of-the-congo---meeting-report" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">48.1% fatality rate</a>. The outbreak is occurring in areas affected by limited health infrastructure, displacement, population movement, insecurity, and violence against health workers, all of which complicate case detection, contact tracing, isolation, and delivery of supportive care. Vaccination remains considerably more complicated than it was during previous Zaire ebolavirus outbreaks. Updated guidance published September 1 concludes that evidence remains insufficient to determine whether Ervebo provides clinically meaningful protection against Bundibugyo virus in humans and recommends use only within <a href="https://www.who.int/publications/i/item/B09884" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">research protocols</a>. For physicians in the United States, the immediate risk remains low. No outbreak-associated <a href="https://www.cdc.gov/ebola/situation-summary/index.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">U.S. cases</a> have been reported, and the likelihood of spread to the United States is considered very low. The practical issue is therefore recognition rather than expectation of widespread domestic disease. A patient with fever, gastrointestinal symptoms, unexplained bleeding, or another compatible syndrome should prompt a detailed travel and exposure history before routine diagnostic pathways proceed. Relevant exposure to an affected region should trigger prompt separation from other patients and early coordination with infection prevention and public health authorities. The geographic expansion of the outbreak makes that travel history increasingly important, even while the absolute risk to most patients seen in the United States remains very low. <b>Finnish cohort links coffee intake to leaner body composition</b> Coffee has accumulated a large observational literature around cardiometabolic health, but a recent Finnish analysis asked a more specific question: do habitual coffee drinkers differ in body composition and metabolic markers even when body mass index (BMI) appears similar? The cross-sectional study included <a href="https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00394-026-04038-z" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">2,264 participants</a> from the Northern Finland Birth Cohort 1966 at age 46 and combined self-reported coffee consumption with body composition measurements, circulating metabolites, cardiometabolic measurements, and sex hormone profiles. The distinction between BMI and more direct body composition measures was one of the more interesting findings. Higher coffee intake groups had lower body fat percentage, lower absolute fat mass, <a href="https://doi.org/10.1007/s00394-026-04038-z" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">lower visceral fat</a>, and greater skeletal muscle mass despite similar BMI across consumption groups. Higher coffee consumption was also inversely correlated with circulating branched-chain amino acids in both sexes, while men demonstrated a more favorable glucose-insulin profile and a distinct androgen-related hormonal pattern. These findings do not establish that coffee caused any of the differences. The analysis was cross-sectional, coffee intake was self-reported, and higher-consumption groups differed in characteristics such as education and smoking. The findings are nevertheless directionally consistent with a broader epidemiologic literature. A systematic review and dose-response meta-analysis of 28 prospective studies involving more than 1.1 million participants found an inverse association between coffee consumption and <a href="https://diabetesjournals.org/care/article-abstract/37/2/569/29536" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">diabetes risk</a>, with associations observed for both caffeinated and decaffeinated coffee. None of this makes coffee a body composition treatment or a reason to encourage escalating caffeine intake. For most healthy adults, approximately <a href="https://www.fda.gov/consumers/consumer-updates/spilling-beans-how-much-caffeine-too-much" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">400 milligrams</a> of caffeine per day is an amount not generally associated with negative effects, although individual sensitivity varies and pregnancy, medications, and certain medical conditions change the discussion. The practical takeaway is modest: habitual coffee consumption does not need to be discouraged solely because of generalized metabolic concerns, but the Finnish findings remain observational and should not be translated into a prescription for increasing muscle mass or reducing visceral adiposity. </div> <p style="font-weight: 400;">©2026 <a href="http://www.2minutemedicine.com" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">2 Minute Medicine, Inc</a>. No article should be construed as medical advice and is not intended as such by the authors or by 2 Minute Medicine, Inc.</p>

刚果民主共和国:随着孩子们重返学校,埃博拉病毒的恐惧与日俱增 - 半岛电视台

DRC: Ebola fears grow as children return to school - Al Jazeera
新闻简报在刚果民主共和国(DRC),随着儿童重返学校,对埃博拉病毒传播的担忧加剧,尤其是在高风险省份伊图里。截至8月31日,由本迪布焦病毒引起的暴发已导致6,041例确诊病例和2,911人死亡,超过了之前2018年至2020年最严重的暴发。政府选择了分阶段的方法,高风险地区使用印刷材料和广播节目进行家庭学习。家长和老师对学校重新开放的安全表示担忧,理由是缺乏针对本迪布焦病毒的经批准的 疫苗,以及扎伊尔埃博拉病毒 疫苗(Ervebo)对抗这种病毒株的有效性有限。世界卫生组织正在支持一项临床试验来评估 Ervebo 的潜在用途。与此同时,教育官员正在培训工作人员,以推广预防措施并提高对埃博拉病毒的认识。情况仍然不确定,我们正在努力平衡教育连续性和公共卫生安全。
查看新闻五要素
时间
截至周二,刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 的儿童正在重返学校,政府数据显示,截至 8 月 31 日,已有 6,041 例确诊病例,2,911 例死亡,超过了 2018 年至 2020 年 暴发 的最差记录。据教育部称,截至 8 月 26 日,在风险最高的地区,学生将在家中使用印刷材料和广播节目学习。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国(DRC),特别是东北部的伊图里省,是 暴发 的震中。受影响的人群包括学校的儿童和教师,家长和教育工作者对重新开放的安全表示担忧。
事件
刚果民主共和国政府决定在部分地区重新开放学校,同时对高风险地区实施居家学习等措施。家长和老师担心埃博拉病毒在学校传播的可能性,政府根据风险级别将区域分为绿色、橙色和红色。暴发 是由本迪布焦病毒引起的,该病毒是一种不同于扎伊尔埃博拉病毒的埃博拉病毒。
规模、影响与风险
截至8月31日,刚果民主共和国累计确诊病例6041例,死亡2911例。截至8月26日,伊图里已有4,802例确诊病例。政府已将40个受影响的教育分区中的18个列为高风险分区。目前还没有批准专门用于本迪布焦病毒病的 疫苗 或 治疗,扎伊尔埃博拉病毒 疫苗(Ervebo)尚未被证明对这种病毒株有效。
应对、状态与不确定性
政府正在实施印刷作业表和广播节目系统,以便在高风险地区进行家庭学习。教育官员正在伊图里培训工作人员,以识别危险行为并推广预防措施。世界卫生组织正在支持一项临床试验,以评估 Ervebo 对 Bundibugyo 病毒的有效性。由于人们对学校重新开放的安全性和采取额外健康措施的必要性感到担忧,情况仍然不确定。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

DRC: Ebola fears grow as children return to school - Al Jazeera

News Brief
In the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), concerns about the spread of Ebola have intensified as children return to school, particularly in the high-risk province of Ituri. The outbreak, caused by the Bundibugyo virus, has resulted in 6,041 confirmed cases and 2,911 deaths as of August 31, surpassing the previous worst outbreak from 2018 to 2020. The government has opted for a phased approach, with high-risk areas using printed materials and radio programmes for home learning. Parents and teachers express fears about the safety of school reopening, citing the lack of an approved vaccine for Bundibugyo virus and the limited effectiveness of the Zaire ebolavirus vaccine, Ervebo, against this strain. The World Health Organization is supporting a clinical trial to evaluate Ervebo's potential use. Meanwhile, education officials are training staff to promote preventive measures and raise awareness about Ebola. The situation remains uncertain, with ongoing efforts to balance educational continuity and public health safety.
Original source text (English)
DRC: Ebola fears grow as children return to school Parents fear school could fuel Ebola spread as authorities shift high-risk pupils to home learning. Goma, DR Congo – Children across the Democratic Republic of the Congo are returning to school on Tuesday as the country battles its largest Ebola outbreak on record, prompting concern among parents and teachers in the worst-hit province of Ituri. Government figures showed 6,041 confirmed cases and 2,911 deaths as of August 31, surpassing the previous worst outbreak, which ran from 2018 to 2020. Recommended Stories list of 4 items - list 1 of 4Over 6,000 Ebola cases confirmed in DRC as death toll nears 3,000 - list 2 of 4DR Congo begins vaccinating against largest and deadliest Ebola outbreak - list 3 of 4Ebola spreads to two new zones in DRC; 60 areas now affected - list 4 of 4World Health Organization says Ebola outbreak in Uganda is over Six of the country’s 26 provinces are affected. Ituri, in the northeast, remains the epicentre, with 4,802 confirmed cases recorded as of August 26. Where are parents’ fears coming from? In Bunia, Ituri’s provincial capital, parents are questioning whether schools should reopen while the outbreak continues to spread. “As a parent, I am concerned about the announcement that schools are reopening, especially as we are facing the Ebola outbreak in our region. We are asking the government what measures they will put in place to protect children from this disease,” said Dieudonne Lotsima, a father of three and resident of Bunia. Lotsima believes classes should be postponed until a vaccine specifically effective against the virus causing the outbreak becomes available. “Children are children,” he told Al Jazeera. “They may not wash their hands at the washbasins, and if they become infected, they risk infecting the whole family. This return to school is uncertain, and if we do not take steps to protect the children, I do not know what we can do.” The outbreak is caused by the Bundibugyo virus, a species of ebolavirus different from the Zaire ebolavirus, which has caused most previous Ebola outbreaks in the Democratic Republic of the Congo. There is currently no approved vaccine or treatment specifically for Bundibugyo virus disease. Ervebo, the vaccine approved against Zaire ebolavirus, is being given to front-line health workers, but its effectiveness against Bundibugyo has not been established. The World Health Organization is supporting a clinical trial to assess whether it can protect against the strain responsible for the current outbreak. How is the government reopening schools? Rather than impose a nationwide closure, the government is using different arrangements according to the level of Ebola risk. In the highest-risk areas, pupils will study from home using printed materials and radio programmes, according to the Ministry of Education. Workbooks and learning sheets covering essential parts of the curriculum will be distributed, while local and community radio stations will broadcast programmes to accompany them. Families without radios may also be provided with them. Raissa Malu, the minister of education, said on August 18 that 40 of the country’s 138 educational subdivisions in Ebola-affected provinces were affected by the outbreak. Eighteen were classified as high risk. The government has divided areas into three categories: green, where there is no reported Ebola transmission; orange, where areas are affected but can continue with additional precautions; and red, where the risk is considered highest. “We are, in fact, going to introduce a system of worksheets that will be produced in line with the curriculum, focusing on essential knowledge, and distributed to children to enable them to continue their work at home,” Malu told Al Jazeera. She stressed that the arrangement was not online or internet-based teaching. But Catherine Neema, a mother of two in Bunia, remains unconvinced. “I’m not sure I’ll send my child to school. When the children start school, there will be too many people. People may come into close contact with one another and become infected,” she said. Neema said her eldest child began the holidays when confirmed Ebola cases stood at about 1,500, with 470 deaths. Why are teachers still worried? The Ituri 1 Teachers’ Inter-Union Committee says the epidemiological situation is not conducive to a safe return to school. Budju Lobo Isaac, a trade union member, said schools needed basic health measures before children and teachers returned. “We cannot sacrifice the lives of children and teachers just to stick to a timetable. Before reopening schools, we must ensure minimum health and safety standards are in place: handwashing facilities, hand sanitiser, thermal scanners, and monitoring and support for teachers,” he told Al Jazeera. Education officials have also been training staff in Ituri to identify risky behaviour, promote preventive measures, and educate pupils about Ebola. “We have trained inspectors, senior officials from the provincial directorate, and other members of teachers’ unions,” said Jean-Baptiste Ngona Kivi, acting head of the General Education Service in the Ituri 1 educational province. “They will be deployed to the sub-districts to pass on this training on Ebola protection techniques to headteachers and teachers.” Can schools also help fight Ebola? Professor Yap Boum, incident manager at the Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention for the response to the Bundibugyo outbreak, sees the return to school as an opportunity to reach families with information about Ebola. “Once they have been made aware of the Ebola virus disease, when they return home, they also help to raise awareness amongst adults,” he told Al Jazeera. “So this is another opportunity we see in children returning to school.”

Children are returning to school in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) as of Tuesday, with government figures showing 6,041 confirmed cases and 2,911 deaths as of August 31, surpassing the previous worst outbreak from 2018 to 2020. In the highest-risk areas, pupils will study from home using printed materials and radio programmes, according to the Ministry of Education, as of August 26.

The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), specifically the province of Ituri in the northeast, which is the epicentre of the outbreak. The affected population includes children and teachers in schools, with parents and educators expressing concerns about the safety of reopening.

The DRC government has decided to reopen schools in some areas while implementing measures such as home learning for high-risk zones. Parents and teachers are concerned about the potential for Ebola spread in schools, and the government has categorized areas into green, orange, and red based on risk levels. The outbreak is caused by the Bundibugyo virus, a species of ebolavirus different from the Zaire ebolavirus.
Scale, impact and risk
As of August 31, there were 6,041 confirmed cases and 2,911 deaths in the DRC. In Ituri, 4,802 confirmed cases were recorded as of August 26. The government has classified 18 of 40 affected educational subdivisions as high risk. There is currently no approved vaccine or treatment specifically for Bundibugyo virus disease, and the Zaire ebolavirus vaccine, Ervebo, has not been proven effective against this strain.

The government is implementing a system of printed worksheets and radio programmes to allow home learning in high-risk areas. Education officials are training staff in Ituri to identify risky behaviour and promote preventive measures. The World Health Organization is supporting a clinical trial to assess the effectiveness of Ervebo against the Bundibugyo virus. The situation remains uncertain, with concerns about the safety of school reopening and the need for additional health measures.

儿童必须以埃博拉 治疗 和预防研究为中心 - 医生无国界

Children must be centered in Ebola treatment and prevention research - Doctors Without Borders
新闻简报无国界医生组织 (MSF) 呼吁在医疗工具和 治疗 的研发中更加关注儿童,以应对刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 正在流行的本迪布焦病毒 暴发。文章强调,与成人相比,儿童死于该疾病的风险要高得多,2026 年 8 月 4 日的数据显示,5 岁以下儿童的 死亡率 发病率显着较高。无国界医生组织提倡将儿童纳入暴露后预防 (PEP) 试验,特别是口服 抗病毒药物 奥贝昔韦,该药物正在 EBO-PEP 研究中进行测试。然而,由于缺乏儿科配方以及公布的片剂粉碎或溶解数据,44 磅以下的儿童被排除在试验之外。无国界医生儿科顾问尼尔·拉塞尔强调了解决这一差距的紧迫性,因为儿童在 暴发 期间面临高风险。尽管吉利德已表示愿意提供儿科制剂,但具体时间表仍不清楚。该文章强调利益相关者需要支持儿科研究并加快其他口服 抗病毒药物 和 PEP 单克隆抗体的评估。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026年9月1日。事件日期为2026年8月4日,即儿童死亡率的数据被记录的时间。
地点与对象
暴发 正在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 发生,特别影响正在流行的本迪布焦病毒 暴发 的儿童。
事件
无国界医生组织 (MSF) 强调需要以儿童为中心进行埃博拉病毒 治疗 和预防研究,特别是针对刚果民主共和国当前的本迪布焦病毒 暴发。文章报告说,与成人相比,儿童死于这种疾病的风险要高得多。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 对 5 岁以下儿童产生了显着影响,确诊儿童的死亡比例高于成人。该文章强调了奥贝昔韦等医疗对策儿科制剂的缺乏,以及为幼儿提供替代 治疗 方案的挑战。
应对、状态与不确定性
无国界医生组织正在倡导将儿童纳入暴露后预防(PEP)试验,并与吉利德科学公司合作开发儿科制剂。然而,这些制剂的可用性以及相关临床试验的启动仍不确定。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Children must be centered in Ebola treatment and prevention research - Doctors Without Borders

News Brief
Doctors Without Borders (MSF) has called for greater focus on children in the research and development of medical tools and treatments for the ongoing Bundibugyo virus outbreak in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC). The article highlights that children are at a disproportionately higher risk of dying from the disease compared to adults, with data from August 4, 2026, showing a significantly higher mortality rate among children under 5. MSF is advocating for the inclusion of children in trials for post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP), particularly for the oral antiviral obeldesivir, which is being tested in the EBO-PEP study. However, the lack of pediatric formulations and published data on tablet crushing or dissolution has excluded children under 44 pounds from the trial. MSF pediatric advisor Neal Russell emphasized the urgency of addressing this gap, as children are at high risk during the outbreak. While Gilead has expressed willingness to provide pediatric formulations, the timeline for this remains unclear. The article underscores the need for stakeholders to support pediatric studies and accelerate the evaluation of other oral antivirals and monoclonal antibodies for PEP.

See latest updates Children must be centered in Ebola treatment and prevention research In the ongoing Ebola outbreak in DR Congo, children with the disease are at a disproportionately higher risk of dying. September 1, 2026 Share Share in FacebookShare in BlueSkyShare in LinkedInShare in TwitterShare in Mail Hospital staff sing and applaud as 3-year-old Furaha is discharged from MSF's Ebola treatment center after recovering from the disease. | DR Congo 2026 © Alexis Huguet/MSF Related Democratic Republic of Congo Access to medicines Ebola Children’s health Outbreaks and epidemics View more Related Democratic Republic of Congo Access to medicines Ebola Children’s health Outbreaks and epidemics View more Children must be included in all research and development of medical tools and treatments for the current Ebola disease outbreak, said Doctors Without Borders/Médecins Sans Frontières (MSF) in a commentary published in The Lancet today. Specifically, children must have timely access to pediatric formulations for ongoing trials of oral post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP), including the oral antiviral pill obeldesivir that is manufactured by US-based Gilead Sciences. The current Ebola disease outbreak in Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC), which is caused by the Bundibugyo virus, has disproportionately affected children. The proportion of children under 5 years old who were diagnosed and subsequently died from the virus is significantly higher than that of adults, as of August 4, among patients with confirmed cases whose age is recorded. Delayed inclusion in trials, lack of pediatric dosing guidance, and safety concerns often prevent children from accessing medical countermeasures like oral antivirals and monoclonal antibodies. We need to generate the evidence now so that children can access effective preventive treatments alongside everyone else, not after the outbreak is over. Dr. Neal Russell, MSF pediatric advisor Currently, the EBO-PEP study, which began on July 14, is evaluating the oral antiviral obeldesivir for preventing infection after high-risk exposure to Ebola disease caused by the Bundibugyo virus. If this medicine proves to be effective, it could offer a practical and scalable method to protect people who have had recent contact with someone with Ebola. While children weighing less than 66 pounds (approximately 12 years old or younger) have been excluded from the trial in its original design, we expect a planned protocol amendment to include children over 44 pounds will soon be accepted. However, due to the lack of an available pediatric formulation of obeldesivir and the absence of published data supporting tablet crushing or dissolution, children under 44 pounds who are age 6 or younger are still excluded from this study. While the trial is conducting a sub-protocol for those excluded — an alternative treatment regimen would be operationally very difficult in outbreak settings, especially for young children. Gilead has recently expressed willingness to make pediatric formulations of obeldesivir available soon, but it is unclear when a related clinical trial with oral formulations would start. Dr. Neal Russell, MSF pediatric advisor, said today: “Too often, the inclusion of children in the development of medical countermeasures to address public health emergencies is left to the ‘small print.’ But today — with children at a disproportionately high risk of dying if infected with Ebola in the current outbreak in DRC — their inclusion is paramount. "Researchers, donors, and other stakeholders must urgently support a pediatric obeldesivir sub-study, build on existing dosing guidance, and accelerate the evaluation of other oral antivirals and monoclonal antibodies as post-exposure prophylaxis. We need to generate the evidence now so that children can access effective preventive treatments alongside everyone else — not after the outbreak is over. "Gilead — the pharmaceutical corporation manufacturing obeldesivir — has committed to reproducing the antiviral in its pediatric formulation. We hope that Gilead will provide a clear and accelerated timeline for doing so, and that stakeholders can organize pediatric studies with this formulation — or with modified adult tablets as soon as possible — so that all children at risk can be included in studies evaluating which post-exposure prophylaxis options are most practical, timely, and effective.” An urgent opportunity to ensure children's access to oral post-exposure prophylaxis trials for Bundibugyo virus disease Read the commentary We speak out.
