B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

埃博拉病毒不断卷土重来,因为准备工作仍然太晚 - Devdiscourse

Ebola Keeps Returning Because Preparedness Still Starts Too Late - Devdiscourse
新闻简报该文于2026-08-31发表,探讨了埃博拉病毒病(暴发)的反复发生特性,尽管科学已取得进展。一篇发表于《感染性疾病报告》的综述指出,尽管抗病毒药物(疫苗)和疫苗(基因组监测)已改善应对能力,但由于脆弱的卫生系统和行动延迟,应对准备仍显不足。2013–2016年西非埃博拉病毒病疫情报告病例超过28,000例,暴露出现有系统的局限性。2021年几内亚疫情再次出现表明,该病毒可在康复者体内持续存在,导致新一轮的埃博拉病毒病(传播)再次发生。综述强调需采用‘一个健康’方法,整合环境与健康数据,并指出埃博拉病毒的自然储存宿主(储存宿主)仍未确认。文中还指出,现有抗病毒药物(疫苗)仅对扎伊尔型埃博拉病毒有效,在应对苏丹型和本迪布焦型等其他病毒时仍存在准备缺口。作者呼吁建立更广泛的疫苗接种平台、区域化生产以及长期投资于卫生基础设施,以有效应对这些挑战。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文发表于2026-08-31。文中引用了2013–2016年西非埃博拉病毒病疫情及2021年几内亚疫情再次出现的情况。
地点与对象
文章讨论了全球和区域背景,包括 2013-2016 年西非疫情和 2021 年几内亚疫情死灰复燃,涉及多个国家和人群。
事件
该文章报道了一篇评论,该评论强调了埃博拉 暴发 由于准备不足和卫生系统薄弱而反复出现的性质,尽管 疫苗 和 基因组监测 等科学进步。该评论由一组研究人员撰写,并发表在《传染病报告》上。
规模、影响与风险
2013-2016年西非疫情报告病例数超过28,000例。2021 年几内亚的疫情死灰复燃表明,该病毒可能在幸存者体内持续存在,并导致新的 传播。该审查强调需要改进 监测、实验室能力和卫生基础设施来应对这些挑战。
应对、状态与不确定性
该评论讨论了 基因组监测 和 疫苗接种 作为响应关键工具的重要性,但指出对这些工具的访问是不平等的。它还强调了“同一个健康”方法和区域能力建设的必要性。作者警告说,埃博拉病毒的自然 储存宿主 仍未得到证实,并且仅部分了解将环境变化与 跨物种溢出 联系起来的机制。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Ebola Keeps Returning Because Preparedness Still Starts Too Late - Devdiscourse

News Brief
The article, published on 2026-08-31, discusses the recurring nature of Ebola outbreaks despite scientific advancements. A review in Infectious Disease Reports highlights that while vaccines and genomic surveillance have improved responses, preparedness remains inadequate due to weak health systems and delayed action. The 2013–2016 West African epidemic, which reported over 28,000 cases, demonstrated the limitations of current systems. The 2021 Guinea resurgence showed that the virus can persist in survivors, leading to renewed transmission. The review emphasizes the need for a One Health approach, integrating environmental and health data, and stresses that the natural reservoir of Ebola is still unconfirmed. It also notes that existing vaccines are only effective against Zaire ebolavirus, leaving gaps in preparedness for other species like Sudan and Bundibugyo. The authors argue for broader vaccine platforms, regional manufacturing, and long-term investment in health infrastructure to address these challenges effectively.
Original source text (English)
IN FOCUS ✕ Home News Analysis Agro-Forestry Art & Culture Technology Economy & Business Education Energy & Extractives Politics Law & Governance Health Science & Environment Social & Gender Sports Transport Urban Development WASH Research LogIn/SignUp Close the sidebar Ebola Keeps Returning Because Preparedness Still Starts Too Late Health COE-EDP, VisionRI Updated: 31-08-2026 10:05 IST | Created: 31-08-2026 09:52 IST Credit: ChatGPT The world knows far more about Ebola than it did a decade ago. It has better vaccines, faster sequencing, stronger diagnostics and new digital tools. Yet outbreaks can still expose the same old failures: delayed detection, fragile health systems, weak trust and unequal access to lifesaving countermeasures. A new review in Infectious Disease Reports argues that this is the real Ebola preparedness problem. Titled “Ebola Virus Disease in the Era of One Health and Global Preparedness: Evolving Epidemiology, Genomic Surveillance, and Future Challenges,” the review by Francesco De Maria, Francesco Branda, Ivailo Alexiev, Dong Keon Yon, Ayşe Banu Demir, Giancarlo Ceccarelli, Fabio Scarpa, Massimo Ciccozzi and Alessandro Russo, examines how Ebola epidemiology and response have changed from 1976 to the 2026 Bundibugyo virus emergency. The key message is that scientific breakthroughs matter, but preparedness fails when they sit on top of weak systems. The decisive advantage may not come from inventing the next technology during an outbreak, but from building surveillance, laboratories, trusted institutions and regional capacity long before one begins. Ebola Is No Longer Just a Virus Problem; It Is a Systems Problem The review shows that Ebola outbreaks are shaped by far more than the virus itself. Rural zoonotic spillover remains the predominant route of emergence, but what happens next depends heavily on context: how quickly cases are detected, how mobile populations are, how resilient health facilities are, whether conflict disrupts response operations, and whether communities trust public institutions. The 2013–2016 West African epidemic demonstrated this with devastating clarity. More than 28,000 cases were reported, and transmission spread through major cities and across borders, exposing weaknesses in surveillance, laboratory capacity and health infrastructure. The review treats this epidemic as a turning point because it shattered the assumption that Ebola would remain geographically limited to remote rural settings. Ecological pressures complicate the picture further. Deforestation, mining, agricultural expansion, biodiversity loss and ecosystem fragmentation can increase contact between people and wildlife, creating conditions more conducive to zoonotic spillover. At the same time, the authors caution that Ebola’s natural reservoir remains formally unconfirmed and that the precise mechanisms connecting environmental change to spillover are still only partially understood. The uncertainty strengthens the case for a One Health approach rather than weakening it. The review argues that human health surveillance must increasingly be connected with wildlife monitoring, land-use change, ecological data and environmental signals. In other words, preparedness should begin well before a patient arrives at a clinic with symptoms. For policymakers, this is a major shift in emphasis. Ebola control can no longer be treated only as a health-ministry function activated during emergencies. It is also tied to environmental governance, border management, conflict response, rural infrastructure and institutional capacity, all of which shape how quickly outbreaks are recognized and whether they can be contained. Genomics and Vaccines Have Changed the Response, but Access Is Still Unequal Among the clearest advances of the past decade is genomic surveillance. Real-time sequencing can now help identify transmission chains, distinguish between new zoonotic spillovers and continued human transmission, and detect links that traditional epidemiological investigation might miss. The 2021 Guinea resurgence was especially significant because genomic evidence showed that the virus was closely related to strains from the 2013–2016 epidemic rather than representing a new animal-to-human introduction. That finding strengthened evidence that Ebola can persist in survivors and later contribute to renewed transmission, expanding the long-term responsibilities of public health systems beyond the end of an outbreak. Vaccination has been another major breakthrough. The rVSV-ZEBOV vaccine showed extremely high efficacy against disease caused by Zaire ebolavirus, and more than 300,000 people were vaccinated during the 2018–2020 eastern DRC outbreak. Later real-world assessments continued to show strong protective effectiveness, proving that emergency vaccination can work even in highly difficult settings. However, the review makes clear that this success is unevenly distributed across Ebola virus species. The existing vaccine is licensed for Zaire ebolavirus and lacks demonstrated clinical efficacy against Sudan and Bundibugyo viruses. That leaves major preparedness gaps in regions where outbreaks caused by other ebolaviruses can occur. This is not just a technical issue; it is a development and equity problem. Countries at highest risk often have the weakest manufacturing capacity, most fragile health systems and greatest dependence on externally financed emergency responses. The result is a recurring pattern in which scientific capability exists globally but is not always available where and when it is most needed. The review therefore supports broader vaccine platforms, regional manufacturing and multi-year investment in laboratory and health-system capacity. That would shift preparedness away from a model of importing solutions during crises toward one of strengthening permanent regional capability. AI, Drones and Digital Health Can Help, but Technology Cannot Replace Weak Health Systems The review is notably cautious about the role of artificial intelligence. AI and machine learning may eventually help integrate mobility patterns, environmental variables and surveillance data to improve forecasting, but the authors stress that most Ebola-specific AI tools have not yet been prospectively validated during live outbreaks. Enthusiasm for digital innovation can easily outrun the evidence. A model that performs well retrospectively is not necessarily a system that improves real-world decisions when laboratories are overloaded, communities are difficult to reach or data are incomplete. Other technologies have stronger operational evidence. Portable genomic sequencing has been used in field settings, drones have transported Ebola test samples from hard-to-reach areas to laboratories, and mobile platforms have supported risk communication and public engagement. However, the review repeatedly returns to the same conclusion: technology works best as a force multiplier, not a substitute for basic health infrastructure. A drone network is of limited value without functioning laboratories. A predictive model cannot compensate for missing surveillance data. A mobile health platform cannot overcome deep mistrust if institutions lack credibility. This has particular relevance for low-resource settings, where unreliable electricity, expensive connectivity, digital literacy gaps and fragmented health information systems can undermine implementation. The authors also warn that digital systems may reinforce inequality if they rely disproportionately on data from urban, wealthier or better-connected populations. For governments and development agencies, the implication is practical: digital health investment should be tied to infrastructure, interoperability, local skills and community co-design. The goal should not be to showcase advanced technology, but to strengthen the functioning of the public health system as a whole. The Real Preparedness Gap Is Political, Financial and Institutional Ebola preparedness remains too reactive. Emergency financing tends to surge when outbreaks become visible, while routine investments in laboratories, cold chains, health workers, survivor monitoring and community trust are harder to sustain between crises. The authors argue for predictable, multi-year financing rather than episodic emergency funding. They also call for stronger regional manufacturing and diversified vaccine platforms to address the inequities that have left some ebolavirus species without licensed vaccines or therapeutics. For African governments, this means preparedness should be treated as core public infrastructure. For international organizations and donors, it means moving beyond short-term project cycles that create pilot programmes without building durable national capacity. For researchers and investors, it means prioritizing technologies and products that can operate under real-world constraints rather than only in high-resource environments. The review is broad and valuable, but it also has limitations. It is a narrative review rather than a systematic one, meaning there was no predefined review protocol, formal risk-of-bias assessment or quantitative meta-analysis. Its synthesis is therefore best understood as an evidence-informed overview rather than a definitive measurement of every intervention’s effectiveness. Several uncertainties also remain unresolved. The natural reservoir of ebolaviruses has not been definitively established, the ecological triggers of spillover are incompletely understood, the durability and cross-protection of vaccines require further study, and the real-world effectiveness of AI-driven early-warning systems in low-resource settings still needs rigorous evaluation. Overall, Ebola preparedness has become a test of whether global health has learned to invest before a crisis rather than after one. Science has delivered powerful tools, but tools alone do not create resilience. What ultimately determines whether the next outbreak remains local or becomes a wider emergency may be less about what the world can invent during a crisis and more about what countries have already built before the first alarm is raised. FIRST PUBLISHED ON: Devdiscourse READ MORE ON One Health Ebola virus disease outbreak preparedness AI in epidemic surveillance genomic surveillance health system resilience Bundibugyo virus Ebola preparedness global health security Ebola vaccine READ MORE PERSPECTIVES AND INSIGHTS LATEST NEWS VIDEOS View All
View five news elements
Time
The article was published on 2026-08-31. It references the 2013–2016 West African epidemic and the 2021 Guinea resurgence.
Location and population
The article discusses global and regional contexts, including the 2013–2016 West African epidemic and the 2021 Guinea resurgence, which involved multiple countries and populations.
Event
The article reports on a review that highlights the recurring nature of Ebola outbreaks due to delayed preparedness and weak health systems, despite scientific advancements like vaccines and genomic surveillance. The review is authored by a group of researchers and published in Infectious Disease Reports.
Scale, impact and risk
The 2013–2016 West African epidemic reported over 28,000 cases. The 2021 Guinea resurgence showed that the virus could persist in survivors and lead to renewed transmission. The review emphasizes the need for improved surveillance, laboratory capacity, and health infrastructure to address these challenges.
Response, status and uncertainty
The review discusses the importance of genomic surveillance and vaccination as key tools in response, but notes that access to these is unequal. It also highlights the need for a One Health approach and regional capacity building. The authors caution that the natural reservoir of Ebola remains unconfirmed, and the mechanisms linking environmental change to spillover are only partially understood.

Response must outpace spread of Ebola: UN relief chief - UN News
新闻简报The UN relief chief, Tom Fletcher, warned on 1 September 2026 that the response to the Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) must outpace the virus's spread. The DRC is facing the fastest-growing Ebola outbreak on record, with 5,515 cases and 2,642 deaths reported in the first 100 days since the outbreak was declared on 15 May. By Monday, local authorities had confirmed 6,100 cases and 2,950 deaths, with over 5,000 cases in Ituri Province alone. The outbreak is occurring in the context of an already severe humanitarian crisis in eastern DRC, where conflict between government forces and M23 rebels has displaced communities and hindered response efforts. The UN has allocated $57 million from the Central Emergency Response Fund to support containment and preparedness, but a $1.1 billion funding gap remains. Fletcher emphasized the need for urgent scaling up of efforts to prevent further spread and loss of life, highlighting the importance of political will, access, and resources in addressing the epidemic.

Facebook Twitter Print Email Response must outpace spread of Ebola: UN relief chief 1 September 2026 Health The world must move faster to contain an Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) that is growing exponentially, the UN’s Emergency Relief Coordinator warned on Tuesday. Although the UN and its partners have scaled up their response, a $1.1 billion funding gap remains as the DRC grapples with the fastest-growing Ebola outbreak on record. During the first 100 days of the 2014-2016 West Africa Ebola epidemic, the World Health Organization (WHO) confirmed 635 cases and 399 deaths across Guinea, Liberia and Sierra Leone. In the first 100 days since the DRC Ebola Bundibugyo outbreak was declared on 15 May, there were 5,515 cases and 2,642 deaths. By Monday, local authorities had reported 6,100 confirmed cases and 2,950 deaths, with more than 5,000 cases recorded in Ituri Province alone. “The response is growing and has been scaled up, but the virus is moving faster,” relief chief Tom Fletcher said. “And unless we scale up urgently, more lives will be lost and the threat will grow.” Tweet URL Cautious success in Uganda Mr. Fletcher commended the DRC Government and UN partners for their efforts to contain the epidemic. He pointed to Uganda, where the WHO last week declared an end to the country’s latest Ebola outbreak. Uganda succeeded in containing the virus through rapid case detection and confirmation, contact tracing, isolation and clinical care, infection prevention and control, community engagement and strengthened surveillance, according to WHO Africa. The UN’s migration agency, IOM, has also conducted nearly 23 million health screenings in the DRC and neighbouring countries, helping monitor population movements, identify potential cases and reduce the risk of transmission across borders, UN Spokesperson Stéphane Dujarric said on Tuesday. Mr. Fletcher has allocated more than $57 million from the UN’s donor-supported Central Emergency Response Fund to Ebola response and preparedness efforts in the DRC and neighbouring countries. But the response is unfolding against the backdrop of an already severe humanitarian crisis, particularly in eastern DRC, where conflict between Government forces and Rwanda-backed M23 rebels has displaced communities and complicated efforts to reach people in need. “It is not simply a health emergency,” Mr. Fletcher said. “It is also a humanitarian emergency layered onto many existing humanitarian emergencies.” The UN peacekeeping mission in the DRC, MONUSCO, recently responded to clashes between the Congolese Armed Forces, Uganda soldiers and suspected members of the so-called Allied Democratic Forces, an Islamic State-affiliated insurgent group based in the east – the epicentre of the record outbreak. MONUSCO is also expanding programmes that have helped more than 60,000 people safely access farmland and schools in Djugu territory, supporting protection efforts and helping create conditions for displaced families to return home, Mr. Dujarric said. World must prioritise Ebola response Mr. Fletcher urged governments to ensure humanitarian workers can reach people in need and to keep the battle against the runaway outbreak high on the international agenda, which he said is essential to preventing the virus from spreading. Closing the funding gap and bringing the outbreak under control will require sustained political commitment and a coordinated international response, he said. “We have the experience, the tools and extraordinary people risking their lives to stop this epidemic. What we need now is political will, access and resources,” Mr. Fletcher said. “We have beaten Ebola before, and we can do it again.” Ebola Virus Disease DR Congo Facebook Twitter Print Email Back to top

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

刚果民主共和国在同情使用框架下推出埃博拉疫苗接种 - 新华社

DR Congo launches Ebola vaccination under compassionate-use framework - Xinhua
新闻简报The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) launched an Ebola vaccination campaign on Thursday, August 26, 2026, using the Ervebo vaccine under a compassionate-use framework in Tshopo Province. This initiative targets the ongoing Bundibugyo ebolavirus outbreak, which has spread across six DRC provinces, including Ituri, North Kivu, South Kivu, Tshopo, Haut-Uele, and Bas-Uele. As of Tuesday, August 24, 2026, the outbreak had resulted in 5,713 confirmed cases and 2,744 deaths, with a 48% case fatality rate. The vaccination aims to protect healthcare workers and frontline personnel, but Ervebo is licensed for Zaire ebolavirus, not Bundibugyo. The DRC government and its partners are assessing whether Ervebo can provide cross-protection against the Bundibugyo virus, with 20,000 doses allocated for a Phase 3 clinical trial and 50,000 for frontline workers. The World Health Organization and Africa CDC approved the initial release of 70,000 doses following a request from the DRC government. The effectiveness of Ervebo against the Bundibugyo virus remains to be established, and data collection is ongoing to evaluate its impact.
查看新闻五要素
时间
刚果民主共和国于2026年8月26日星期四推出疫苗接种,活动日期为2026年8月26日。发布日期为2026年8月28日。
地点与对象
疫苗接种 在东北部乔波省推出,特别是在乔波省首府基桑加尼。受影响人群包括医护人员、一线工作人员以及与患者接触过的个人。
事件
刚果民主共和国在同情使用框架下使用 Ervebo 疫苗 发起了埃博拉 疫苗接种 运动,以应对持续存在的本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 暴发。这是官方通讯社新华社报道的。
规模、影响与风险
The Bundibugyo ebolavirus outbreak has affected six provinces in the DRC, including Ituri, North Kivu, South Kivu, Tshopo, Haut-Uele, and Bas-Uele. As of Tuesday, August 24, 2026, there were 5,713 confirmed cases and 2,744 deaths, with an overall case fatality rate of 48 percent. The vaccination rollout aims to protect healthcare workers and frontline personnel, but the effectiveness of Ervebo against the Bundibugyo virus is not yet established.
应对、状态与不确定性
刚果民主共和国卫生部和非洲疾病预防控制中心确认启动 疫苗接种 活动。然而,Ervebo(获得扎伊尔埃博拉病毒许可)的使用正在接受调查,因为它对本迪布焦病毒具有潜在的交叉保护作用。数据收集正在进行中,以评估 疫苗 的影响,其对人类的有效性仍有待确定。

Home China Biz Culture & Lifestyle World Video & Live GLOBALink Xinhua Headlines More Sports Photos In-depth Latest Posters Xinhua New Media Special Reports B&R Initiative Biz China Weekly Editions Asia & Pacific Europe Africa North America 中文 Français Русский Deutsch Español عربي 한국어 日本語 Português Tuesday, September 1, 2026 Xi's Time Home Latest News China Biz Culture & Lifestyle World Photos Video & Live In-depth Sports Posters GLOBALink Xinhua Headlines Special Reports Editions Asia & Pacific Europe Africa North America 中文 Français Русский язык Deutsch Español عربى 한국어 日本語 Português DR Congo launches Ebola vaccination under compassionate-use framework-Xinhua DR Congo launches Ebola vaccination under compassionate-use framework Source: Xinhua Editor: huaxia 2026-08-28 21:12:15 A medical worker puts on a protective suit at an Ebola treatment center in Beni, North Kivu Province, the Democratic Republic of the Congo, Aug. 16, 2026. (Xinhua) The Democratic Republic of the Congo on Thursday launched Ebola vaccination with the Ervebo vaccine under a compassionate-use framework in the northeastern province of Tshopo, as the country steps up efforts to contain the ongoing Bundibugyo ebolavirus outbreak. KINSHASA, Aug. 28 (Xinhua) -- The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) on Thursday launched Ebola vaccination with the Ervebo vaccine under a compassionate-use framework in the northeastern province of Tshopo, as the country steps up efforts to contain the ongoing Bundibugyo ebolavirus outbreak. DRC health minister Roger Kamba officially launched the vaccination in Kisangani, capital of Tshopo Province, with health care and frontline workers among the first groups to receive the vaccine. "We have decided to use the Ervebo vaccine to protect first those who are on the front line, therefore our health care providers, but also people who have been in contact with patients," Kamba said. More than 50,000 doses of Ervebo have been received, Kamba said, quoted by the DRC official news agency ACP. The health ministry said on the social media platform X that the launch was an important step in the Ebola response, saying the vaccination would reinforce existing measures aimed at protecting communities, breaking chains of transmission and containing the spread of the disease. The Africa Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (Africa CDC) also confirmed at a press briefing Thursday that preventive vaccination had begun in Kisangani. Placide Mbala Kingebeni, Africa CDC's Director for Research, Clinical Trials and Innovation, told the briefing that the vaccine was being deployed among health care and frontline workers under a compassionate-use framework, which is expected to cover 14 selected health zones in Tshopo, Bas-Uele and Haut-Uele, three provinces more recently affected by the outbreak. Roger Kamba (L, front), health minister of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), receives the first batch of Ebola vaccine doses in Kinshasa, DRC, Aug. 21, 2026. (DRC health ministry/Handout via Xinhua) Data will be collected during the rollout to assess the vaccine's potential effectiveness against the virus responsible for the current outbreak, he said. Ervebo is licensed for the prevention of Ebola disease caused by the Zaire ebolavirus, rather than the Bundibugyo ebolavirus behind the ongoing outbreak. There is currently no approved vaccine specifically targeting the Bundibugyo ebolavirus. Laboratory studies have suggested that Ervebo may offer some degree of cross-protection against the Bundibugyo virus, but its effectiveness in humans remains to be established, he said. The DRC government and its partners would assess whether Ervebo can provide cross-protection against the Bundibugyo virus. Several other candidate vaccines specifically targeting the virus are also under development, explained Kingebeni. The World Health Organization and the Africa CDC said last Thursday that the International Coordinating Group on Vaccine Provision had approved an immediate initial release of 70,000 doses of Ervebo following a request from the DRC government. Of the allocation, 20,000 doses will be used in a Phase 3 clinical trial to assess the vaccine's impact against the Bundibugyo virus, while 50,000 doses are intended for frontline and health workers. The vaccination rollout comes as the Ebola outbreak continues to spread across six DRC provinces, Ituri, North Kivu, South Kivu, Tshopo, Haut-Uele and Bas-Uele. According to figures released Thursday by the country's health authorities, the DRC had recorded 5,713 confirmed cases and 2,744 deaths as of Tuesday, with an overall case fatality rate of 48 percent. ■ Comments Comments (0) Send You may like Multi-pronged rescue efforts under way after fatal mudslide hits China's Xizang border Shenzhou-23 astronauts complete first series of extravehicular activities Rural tourism thrives in Wuyuan County, E China Aircraft, drones help ensure autumn grain harvest in China's Heilongjiang New tunnel across Yellow River opens to traffic in Jinan, China's Shandong Village in China's Jiangxi draws sketching tourists in early autumn More from GLOBALink Land, water and air: Rescuers reach hardest-hit area after mudslide in Xizang Chinese games shift from "going global" to "creating global" at Gamescom 2026 Vlog | Made by China: How embodied AI robots get down to real-world tasks Follow us on More from GLOBALink Land, water and air: Rescuers reach hardest-hit area after mudslide in Xizang Chinese games shift from "going global" to "creating global" at Gamescom 2026 Vlog | Made by China: How embodied AI robots get down to real-world tasks Follow us on DR Congo launches Ebola vaccination under compassionate-use framework NewsChinaBizCulture & LifestyleWorldSportsIn-depth RegionsAsia&PacificEuropeMiddle EastAfricaNorth AmericaSouth AmericaOrganizations ServicesInvestmentDoing BusinessLive & Work in ChinaStudy in ChinaVisit ChinaWeather MoreSpecial ReportsVideo&LivePhotosCorrectionsAbout UsContact Us MediaPeople's DailyChina.org.cnenglish.cctv.comChina DailyCRI.cnYouth.cnCE.cnTaiwan.cnGMW.cnEcns.cnQiushi Journal Copyright©2000-2026XINHUANET.com All rights reserved. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Over 6,000 Ebola cases confirmed in DRC as death toll nears 3,000 - Al Jazeera

News Brief
The Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) has reached alarming levels, with over 6,000 confirmed cases and nearly 3,000 deaths as of August 31, 2026. This outbreak, attributed to the Bundibugyo strain, is described as the fastest-spreading Ebola epidemic ever recorded, according to the United Nations Secretary-General Antonio Guterres. The DRC Ministry of Communications reported 6,041 cases, including 1,366 recoveries and 2,911 deaths, with an additional 619 patients in isolation or hospitalised. The response is hindered by challenges such as limited contact tracing, insecurity in eastern DRC, and disruptions in healthcare due to airport closures and flight restrictions. Frontline healthcare workers also protested unpaid wages and poor working conditions. Despite these difficulties, the situation remains under investigation, and containment efforts are ongoing to address the rapidly spreading virus.
Original source text (English)
Over 6,000 Ebola cases confirmed in DRC as death toll nears 3,000 Outbreak in the DRC ‘the fastest-spreading Ebola epidemic ever recorded,’ as country struggles to contain infections. The Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) has topped prior surges – becoming the worst the country has experienced – as new data confirms there are now over 6,000 confirmed cases and nearly 3,000 deaths. There is currently no approved vaccine or treatment for the Bundibugyo strain of the Ebola virus, which is rapidly spreading across the country. Recommended Stories list of 3 items - list 1 of 3World Health Organization says Ebola outbreak in Uganda is over - list 2 of 3Nearly one in two Ebola patients dying in DR Congo outbreak - list 3 of 3Ebola spreads to two new zones in DRC; 60 areas now affected The current Ebola outbreak is “the fastest-spreading Ebola epidemic ever recorded”, according to United Nations Secretary-General Antonio Guterres, as people continue to be infected with the virus faster than frontline workers can track and respond to new cases. This is partly due to many new infections occurring among people who are not being monitored through contract tracing, according to the Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention. The DRC Ministry of Communications said on social media that 6,041 cases have been confirmed, “including 1,366 recoveries and 2,911 deaths. [Another] 619 patients are in isolation or hospitalised.” The response to the outbreak faces several challenges as cases are mostly concentrated in the eastern provinces of the DRC where densely-populated areas experience insecurity related to armed conflict, displacement and pre-existing humanitarian challenges. The medical charity Doctors Without Borders says existing health needs, as well as airport closures and flight restrictions, are complicating the provision of healthcare, disrupting the flow of staff, diagnostics, referrals and supply chains. Frontline healthcare workers went on strike in early August to protest their unpaid wages and the dire working conditions. Uganda was declared free of the Ebola virus after no new cases were reported for 42 days on August 28

Scale, impact and risk
6,041 confirmed cases, including 1,366 recoveries and 2,911 deaths. The outbreak is described as the fastest-spreading ever recorded, with challenges in containment due to limited monitoring and response capabilities. The Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention notes that many new infections occur among unmonitored individuals.
Response, status and uncertainty
The response faces challenges such as limited contact tracing, insecurity in eastern DRC, and disruptions in healthcare due to airport closures and flight restrictions. Frontline healthcare workers also protested unpaid wages and poor working conditions. The situation remains under investigation and requires further containment efforts.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

More than 6,000 people infected with Ebola in Congo, government figures show - CNBC Africa

News Brief
The Democratic Republic of Congo is experiencing a significant Ebola outbreak, with over 6,000 confirmed cases and 2,911 deaths, according to government data released on August 31, 2026. This outbreak, caused by the Bundibugyo strain of the Ebola virus, is the largest in the country's history, surpassing the previous worst outbreak from 2018 to 2020. The Bundibugyo virus has no approved vaccines or treatments, raising concerns about the potential for further spread and mortality. Response teams are currently operating in six provinces, including Ituri, North Kivu, and Haut-Uele, to contain the outbreak. The report highlights the severity of the situation and the lack of effective medical interventions, emphasizing the need for continued public health efforts.

柏林开发的埃博拉病毒 疫苗 即将在非洲进行试验 - 欧洲生物技术杂志

Berlin-developed Ebola vaccine heads toward African trial - European Biotechnology Magazine
新闻简报Minapharm 子公司 ProBioGen 使用 MVA-CR19 平台开发的柏林开发的埃博拉 疫苗 候选药物将在非洲进入一期试验。该试验针对的是 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒,该组织负责刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 正在进行的 暴发 项目。截至2026年8月中旬,世界卫生组织报告近5000例感染病例、2300多人死亡,疫情蔓延至六个省份。暴发 不是由扎伊尔 埃博拉病毒 引起的(存在已批准的 疫苗 Ervebo),而是由更罕见的 Bundibugyo 变种引起。目前尚无针对该病毒的获批 疫苗 或 治疗。CEPI 正在资助 Minapharm 疫苗 的开发,并支持评估现有 Zaire 疫苗 交叉反应性的研究。世界卫生组织建议在 III 期试验中研究 Ervebo,但其对 Bundibugyo 的有效性仍不清楚。此次回应是对 疫苗 产品组合和平台准备情况的测试,目标是在埃及建立本地生产。由于冲突和流离失所等挑战,局势“远未得到控制”。
查看新闻五要素
时间
The article was published on 2026-08-27. The event date is mid-August 2026, when the WHO reported nearly 5,000 infections and over 2,300 deaths from the Bundibugyo Ebola virus outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC).
地点与对象
暴发 正在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 发生,特别是跨越六个省和众多卫生区。截至 2026 年 8 月中旬,受影响人群包括近 5,000 名感染者和 2,300 多人死亡。

Minapharm 子公司 ProBioGen 使用 MVA-CR19 平台开发的柏林开发的埃博拉 疫苗 候选药物将在非洲进入一期试验。该试验旨在推进 疫苗 对抗 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒,后者负责刚果民主共和国正在进行的 暴发。世界卫生组织指出,暴发 不是由扎伊尔 埃博拉病毒 引起的,而是由更罕见的 Bundibugyo 变种引起的,并且目前还没有批准用于该病毒的 疫苗 或 治疗。

截至2026年8月中旬,暴发已感染近5,000人,造成2,300多人死亡,疫情蔓延至六个省份。世界卫生组织称,由于冲突、流离失所和人口流动性大等因素,局势“远未得到控制”。该风险并未明确说明,但 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒 缺乏批准的 疫苗 或 治疗 凸显了进一步传播和 死亡率 的可能性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Berlin-developed Ebola vaccine heads toward African trial - European Biotechnology Magazine

News Brief
A Berlin-developed Ebola vaccine candidate, developed by Minapharm’s subsidiary ProBioGen using the MVA-CR19 platform, is set to enter a Phase I trial in Africa. The trial targets the Bundibugyo Ebola virus, which is responsible for an ongoing outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). As of mid-August 2026, the WHO reported nearly 5,000 infections and over 2,300 deaths, with the epidemic spreading across six provinces. The outbreak is not caused by Zaire Ebola virus, for which the approved vaccine Ervebo exists, but by the rarer Bundibugyo variant. There is currently no approved vaccine or treatment for this virus. CEPI is funding the development of the Minapharm vaccine and supporting studies to assess cross-reactivity of existing Zaire vaccines. The WHO recommends studying Ervebo in a Phase III trial, but its effectiveness against Bundibugyo remains unclear. The response is a test of vaccine portfolio and platform readiness, with the goal of establishing local production in Egypt. The situation is described as 'far from being under control' due to challenges like conflict and displacement.
Original source text (English)
ADVERTISEMENT Stay up to date every week with the european biotechnology newsletter. Log in here to read the desired article. Email This field is for validation purposes and should be left unchanged. Email(Required) This field is hidden when viewing the form EBcom NL confirm list This field is hidden when viewing the form Consent I agree to the privacy policy. go to the article go back to the homepage Subscription Newsletter Advertise Search Menu Menu Latest news Background Opinion The Mag Issues Special Corporate publications Subscribe Euro Biotech Guide Market Place Products Sponsored Publications Advertise Associations Service Newsletter Contact Us Editorial Team Events Search Menu Menu Photo by Gani Nurhakim on Unsplash Infectious disease Berlin-developed Ebola vaccine heads toward African trial The Coalition for Epidemic Preparedness Innovations (CEPI) is providing Egyptian biopharmaceutical company Minapharm with up to $16.5 million (€14 million) to advance another vaccine candidate against Bundibugyo Ebola virus into clinical development. The candidate was developed by Minapharm’s Berlin-based subsidiary ProBioGen using its proprietary MVA-CR19 platform. A Phase I trial is planned in Africa, with the broader aim of helping the continent build the capacity to respond rapidly to emerging outbreaks. ADVERTISEMENT Why it matters: The Ebola epidemic in the Democratic Republic of the Congo is becoming a stress test for global vaccine preparedness. The outbreak is not being caused by Zaire Ebola virus, for which the approved vaccine Ervebo is already available, but by the much rarer Bundibugyo Ebola virus. There is currently neither an approved vaccine nor a specific treatment for this virus. At the same time, case numbers in the DR Congo continue to rise exponentially. According to the WHO, nearly 5,000 people had already been infected and more than 2,300 had died by mid-August. The figures have since risen further, with the epidemic spreading across six provinces and numerous health zones. In mid-August, the WHO explicitly described the situation as “far from being under control.” Conflict, displacement, high population mobility and difficulties with contact tracing are making containment even more challenging. The big picture: The Minapharm candidate is by no means an isolated project. Under the pressure of the current outbreak, an unusually broad vaccine pipeline has emerged within just a few months. CEPI is now supporting five Bundibugyo-specific candidates. They are based on different technologies — a deliberate portfolio strategy, given the limited amount of available data and the risks of relying on a single platform. ChAdOx1 BDBV is one of the most advanced candidates, developed by the University of Oxford and the Serum Institute of India. The adenovirus vector-based vaccine uses the same underlying platform as the Oxford/AstraZeneca COVID-19 vaccine. The first Phase I trial began in Oxford in July and is enrolling 50 healthy adults. In parallel, the Serum Institute produced around 620,000 doses in just two weeks for potential future use. rVSVdeltaG-BDBV-GP, a candidate from IAVI and the University of Texas Medical Branch (UTMB), is based on the recombinant vesicular stomatitis virus (rVSV) platform that also underpins the approved Zaire Ebola vaccine Ervebo. Preclinical data suggest that a single dose could provide rapid protection. The WHO had already identified the candidate as particularly promising in May. Other platforms are also under development, including an mRNA vaccine from Moderna and, now, the MVA-based approach from Minapharm and ProBioGen. This technological diversity is itself part of the strategy: Established vector platforms benefit from extensive prior experience, while technologies such as mRNA and MVA could allow rapid adaptation and manufacturing. A recent review in npj Vaccines identifies rVSV, ChAdOx1 and mRNA among the prioritized approaches, while other programs are using measles virus or MVA vectors, among others. Also notable: Paradoxically, an already approved vaccine is nevertheless being used during the current epidemic. The WHO has made 70,000 doses of MSD-developed Ervebo available to the DR Congo, with the first shipments already delivered. However, Ervebo is approved against Zaire Ebola virus, not Bundibugyo. Whether, and to what extent, it protects against the virus responsible for the current outbreak remains unclear. The WHO now recommends studying Ervebo in a Phase III trial during the current outbreak. In parallel, CEPI is funding four studies using blood samples from vaccinated people to determine whether existing Zaire Ebola vaccines can induce at least some cross-reactive immune response or provide a degree of protection against Bundibugyo. Worth watching: The case also illustrates how the logic of vaccine development for emerging pathogens is changing. Through its “100 Days Mission,” CEPI aims to bring vaccines against new epidemic threats into clinical development within roughly 100 days. In the case of Bundibugyo, however, the starting point is not sufficient to fully achieve that goal: Before 2026, the virus had only been associated with a small number of outbreaks, leaving a correspondingly limited clinical evidence base. The current response is therefore more of a test of portfolio and platform readiness. Oxford was able to move ChAdOx1 into the clinic relatively quickly because the platform, manufacturing partner and regulatory experience were already in place. IAVI can build on the rVSV technology used for Ervebo. Minapharm and ProBioGen, meanwhile, are contributing an MVA platform as well as manufacturing capacity in Africa. That last point could ultimately prove more important than the individual vaccine candidate itself. Minapharm will initially have the active substance manufactured under GMP conditions at ProBioGen in Germany, after which the material will be processed into the finished vaccine in Cairo. The plan is also to transfer active-substance manufacturing to Egypt. The aim is therefore to establish production that is as local and self-contained as possible. Between the lines: This gives the project an industrial-policy dimension as well. The Ebola crisis highlights how dependent many African countries remain on manufacturing sites outside the continent for vaccines and other medical countermeasures. The Minapharm/ProBioGen approach therefore combines vaccine development, technology transfer and the expansion of African manufacturing capacity. This is consistent with the approach increasingly pursued by CEPI and Africa CDC: Epidemic preparedness should not consist solely of sourcing finished vaccines from Europe, the US or Asia once a crisis has begun. Instead, scientific, regulatory and industrial capabilities should already be available on the continent before the next outbreak occurs. The current Ebola outbreak also demonstrates the limitations of this strategy. Even if a vaccine candidate enters the clinic within a matter of months, efficacy, regulatory approval and sufficient manufacturing volumes are by no means guaranteed. The critical gap lies between being “clinically ready” and being genuinely available during an outbreak. Yes, but: For the global vaccine industry, Bundibugyo therefore provides an interesting counterpoint to the COVID-19 experience. During the pandemic, developers were able to draw on platform technologies built over decades, enormous investment and a huge commercial market. With rare pathogens such as Bundibugyo, by contrast, there is little commercial incentive to develop and stockpile vaccines long before an outbreak occurs. This is precisely where organizations such as CEPI come in: They finance candidates and platforms before a conventional market exists. The fact that several technologies are now being pursued in parallel is less a sign of inefficiency than of risk management. The more independent programs there are, the greater the likelihood that at least one approach will be able to demonstrate safety, immunogenicity, efficacy and manufacturing readiness quickly enough. Bottom line: The Ebola outbreak in the Congo offers a sobering but important demonstration of the logic behind pandemic prevention: Vaccine preparedness cannot begin only once an epidemic is already underway. The current race may help narrow the existing gap but closing it will require vaccine platforms, manufacturing capacity, clinical trial infrastructure and financing to remain in a state of standby readiness, much like cars waiting at a traffic light. When a threat emerges, the system should only need to switch to a vaccine adapted to the pathogen and ramp up production capacity. Many pandemic-preparedness projects already rehearse such processes theoretically, almost like a dry run. Whether the funding currently allocated to these initiatives is sufficient to maintain them sustainably remains very much open to question. What all stakeholders should recognize, however, is that the savings generated by preventing the pandemic-scale spread of emerging infectious diseases at an early stage — or preventing known infections from spreading far beyond their usual regional boundaries — are likely to be substantially greater than the cost of maintaining laboratory and manufacturing capacity in readiness. 刚果民主共和国开始 疫苗接种 抗击增长最快的埃博拉病毒 暴发

DR Congo begins vaccinations to fight fastest-growing Ebola outbreak
新闻简报刚果民主共和国发起了针对埃博拉病毒的疫苗接种行动,使用的是Ervebo 疫苗,该药物在之前由不同毒株引起的暴发中有效。该活动优先考虑一线工作人员和卫生人员,临床试验正在进行中,以评估 疫苗 对本迪布焦病毒的功效,该病毒是当前 暴发 的罪魁祸首。截至周五,该国报告确诊病例 5,794 例,死亡 2,786 例,暴发 疫情蔓延至北基伍省的新卫生区。世界卫生组织(WHO)警告称,疫情仍处于失控状态,可能会超过2014-2016年西非暴发。已收到超过 50,000 剂 Ervebo,其中 70,000 剂已获批使用。疫苗 正在一项同情使用计划下使用,其对抗 Bundibugyo 病毒的有效性仍在调查中。该活动旨在覆盖乔波省、下韦莱省和上韦莱省的 14 个卫生区,卫生当局强调需要保护工人以防止社区传播。
查看新闻五要素
时间
刚果民主共和国于周四开始 疫苗接种 活动,刚果民主共和国政府截至周五(2026-08-28)的最新数据。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国,特别是乔波省、下韦莱省和上韦莱省,暴发 影响了东部的六个省份。
事件
刚果民主共和国使用 Ervebo 疫苗 发起了针对埃博拉病毒的 疫苗接种 行动,该行动在过去由不同类型的 埃博拉病毒 引起的 暴发 中有效。该活动针对的是一线工作人员和卫生工作者,目前正在针对引起当前 暴发 的 Bundibugyo 病毒进行临床试验。
规模、影响与风险
截至周五,已有 5,794 例确诊病例,2,786 例死亡。暴发 正在蔓延到新的卫生区,并被描述为该国过去 17 个 暴发 中最致命的一次。世界卫生组织(WHO)表示,暴发 仍处于失控状态,并有望超越 2014-2016 年西非 暴发。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗接种 活动正在进行中,已收到超过 50,000 剂 Ervebo,并批准使用 70,000 剂。疫苗 正在一项同情使用计划下使用,其对抗本迪布焦病毒的有效性仍在研究中。此次活动预计将覆盖三个省份的 14 个卫生区。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

DR Congo begins vaccinations to fight fastest-growing Ebola outbreak

News Brief
The Democratic Republic of Congo has launched a vaccination campaign against Ebola, using the Ervebo vaccine, which was effective in previous outbreaks caused by different strains. The campaign prioritizes frontline workers and health personnel, with clinical trials ongoing to assess the vaccine's efficacy against the Bundibugyo virus, which is responsible for the current outbreak. As of Friday, the country reported 5,794 confirmed cases and 2,786 deaths, with the outbreak spreading to new health zones in North Kivu province. The World Health Organisation (WHO) has warned that the situation remains out of control and could surpass the 2014-2016 West Africa outbreak. Over 50,000 doses of Ervebo have been received, and 70,000 doses have been approved for use. The vaccine is being used under a compassionate-use program, and its effectiveness against the Bundibugyo virus is still under investigation. The campaign aims to cover 14 health zones in Tshopo, Bas-Uele, and Haut-Uele provinces, with health authorities emphasizing the need for protection of workers to prevent community spread.
Original source text (English)
The Democratic Republic of Congo on Thursday began vaccinating people against Ebola as health authorities work to slow the fastest-growing outbreak of the disease in history. Health workers and other frontline workers in the Ebola response are being prioritised to receive the Ervebo vaccine that was effective in past Ebola outbreaks caused by a different, more common type. Clinical trials are going on to find a licensed vaccine for the strain responsible for the current outbreak. "We have decided to use the Ervebo vaccine to protect first those who are on the front line," Health Minister Roger Kamba said at the launch of the vaccination campaign in Kisangani, the capital of Tshopo province. Man heads to Nepal in search of wife and family missing in 'devastating' floods Nepal floods: Search continues for 1,500 missing amid threat of further flooding Tshopo is one of the six provinces affected by Ebola in the east. As of Friday there have been 5,794 confirmed cases, including 2,786 deaths, the latest figures from the DR Congo government shows. It is concentrated in Ituri province and is known to be the deadliest of all past 17 Ebola outbreaks in the country. The outbreak is spreading under extremely difficult conditions, fuelled by insecurity, displacement, a health workers' strike and intense population movements. Friday's update shows the situation appears to be worsening, with the outbreak spreading to two new health zones – Biena and Manguredjipa, in the North Kivu province The World Health Organisation (WHO) has said it remains out of control and is on track to surpass the 2014-2016 West Africa Ebola outbreak, the deadliest on record, which killed more than 11,000 people, primarily in Guinea, Liberia and Sierra Leone. Subscribe for free to our weekly newsletter for exclusive and original coverage from ITV News. Direct to your inbox every Friday morning. More than 50,000 doses of the Ervebo vaccine have been received, according to The Democratic Republic of Congo's state news agency. WHO said last week that 70,000 doses of Ervebo had been approved for use in The Democratic Republic of Congo. About 50,000 doses are intended for frontline and health workers, while 20,000 doses will be used in a clinical trial to study whether the vaccine protects against Bundibugyo virus, which is causing the current outbreak and has no licensed vaccine nor treatment. The vaccine is being used under a compassionate-use program, which allows a medical product to be used in a serious disease situation even though it has not been specifically approved for that particular use. UNICEF, workers unload doses of Ebola (Zaire) vaccines from a plane at Ndjili International Airport in Kinshasa, DR Congo. Credit: UNICEF via AP Health authorities said Ervebo could offer some protection against the Bundibugyo virus because the two viruses are related. But whether the vaccine can actually prevent illness in people infected with Bundibugyo Ebola is still being studied. Placide Mbala Kingebeni, Africa CDC's director for research, clinical trials and innovation, said data will be collected during the vaccination campaign to measure how well the vaccine works. The campaign is expected to cover 14 health zones in Tshopo, Bas-Uele and Haut-Uele provinces. Kamba strongly encouraged health workers to get vaccinated, saying the vaccine could protect them from the disease and help prevent them from carrying the virus into their communities. Congo Ebola Outbreak Passes 6,000 Cases and 2,911 Deaths as Attackers Force a Burial Team to Flee - Medical Daily

News Brief
The Ebola outbreak in eastern Congo has surpassed 6,000 confirmed cases and 2,911 deaths, according to figures reported from Bunia by the Associated Press and confirmed by Reuters. The rapid spread of the disease, which reached 1,000 cases within 40 days of response activation, has raised concerns about the outbreak's severity. Attackers forced a burial team to flee, highlighting the challenges faced by health workers. The government reported 1,360 recoveries, calling it an encouraging development. In response, the Minister of Public Health launched a vaccination campaign for frontline workers using the Ervebo vaccine, targeting provinces such as Tshopo, Bas-Uele, and Haut-Uele. The CDC notes that this outbreak's speed is notable, as it reached 1,000 cases much faster than the 2018 outbreak in the same region. The situation remains under active investigation, with ongoing efforts to control the spread and provide care to affected individuals.

Conditions Congo Ebola Outbreak Passes 6,000 Cases and 2,911 Deaths as Attackers Force a Burial Team to Flee Published Sep 01, 2026 6:01 AM EDT By Joseph James Jospin Mwisha | AFP via Getty Images Share on Twitter Share on Facebook Share on Pocket Congo's authorities said Monday that the Ebola outbreak in the country's east has passed 6,000 confirmed cases and 2,911 deaths, according to figures reported from Bunia by the Associated Press. Reuters, citing the same government data, put the confirmed case count at 6,041. Officials said more than 1,360 people have recovered, a development they called encouraging. The speed is what makes the number significant. The outbreak was declared on May 15, meaning the toll was reached in roughly three and a half months. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention notes that this outbreak passed 1,000 confirmed cases within 40 days of response activation, while the 2018 outbreak in the same country took about 235 days to reach that mark. Officials now believe the virus had been circulating since February, before anyone was looking for it. The World Health Organization has said the outbreak remains out of control and is on track to surpass the 2014 to 2016 West Africa epidemic, which killed more than 11,000 people. CDC currently describes the Congo outbreak as the second largest on record. Travel to Congo Is Now Restricted, Not Just Discouraged For readers in the United States, the practical question is narrow but real. CDC said in its outbreak situation summary that no cases tied to this outbreak have been confirmed in the United States and that the risk to the American public and to travelers remains low. The travel guidance has hardened well past an advisory. CDC recommends avoiding all travel to Ituri and North Kivu provinces and avoiding nonessential travel to Haut-Uele and Tshopo. Beyond that, U.S. entry for people recently in Congo is temporarily restricted. Travelers, including Americans, who have been in Congo within 21 days of their flight are not permitted to board commercial flights bound for the United States, and that applies even to passengers who merely stop in Congo without leaving the aircraft. Travelers who have been in Uganda or South Sudan but not Congo must enter through designated airports for enhanced screening. Families with a member planning aid work, mission travel, or a visit to relatives in the region need to build a 21-day gap into any return plan rather than assume they can fly home on schedule. A Response Team That Had to Run from a Funeral The outbreak is spreading in conditions that would strain any health system. Congolese authorities cited insecurity, mass displacement, a health workers' strike, and heavy population movement. Displacement sites are a particular concern because residents already live in crowded and precarious conditions. On Saturday, an Ebola response team was attacked on the outskirts of Mambasa in Ituri province while responding to a call to secure a body. Young people armed with machetes stormed the funeral site, forcing the team to flee and injuring one member. "We demand greater security so we can operate in the field," said Floribert Magene, a member of the response team, in comments to the Associated Press. That detail is not incidental. Safe burial is one of the few interventions that reliably interrupts Ebola transmission, because bodies are highly infectious at death. When burial teams cannot reach a community, transmission continues in households. The Vaccine Now Being Given Was Not Made for This Strain On August 27, Congo's Minister of Public Health launched a vaccination campaign for frontline workers using the Ervebo vaccine, starting in Kisangani and running primarily in Tshopo, Bas-Uele and Haut-Uele provinces, according to the European Center for Disease Prevention and Control. Here is the limitation that belongs high in the story rather than buried. Ervebo was developed and licensed against Zaire ebolavirus. This outbreak is caused by Bundibugyo virus, a different species with no approved vaccine or treatment. Whether Ervebo provides meaningful cross-protection against Bundibugyo has not been established. Clinical trials aimed at licensing a Bundibugyo vaccine are underway, and separate trials are testing the antiviral remdesivir and the antibody therapy MBP134 against this virus. Uganda is the counterexample officials keep citing. Its health ministry reported 20 confirmed cases and two deaths, discharged its last patient in mid-July, and declared the outbreak over later that month. WHO confirmed the end of the Ugandan outbreak on August 26 after 42 days without a new case. CDC, which counts confirmed and probable cases together, lists 21 cases and three deaths there. Where the American Exposure Actually Sits The pathway from eastern Congo to a U.S. household is not casual travel. It runs through aid workers, medical missions, researchers, and people with family ties to the region. That pathway has already been used. Two U.S. citizens working in Congo contracted Bundibugyo virus and were medically evacuated to Germany. The first, a missionary physician, was treated in Berlin and later discharged. The second, a humanitarian aid worker, was flown to a university hospital in Frankfurt and reported in stable condition by WHO. France reported one imported case on June 24. Each of those cases was identified and isolated, which is the outcome the surveillance system is designed to produce. MedicalDaily reported earlier in the outbreak that the confirmed total surpassed the Kivu epidemic's case count to become the largest in Congo's history. Anyone returning from an affected province who develops fever, severe headache, muscle pain, vomiting, diarrhea, or unexplained bleeding should call ahead to a clinician or emergency department and say where they have been, rather than walking into a waiting room unannounced. Counting Problems That Make the Real Total Uncertain Several things remain unconfirmed, and they matter for how the numbers should be read. The true case count is unknown. Congolese officials have said most cases and deaths are now occurring outside the network of monitored contacts, which means transmission is happening where surveillance is weakest. Case and death data reported by Congo are under continuous review, so figures can be revised in either direction. The outbreak has spread from three health zones to roughly 60 of the country's 151, across six provinces. Doctors Without Borders, which has more than 1,400 staff responding, warned that communities still lack adequate support to contain the disease at the 100-day mark. The group also notes that limited testing capacity has left hundreds of samples untested, which is its own source of undercounting. Whether Ervebo will help frontline workers against this strain is unresolved. Whether cross-border spread resumes is also unresolved, and WHO said last week that the risk remains despite Uganda's all-clear. The confirmed picture today is a fast-moving outbreak that has killed 2,911 people, is still expanding geographically, and has no approved treatment or vaccine for the strain involved. The people who face real risk are in eastern Congo and in the small group of Americans who travel or work there. For everyone else, the reasonable response is to follow official travel rules rather than change daily behavior. MedicalDaily will report revised case counts, any confirmed importation into the United States, and any change in federal travel restrictions. Congolese authorities said Monday that confirmed cases have passed 6,000 and deaths reached 2,911, with more than 1,360 recoveries. Which virus is involved? Bundibugyo virus, a rare Ebola species. It has no approved vaccine or treatment, unlike the more common Zaire species. Is there any risk in the United States? CDC says no cases from this outbreak have been confirmed in the United States, and the risk to the public and travelers remains low. Can Americans still fly home from Congo? Not immediately. Anyone who has been in Congo within 21 days is barred from boarding commercial flights to the United States and must wait out that period elsewhere. Why is a vaccine being given if none is approved for this strain? Congo began giving Ervebo to frontline workers on August 27. Ervebo is licensed against Zaire ebolavirus, and cross-protection against Bundibugyo has not been established. What symptoms matter after travel to the region? Fever, severe headache, muscle pain, vomiting, diarrhea, or unexplained bleeding. Call ahead to a clinician and disclose the travel history. Are the numbers final? No. Congo's figures are under continuous review, and limited testing capacity means confirmed counts likely understate the true total. Schools resume classes in Congo’s Ebola epicenter despite concerns from parents and teachers
新闻简报尽管家长和老师对正在发生的埃博拉病毒 暴发 带来的风险表示担忧,刚果伊图里省的学校仍于 2026 年 9 月 1 日星期二恢复上课。截至目前,由本迪布焦病毒引起的 暴发 已导致 6,100 例确诊病例中的 2,950 人死亡。4岁及以下儿童的病死率最高,达60%。虽然一些学校配备了非接触式温度计、洗手液和卫生盆,但伊图里当局没有对 传播 预防措施发表评论。专家们指出了重大挑战,包括缺乏清洁水和资金,这阻碍了遏制工作。暴发 正在迅速传播,已到达 60 多个卫生区,当局分发了 Ervebo 疫苗 作为潜在的保护措施。然而,卫生工作者对延迟付款提出抗议,使应对工作进一步复杂化。

尽管家长和老师担心儿童面临的风险,伊图里省埃博拉 暴发 震中的学校仍恢复上课(N3、N6)。
规模、影响与风险
截至 2026 年 9 月 1 日星期二,暴发 已导致 6,100 例确诊病例中的 2,950 人死亡。4岁及以下儿童病死率最高,达60%(N5、N15)。
应对、状态与不确定性
伊图里当局未回应关于确保教室内无传播的措施的置评请求。学校正配备非接触式红外体温计、洗手液和洗手池,但由于缺乏清洁用水和资金,仍面临挑战(N8、N11、N13)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Schools resume classes in Congo’s Ebola epicenter despite concerns from parents and teachers

News Brief
Schools in Ituri province, Congo, resumed classes on Tuesday, Sept. 1, 2026, despite concerns from parents and teachers about the risks posed by the ongoing Ebola outbreak. The outbreak, caused by the Bundibugyo virus, has resulted in 2,950 deaths among 6,100 confirmed cases as of that date. Children aged 4 and below have the highest case-fatality rate at 60%. While some schools have been equipped with noncontact thermometers, hand sanitizers, and hygiene basins, authorities in Ituri did not comment on transmission prevention measures. Experts note significant challenges, including a lack of clean water and funding, which have hindered containment efforts. The outbreak is spreading rapidly and has reached over 60 health zones, with authorities distributing the Ervebo vaccine as a potential protective measure. However, health workers have protested delays in payments, further complicating the response.
Original source text (English)

Edit Close Log In My Account My Account Dashboard Profile Saved Items Subscribe Manage Service Log Out Subscribe Manage Service Menu Search Site search Search Return to M-I Return to McLean County News Return to Grayson County News Return to Breckinridge Herald-News Today's E-Edition Tuesday, Sept. 1, 2026 Kentucky Extras Kentucky Extra Close AP Schools resume classes in Congo’s Ebola epicenter despite concerns from parents and teachers A new academic year has started across Congo despite concerns by parents and teachers that the country’s Ebola outbreak still poses risks to children in the epicenter By CONSTANT SAME BAGALWA - Associated Press Sep 1, 2026 7 hrs ago 0 Facebook Twitter WhatsApp SMS Email Facebook Twitter WhatsApp SMS Email Print Copy article link Save BUNIA, Congo (AP) — A new academic year began across Congo on Tuesday despite concerns by parents and teachers that the country's Ebola outbreak still poses risks to children in the epicenter. The resumption of classes comes in the middle of a ravaging disease outbreak that is spreading rapidly. According to the latest government data, 2,950 people have been killed by the virus since mid-May among 6,100 confirmed cases as of Tuesday. The union representing teachers in Ituri province, the epicenter of the outbreak, had called for a delay in restarting classes, but the government pressed ahead. Some schools in Ituri, especially those owned by the government, recorded low turnouts. Authorities in Ituri did not respond to a request for comment on measures to ensure a lack of transmission in classrooms. Aimerance Vahweka, a parent in the Bunia, the capital of Ituri, said the school restart came as a surprise and she is keeping her child at home for now. “I do not really see an increase in prevention measures,” she said. Other education officials said they are equipping schools with noncontact infrared thermometers, hand sanitizers and hand-washing basins. “We also raise student awareness about adhering to hygiene protocols, particularly regular handwashing and the use of hand sanitizer,” said Etienne Ndjabu, a teacher in a Bunia private school. Experts say major challenges still remain, as many schools lack access to clean water and funding remains below target. “There are also significant logistical challenges, particularly in remote areas, where the delivery of equipment and learning materials may be delayed,” said Greg Ramm, director for Save the Children in Congo. Children aged 4 and below have the highest case-fatality rate of any age group in the outbreak at 60%, according to available health data. The latest outbreak, which is caused by the rare Bundibugyo virus, is the fastest in the disease's history and has spread to more than 60 health zones. Authorities believe it had been spreading undetected among communities since at least February. The World Health Organization said the outbreak is on track to beat the West Africa's regional outbreak between 2014 and 2016. Health workers in affected provinces have gone on several strikes to protest delays in payments, which has affected the response to the deadly outbreak. Efforts to contain the disease have been hampered by a lack of an approved vaccine or treatment. Congolese authorities had started distributing thousands of Ervebo vaccine, which the WHO said might provide some protection. Conflict between the government and rebel groups in the eastern region, attacks on health workers and an intense population movement also have worsened the outbreak. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

什么是本迪布焦病毒?埃博拉疫情在刚果第六省蔓延,死亡人数和确诊病例不断上升 - arch-web.com.tw

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报刚果民主共和国正在与确诊的本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 作斗争,该病毒现已蔓延到第六个省,截至 2026 年 8 月 10 日,报告有 4,449 例病例和 2,061 人死亡。非洲疾病控制和预防中心警告说,如果遏制失败,暴发 可能会持续一年多,并成为该国最严重的埃博拉相关事件。最近,下韦莱省发生了一起死亡事件,患者是从上韦莱省旅行过来的,这凸显了跨省传播。当局报告称,暴发 于 2 月初开始实施,并于 5 月 15 日正式宣布。应对工作包括快速病例发现、隔离、接触者追踪、安全埋葬和社区参与,但由于早期症状与疟疾重叠以及缺乏批准的 疫苗 或特定的 治疗,挑战仍然存在。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 8 月 29 日发布;该事件至少自 2026 年 2 月起持续进行,并由 DRC 于 2026 年 5 月 15 日宣布。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国(DRC),特别是新受影响的下韦莱省以及先前受影响的省份,包括上韦莱省和伊图里省;受影响的人群包括下韦莱的社区以及从上韦莱到下韦莱的旅行者。
事件
非洲疾病控制和预防中心 (Africa CDC) 和刚果民主共和国政府报告,确诊的本迪布焦病毒病 (BVD) 暴发 已蔓延至第六个省份,有可能成为该国最严重的埃博拉相关事件。
规模、影响与风险
截至 2026 年 8 月 10 日,刚果民主共和国政府报告确诊病例 4,449 例,死亡 2,061 例;下韦莱省的一例死亡与来自上韦莱省的旅行有关;非洲疾病预防控制中心警告称,如果不加控制,暴发 可能会持续一年以上,并成为有史以来最大的疫情;乌干达(2007 年)和刚果民主共和国(2012 年)BVD 的历史病死率分别为 30% 和 50%。
应对、状态与不确定性
刚果民主共和国于2026年5月15日宣布本迪布焦病毒为暴发;应对措施包括病例识别、隔离、接触者追踪、安全埋葬和社区参与;不存在经批准的 BVD 疫苗 或特定 治疗;实验室确认依赖于PCR和抗原/抗体检测;早期症状与疟疾和其他发热性疾病重叠,延迟了 诊断;传播 在 IPC 措施不足的医疗机构和不安全埋葬期间的风险很高。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
什么是本迪布焦病毒?埃博拉疫情在刚果第六省蔓延,死亡人数和确诊病例不断上升 - arch-web.com.tw
News Brief
The Democratic Republic of the Congo is battling a confirmed Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak that has now spread to a sixth province, with 4,449 cases and 2,061 deaths reported as of 10 August 2026. The Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention warns the outbreak could persist for more than a year and become the country’s most severe Ebola-related event if containment fails. One recent death occurred in Bas-Uélé after the patient traveled from Haut-Uélé, highlighting cross-provincial spread. Authorities report the outbreak began in early February and was officially declared on 15 May. Response efforts include rapid case detection, isolation, contact tracing, safe burials, and community engagement, but challenges remain due to overlapping early symptoms with malaria and the absence of an approved vaccine or specific treatment.
Original source text (Chinese)
综合新闻 世界新聞 體育新聞 科學技術 娛樂 生活方式 Search ARCH雅趣 综合新闻 世界新聞 體育新聞 科學技術 娛樂 生活方式 Home 科學技術 什么是本迪布焦病毒?埃博拉疫情在刚果第六省蔓延,死亡人数和确诊病例不断上升 科學技術 什么是本迪布焦病毒?埃博拉疫情在刚果第六省蔓延,死亡人数和确诊病例不断上升 By 林美玉 (Lin Meiyu) - 29 8 月 2026 46 0 Facebook X Pinterest WhatsApp 非洲疾病控制和预防中心(非洲疾病预防控制中心)警告说,刚果民主共和国(刚果民主共和国)的埃博拉疫情已蔓延到第六个省,如果疫情蔓延不尽快得到控制,可能成为该国历史上最严重的疫情。 埃博拉疫情是由本迪布焦病毒(BDBV)引起的。 非洲疾病预防控制中心总干事让·卡塞亚周四表示,下韦莱省有一人死于埃博拉病毒,他是从伊西罗镇所在的邻近上韦莱省前往该省的。 刚果政府周三公布的数据显示,截至 8 月 10 日,该国已报告 4,449 例埃博拉确诊病例,2,061 人死亡。 卡塞亚在视频通话中对记者说:“如果我们不阻止这次喷发,它将持续一年多,并且将成为世界上最大规模的喷发。” 彭博社。 阅读更多 | 牛津大学开始首次人体试验 Puntibukyo 埃博拉疫苗 他指出,刚果在 5 月份正式宣布爆发 Bundibugyo 病毒株,尽管据信疫情是在 2 月初开始的。 什么是本迪布焦病毒病? 本迪布焦病毒病 (BVD) 是埃博拉病毒的一种严重形式,由正波病毒属本迪布焦病毒引起。这是一种人畜共患感染,据世界卫生组织 (WHO) 称,果蝠被认为是该病毒的天然宿主。 人们可以通过密切接触受感染野生动物(包括蝙蝠和非人类灵长类动物)的血液或身体分泌物来感染该病毒。一旦进入人体,感染就会通过直接接触感染者的血液、分泌物、器官或其他体液以及受污染的表面和材料传播。 阅读更多 | 法国首例埃博拉病例:从刚果返回的医生检测呈阳性 在感染预防和控制措施不充分的医疗机构中,传播风险尤其高。当人们直接接触死于该疾病的人的尸体时,不安全的埋葬方式也会导致传播。 本迪布焦病毒病 (BVD) 的潜伏期通常为 2 至 21 天。感染该病毒的人在开始出现症状之前不被认为具有传染性。早期症状,包括发烧、疲劳、肌肉酸痛、头痛和喉咙痛,很难与其他疾病区分开来,这使得早期诊断具有挑战性并延误诊断。 阅读更多 | JP Natta 在谵妄后被送往 AIIMS,并接受了冠状动脉造影。 世界卫生组织指出,随着疾病的进展,患者可能会出现与胃肠道问题、器官衰竭相关的症状,在某些情况下还会出现出血。 乌干达和刚果民主共和国分别于 2007 年和 2012 年爆发 BVD,病死率 (CFR) 分别为 30% 和 50%。 BVD 可能很难诊断,因为其症状与疟疾等其他常见发热性疾病重叠。应确认实验室检测,包括 PCR 和基于抗原或抗体的检测。 遏制疫情的爆发取决于快速识别病例、隔离和治疗患者、追踪接触者、确保安全埋葬以及当地社区的参与。目前尚无批准的 BVD 疫苗或具体治疗方法。 RELATED ARTICLESMORE FROM AUTHOR 放弃“美国湖”这个名字后,MapQuest 在 App Store 上排名第一 OpenAI Astra 是解决量子数学的神秘新模型 OpenAI 确认 Astra 已达到“关键”互联网阈值 最近的文章 谷歌对好莱坞的需要胜过电影公司对人工智能的需要 2 9 月 2026 PlayStation 玩家起诉索尼“购买”数字游戏的真正含义 2 9 月 2026 黄石公园明星吉尔·伯明翰为 X 战警 97 年需要更多关爱的角色配音 2 9 月 2026 粉丝们哀叹爱莉安娜·格兰德的伦敦最后一场演唱会“苦乐参半” 1 9 月 2026 随着处决和酷刑报告的增加,古特雷斯会见伊朗总统 1 9 月 2026 托特纳姆热刺队以双重交易从切尔西签下了米哈伊洛·穆德里奇和托辛·阿达拉比奥。 1 9 月 2026 25% 的美国成年人正在寻找 iPhone Ultra,但不是 2,000 美元的价格标签 1 9 月 2026 所有類別 综合新闻69720 體育新聞50111 世界新聞41892 娛樂29323 科學技術11785 生活方式811
View five news elements
Time
Published 29 August 2026; event ongoing since at least February 2026 and declared by DRC on 15 May 2026.
Location and population
Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), specifically the newly affected province of Bas-Uélé and previously affected provinces including Haut-Uélé and Ituri; affected populations include communities in Bas-Uélé and travelers from Haut-Uélé to Bas-Uélé.
Event
A confirmed Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD) outbreak, reported by the Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention (Africa CDC) and the DRC government, with spread to a sixth province and risk of becoming the country's most severe Ebola-related event.
Scale, impact and risk
4,449 confirmed cases and 2,061 deaths reported by DRC government as of 10 August 2026; one death in Bas-Uélé linked to travel from Haut-Uélé; Africa CDC warns outbreak could persist over a year and become the largest ever if uncontrolled; historical CFRs for BVD in Uganda (2007) and DRC (2012) were 30% and 50% respectively.
Response, status and uncertainty
DRC declared the Bundibugyo virus outbreak on 15 May 2026; response includes case identification, isolation, contact tracing, safe burials, and community engagement; no approved BVD vaccine or specific treatment exists; laboratory confirmation relies on PCR and antigen/antibody tests; early symptoms overlap with malaria and other febrile illnesses, delaying diagnosis; transmission risk high in healthcare settings with inadequate IPC measures and during unsafe burials.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

刚果开始疫苗接种抗击历史上增长最快的埃博拉病毒 暴发

Congo begins vaccinations to fight the fastest-growing Ebola outbreak in history
新闻简报刚果针对本迪布焦病毒发起了 疫苗接种 运动,该病毒导致了历史上增长最快的埃博拉病毒 暴发。该活动于 2026 年 8 月 27 日星期四在基桑加尼发起,优先支持乔波省、下韦莱省和上韦莱省的卫生工作者和一线人员。截至 2026 年 8 月 25 日星期二,该国报告确诊病例 5,713 例,死亡 2,744 例,暴发 集中在伊图里省。Ervebo 疫苗 以前对扎伊尔埃博拉病毒有效,目前正在同情使用计划下使用,但其对本迪布焦病毒的功效仍在研究中。世界卫生组织警告称,暴发 仍处于失控状态,其影响可能超过 2014-2016 年西非 暴发。已收到超过 50,000 剂,并已批准使用 70,000 剂,临床试验正在进行中以评估 疫苗 的有效性。该活动旨在覆盖受影响地区的 14 个卫生区。
查看新闻五要素
时间
据文章报道,刚果于 2026 年 8 月 27 日星期四开始 疫苗接种。暴发 一直在进行中,被描述为历史上增长最快的一次,截至 8 月 12 日星期二已有病例和死亡记录。
地点与对象
疫苗接种 活动正在乔波省首府基桑加尼进行,该省是受 暴发 影响的六个省份之一。受影响人群包括乔波、下韦莱和上韦莱地区的卫生工作者和一线人员。
事件
刚果使用 Ervebo 疫苗 发起了 疫苗接种 活动,以对抗当前由本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病毒 暴发。该活动优先考虑卫生工作者,并在同情使用计划下进行。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年12月25日,刚果报告确诊病例5,713例,死亡2,744例。暴发 集中在伊图里省,被认为是该国历史上最致命的一次。世界卫生组织表示,暴发 仍处于失控状态,严重程度可能超过 2014-2016 年西非 暴发。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗接种 活动正在进行中,已收到超过 50,000 剂 Ervebo,并批准使用 70,000 剂。正在进行临床试验以评估 疫苗 对 Bundibugyo 病毒的有效性。世界卫生组织尚未确认 疫苗 的功效,预计该活动将覆盖乔波省、下韦莱省和上韦莱省的 14 个卫生区。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Congo begins vaccinations to fight the fastest-growing Ebola outbreak in history

News Brief
Congo has launched a vaccination campaign against the Bundibugyo virus, which is causing the fastest-growing Ebola outbreak in history. The campaign, initiated on Thursday, August 27, 2026, in Kisangani, prioritizes health workers and frontline personnel in Tshopo, Bas-Uele, and Haut-Uele provinces. As of Tuesday, August 25, 2026, the country reported 5,713 confirmed cases and 2,744 deaths, with the outbreak concentrated in Ituri province. The Ervebo vaccine, previously effective against Zaire ebolavirus, is being used under a compassionate-use program, though its efficacy against Bundibugyo virus is still under study. The World Health Organization has warned that the outbreak remains out of control and could surpass the 2014-2016 West Africa outbreak in terms of impact. Over 50,000 doses have been received, and 70,000 doses have been approved for use, with clinical trials ongoing to evaluate the vaccine's effectiveness. The campaign aims to cover 14 health zones in the affected regions.
Original source text (English)
Skip to contentMenuToday's paper Long IslandSportsNewsInvestigationsRestaurantsOpinionBusinessThings to DoSchoolsObitsNewsday TVSearchTOGETHER LI Nation/WorldFirst day of school on LI datesNewsday InvestigatesWallet WatchLabor Day weekend on LI NewsNation/World Congo begins vaccinations to fight the fastest-growing Ebola outbreak in history Congo’s Health Minister Roger Kamba administers an Ebola vaccine to a health care worker in Kisangani, Congo, Thursday, Aug. 27, 2026. Credit: AP/Samy Kamanga By The Associated PressUpdated August 27, 2026 8:05 pm Share KISANGANI, Congo — Congo on Thursday began vaccinating people against Ebola as health authorities work to slow the fastest-growing outbreak of the disease in history. Health workers and other frontline workers in the Ebola response are being prioritized to receive the Ervebo vaccine that was effective in past Ebola outbreaks caused by a different, more common type. Clinical trials are going on to find a licensed vaccine for the current outbreak. “We have decided to use the Ervebo vaccine to protect first those who are on the front line,” Health Minister Roger Kamba said at the launch of the vaccination campaign in Kisangani, the capital of Tshopo, which is one of the six provinces affected by Ebola in the east. As of Tuesday, the country had recorded 5,713 confirmed cases, including 2,744 deaths, according to government figures. It is concentrated in Ituri province and is known to be the deadliest of all past 17 Ebola outbreaks in the country. The outbreak is spreading under extremely difficult conditions, fueled by insecurity, displacement, a health workers’ strike and intense population movements. The World Health Organization has said it remains out of control and is on track to surpass the 2014-2016 West Africa Ebola outbreak, the deadliest on record, which killed more than 11,000 people, primarily in Guinea, Liberia and Sierra Leone. Sign up for the Breaking News newsletter Get the latest breaking news as it happens. Sign up By clicking Sign up, you agree to our privacy policy. More than 50,000 doses of the Ervebo vaccine have been received, according to Congo’s state news agency. The World Health Organization said last week that 70,000 doses of Ervebo had been approved for use in Congo. In this photo released by UNICEF, workers unload doses of Ebola (Zaire) vaccines from a plane at Ndjili International Airport in Kinshasa, Congo, Friday, Aug. 21, 2026. Credit: AP/Ndomba Mbikayi About 50,000 doses are intended for frontline and health workers, while 20,000 doses will be used in a clinical trial to study whether the vaccine protects against Bundibugyo virus, which is causing the current outbreak and has no licensed vaccine nor treatment. The vaccine is being used under a compassionate-use program, which allows a medical product to be used in a serious disease situation even though it has not been specifically approved for that particular use. Health authorities said Ervebo could offer some protection against the Bundibugyo virus because the two viruses are related. But whether the vaccine can actually prevent illness in people infected with Bundibugyo Ebola is still being studied. Placide Mbala Kingebeni, Africa CDC’s director for research, clinical trials and innovation, said data will be collected during the vaccination campaign to measure how well the vaccine works. In this photo released by UNICEF, workers unload doses of Ebola (Zaire) vaccines from a plane at Ndjili International Airport in Kinshasa, Congo, Friday, Aug. 21, 2026. Credit: AP/Ndomba Mbikayi The campaign is expected to cover 14 health zones in Tshopo, Bas-Uele and Haut-Uele provinces. Kamba strongly encouraged health workers to get vaccinated, saying the vaccine could protect them from the disease and help prevent them from carrying the virus into their communities. Time
Congo began vaccinations on Thursday, Aug. 27, 2026, as reported in the article. The outbreak has been ongoing and is described as the fastest-growing in history, with cases and deaths recorded as of Tuesday, Aug.
Location and population
The vaccination campaign is taking place in Kisangani, the capital of Tshopo province, which is one of the six provinces affected by the outbreak. The affected population includes health workers and frontline personnel in the regions of Tshopo, Bas-Uele, and Haut-Uele.
Event
Congo initiated a vaccination campaign using the Ervebo vaccine to combat the current Ebola outbreak, which is caused by the Bundibugyo virus. The campaign prioritizes health workers and is being conducted under a compassionate-use program.
Scale, impact and risk
25, 2026, Congo had recorded 5,713 confirmed cases and 2,744 deaths. The outbreak is concentrated in Ituri province and is considered the deadliest in the country's history. The World Health Organization has stated that the outbreak remains out of control and could surpass the 2014-2016 West Africa outbreak in terms of severity.

Clinical trials are being conducted to assess the vaccine's effectiveness against the Bundibugyo virus. The World Health Organization has not yet confirmed the vaccine's efficacy, and the campaign is expected to cover 14 health zones in Tshopo, Bas-Uele, and Haut-Uele provinces.

Original title (Japanese)
民主刚果埃博拉病毒确诊病例已超过6000例,其中2911人病逝 - 禁闻网
News Brief
The news report highlights the ongoing Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), where over 6,000 confirmed cases have been reported, resulting in 2,911 deaths as of August 31, 2026. The DRC government and the World Health Organization (WHO) are actively involved in containment efforts, with the WHO declaring that Uganda has successfully ended its outbreak after 42 days without new cases. The outbreak is attributed to the Bundibugyo virus, and there are currently no approved vaccines or treatments available. Meanwhile, Egypt's pharmaceutical company has received funding to develop an experimental vaccine targeting the Bundibugyo virus, aiming to enhance local production capabilities. The report also notes that the outbreak is the largest in DRC's history, surpassing the previous one from 2018 to 2020. The WHO commends Uganda's government and healthcare workers for their efforts in controlling the spread. The information is sourced from the DRC government and WHO, with no mention of hard exclusions or related entities being analyzed as the primary target.

刚果民主共和国政府8月31日公布的资料显示,当地埃博拉病毒确诊病例已超过6000例,其中2911人病逝。 路透社报导,今年5月开始的这波疫情已成为中非国家刚果民主共和国(民主刚果)史上最大规模的埃博拉(Ebola)疫情,超越2018年至2020年的上一波最严重疫情。 本次疫情是由邦迪布焦埃博拉病毒(Bundibugyo virus disease, BVD)引发,目前尚无获核淮的疫苗【小编推荐:显微镜学家发表对四家疫苗公司的成分分析】或治疗方法。 民主刚果通讯部在社群平台X表示,确诊病例增至6041例,其中2911人病逝。 民主刚果通讯部补充,防疫团队目前正在6个受到疫情影响的省分工作,并特别关注伊图里省(Ituri)、北基伍省(North Kivu)、上韦莱省(Haut-Uele)等地。 好的消息显示:非洲东部国家乌干达已经数十天没有新增埃博拉病例,世界卫生组织(World Health Organization)27日宣布,当地正式摆脱疫情。 该组织在社群媒体X平台发文写道:从2026年7月16日最后一件确诊移入性埃博拉病例出院以来,乌干达度过42天,没有发现该病例相关的后续传播,代表着疫情结束。
